- **why:** The payoff of the whole AIDC lane for a BESS seller: after the equipment walk and the campus view, this lesson says where the product fits. It inherits the guidance module's `reviewBy` (2026-12-10) and its dated gates, so it is the first lesson the refresh pipeline will re-open.

### 3.5 · Track `market-access` — Selling Into the Market (Market Access & Bankability)

**Track `short`:** "Finish this and you can name who signs the PO, under which contract, against which certification file, inside which policy fence — and say what 'bankable' concretely means to the lender who decides." **Order (v05.09r):** `who-buys-storage` → `how-a-storage-project-happens` → `contracts-and-revenue` → `reading-the-numbers` *(added at S0, v05.09r)* → `how-a-utility-buys` *(added v05.07r)* → `the-certification-stack` *(guidance)* → `what-bankable-means` *(guidance)* → `the-china-policy-stack` *(guidance)* → `utility-procurement-meets-ai-load` *(guidance)*. **Prereqs:** `bess-foundations`, `aidc-grid-to-chip`. The five public lessons front-load the track so an analyst finishes something coherent before the withheld count begins.

#### `who-buys-storage` — Eight Buyer Classes
- **short:** Supplier, integrator, developer, IPP, EPC, GC, hyperscaler, neocloud — the roster's own categories as a map of who buys what, on which criteria, and from which desk.
- **group:** Market Access & Bankability · **gate:** `tracks`
- **inputs:** `profile:plus-power` (the tolling franchise; dual-sourcing) · `profile:nextera-energy-resources` (the market-maker IPP) · `profile:arevon` (a single-vendor-locked IPP) · `profile:jupiter-power` (a developer as a cell maker's anchor customer) · `study:constellation-energy` (merchant power; capacity markets) · `study:equinix` (retail, wholesale, build-to-suit) · `study:vantage` (the operator-landlord model) · `study:mortenson` (EPC vs GC: who owns the power-plant problem) · `study:turner-construction` (why procurement became the product) · `study:stack-infrastructure` (the product ladder) · `concepts:profiler-concepts` (hyperscaler, neocloud, EPC, PPA, tolling agreement, offtake, colocation)
- **outline:** `eight-classes` (table — class · what they buy · what they buy on · who signs · how fast) · `the-ipp` (prose — merchant vs contracted, safe-harboured fleets, why dual-sourcing is a strategy) · `epc-versus-gc` (proscons) · `the-landlord-tiers` (prose — retail, wholesale, build-to-suit; powered shell vs turnkey) · `where-it-fails` (callout — pitching storage criteria to an AI-criteria desk and vice versa; selling to the utility when the developer picks the OEM) · `drill` · `check-yourself`
- **why:** The single most common early-career sales error is selling to the wrong desk. The reader leaves with the roster's eight categories as a working model of who decides, so every dossier they open afterwards slots into a buyer class before they read a word of it.
- **v05.07r scope:** **eleven** buyer classes, not eight — the registry added `utility` (6), `investor` (5) and `advisor` (2) after planning. + `study:dominion-energy` (the utility as a buyer — one row here; the machinery is `how-a-utility-buys`) · `profile:brookfield` · `profile:blackstone` · `profile:mgx` · `study:excelsior-energy-capital` (`start-here-the-fund-above-the-platform`, `own-operate-or-develop`, `what-a-reservation-agreement-is` — the investor as the desk that owns the platform; C10: Brookfield's Aypa purchase signed, not closed) · `study:dnv` · `study:sargent-lundy` (the advisor as the desk that gates the sale) · the buyer guides that did not exist — `study:aypa-power` · `study:spearmint-energy` · `study:intersect-power` · `study:invenergy` · `study:gridstor` · `study:available-power` · `study:esvolta` · `study:strata-clean-energy` · `study:hunt-energy-network` (B3–B6) and `study:engie-north-america` · `study:aes-clean-energy` · `study:rwe-clean-energy` · `study:clearway-energy` (C5–C6, with their recorded verdicts). Title and `eight-classes` become "Eleven Buyer Classes" / `the-classes` at authoring. An authoring session pins only the guides it draws a row from.

#### `how-a-storage-project-happens` — From Site Control to Commercial Operation
- **short:** The lifecycle of a grid-storage project: site, interconnection, offtake, financing, EPC, commissioning, the meter turning on — and where along it a supplier actually gets chosen.
- **group:** Market Access & Bankability · **gate:** `tracks`
- **inputs:** `study:fluence` (how a project gets built and operated) · `study:tesla` (how a utility-scale project actually happens) · `study:mortenson` (developer vs builder; entitlement and environmental review; MW vs MWh) · `study:primoris` (how a solar farm gets built; backlog quality; percentage-of-completion) · `profile:solv-energy` · `profile:blattner` (the pure-play storage builders) · `study:hitt` (commissioning as proof) · `concepts:profiler-concepts` (EPC, interconnection queue, offtake, ITC, PPA)
- **outline:** `the-lifecycle` (timeline — site control → interconnection → offtake → financing → EPC award → construction → commissioning → COD) · `who-does-what-when` (table — stage · owner · what is decided · what a supplier can influence) · `where-the-seller-enters` (prose — bid-stage BOM lock, approved-vendor lists, the IE's technical review) · `how-overruns-hide` (callout — percentage-of-completion, backlog that is not one number) · `where-it-fails` (callout — offtake that does not close; the interconnection date that moves the pro forma; a vendor qualified after the RFP cycle) · `drill` · `check-yourself`
- **why:** Knowing the product is not knowing the sale. After this lesson the reader knows at which stage the OEM is chosen, by whom, and what must already be true (qualification, certification, bankability) for their product to be in the running.
- **v05.07r inputs:** + the developer guides above for `who-does-what-when` and `where-the-seller-enters` — `study:aypa-power`, `study:intersect-power` (the co-located energy-park model), `study:invenergy` (`build-and-transfer-selling-a-gas-plant-to-a-utility`), `study:excelsior-energy-capital` (`what-a-reservation-agreement-is`), `study:recurrent-energy` (C6: a pipeline 84.7 % early-stage against ~1.5 GWh operating — what "pipeline" means) · `study:dnv` (`the-financing-sequence`, `inside-the-ie-report`) for the IE's technical review · `study:mastec` · `study:samsung-ct` (EPC guides that did not exist).

#### `contracts-and-revenue` — Who Carries Which Risk
- **short:** PPA, tolling, take-or-pay, capacity, merchant, hedge — the instruments a storage or compute asset is financed on, and which party each one hands the risk to.
- **group:** Market Access & Bankability · **gate:** `tracks`
- **inputs:** `study:constellation-energy` (merchant power; capacity markets paying plants to exist; restarting a reactor; attribute markets) · `study:coreweave` (take-or-pay; GPU-backed debt) · `study:core-scientific` (anatomy of a take-or-pay colocation contract) · `study:amazon` (how a corporate PPA actually works; the co-location cost-shifting fight) · `study:google` (hourly matching; clean firm power) · `profile:plus-power` (tolling as a franchise) · `concepts:profiler-concepts` (PPA, tolling agreement, offtake, ERCOT, PJM)
- **outline:** `the-instruments` (table — instrument · who pays for what · who carries price risk · who carries volume risk · who finances on it) · `capacity-markets` (prose — being paid to exist; accreditation as the fight) · `take-or-pay` (prose — committed capacity from colocation to compute) · `clean-firm-and-the-restart` (callout — nuclear restarts, corporate PPAs, and why hourly matching changes what "100 % renewable" buys) · `where-it-fails` (callout — a merchant asset's software; a toller's warranty cycle budget; a PPA priced on a queue date) · `drill` · `check-yourself`
- **why:** `duration-and-degradation` taught the revenue *shapes*; this teaches the *contracts* that turn them into a financeable asset, which is what the developer on the other side of the table is actually optimising. The reader can then read a tender's commercial section as carefully as its technical one.
- **v05.07r inputs:** + `study:talen-energy` (`what-a-merchant-generator-sells`, `behind-the-meter-versus-in-front-of-it`, `what-ferc-actually-decided`, `the-workaround` — C4's corrected record: FERC rejected the behind-the-meter arrangement; today's deal is front-of-meter) · `study:vistra` · `study:nrg-energy` · `study:hut-8` (`four-treatments`, `ringfence`, `price-of-credit`) · `study:cipher-mining` (lease anatomy; the Google backstop) · `study:excelsior-energy-capital` (`the-two-battery-frameworks-side-by-side`) · `study:plus-power` (tolling — now a guide, not only a dossier) · `study:core-scientific`. The `clean-firm-and-the-restart` callout stays as the instrument view; the physics is `clean-firm-power` (§3.4).

#### `reading-the-numbers` — One Dollar, Four Names *(specified 2026-09-07 at S0, v05.09r — §7 row 25)*
- **short:** The same dollar shows up as an order, a backlog, a percentage-of-completion revenue line, a lease treatment and a tax-equity allocation. How to read each, why they disagree, and what the corpus's normalized KPI overlay lets you compare.
- **group:** Market Access & Bankability · **gate:** `tracks` (public)
- **inputs:** `study:siemens-energy` (`backlog-economics-reading-an-oem-s-future-in-its-order-book` — book-to-bill, conversion, what an order book says about pricing power) · `study:primoris` (`backlog-quality-why-13-9-billion-isn-t-one-number`, `percentage-of-completion-how-overruns-hide-and-then-explode`) · `study:hut-8` (`four-treatments` — the same economics in four places) · `study:clearway-energy` (`tax-equity-and-hlbv` — why someone else takes the loss) · `profile:hut-8` · `profile:cipher-mining` · `profile:galaxy-digital` (the C9 finding: the same leases quoted as base-term lease value, contracted revenue and an ASC 842 future minimum — three numbers, three bases, none an enterprise value) · **the normalized KPI overlay** (PROFILER-SCHEMA.md → Normalized KPI fields: `kpi` keys `revenue` · `backlog` · `orders` · `capex` with `usdMillions` + `fxBasis`, and the physical keys `gwh-shipped` · `backlog-gwh` · `mw-energized` · `mw-contracted` with `qty`; `periodType` / `periodEnd` on the period) read through the `profile:` refs that carry it — e.g. `profile:fluence` (a fiscal year ending 30 September beside calendar-year peers), `profile:catl` (an RMB figure and its cited conversion), `profile:tesla` · `concepts:profiler-concepts` (backlog, book-to-bill, percentage-of-completion, tax equity, HLBV, take-or-pay, contracted revenue). An authoring session pins only the profiles whose overlay rows it actually puts in a table.
- **outline:**

  | Section id | Kind | Teaches |
  |---|---|---|
  | `one-dollar-four-names` | prose | Order → backlog → revenue recognised → cash: where one dollar is on each date, and which line each counterparty headlines |
  | `reading-a-backlog` | table | OEM · EPC · landlord · IPP: what "backlog" means in each, what converts it, what can un-book it, and the book-to-bill that says whether it is growing |
  | `percentage-of-completion` | prose | How an overrun hides inside the estimate-to-complete and lands in one quarter — the mechanism, taught from the contractor's side |
  | `the-same-lease-three-ways` | table | Base-term lease value · contracted revenue · ASC 842 future minimum · enterprise value, on the three miner-landlords' own published numbers — why "$19B combined" failed |
  | `tax-equity-and-hlbv` | callout | Why a yieldco reports a loss that is someone else's tax benefit, what HLBV allocates, and what a material weakness there means for the reader |
  | `the-overlay` | table | The corpus's normalized KPI keys — what each is, the fiscal-year offset `periodEnd` makes explicit, the `fxBasis` a conversion must carry — and how the Compare view uses them |
  | `where-it-fails` | callout | A segment figure compared to a whole-company one; a book-to-bill read without the cancellation line; an enterprise value quoted as a contract; a converted figure with no rate cited |
  | `drill` · `check-yourself` | flashcards · quiz | 6 cards, 5 items — bases and mechanisms, never a company's figure for its own sake |
- **why:** The seller reads a counterparty's numbers every week and repeats them in the next meeting. C9 and C10 found that the same leases carry three bases, that every "(N dossiers)" count in a brief was wrong, and that an enterprise value and an equity cheque circulate as one number — this lesson is that evidence taught. After it the reader says which basis a headline figure is on before repeating it, and can read the overlay the Compare view already draws. Position 4: after `contracts-and-revenue` (the instruments that produce these numbers) and before `how-a-utility-buys` (whose numbers are a rate case, a different grammar).

#### `how-a-utility-buys` — The Regulated Machine *(admitted 2026-09-07, v05.07r — §2.2, utility)*
- **short:** A regulated utility does not shop; it plans, files, proves and recovers. The revenue requirement, the IRP-to-RFP ladder, the rate case, the large-load tariff, who pays for the wires — and where a storage seller enters the machine.
- **group:** Market Access & Bankability · **gate:** `tracks`
- **inputs:** `study:dominion-energy` (the gatekeeper; the monopoly bargain; who decides what; from plan to plant; three documents, three questions; the rate case and the docket; anatomy of a large-load tariff; the minimum-demand arithmetic; interconnecting a load; who pays for the wires; why the clock is years) · `study:aep` (the tariff as an instrument; eight tariffs, one argument; what a tariff does to a queue; three ways to buy capacity; who pays for a multi-state line) · `study:oncor` (the wires-only utility; the energy-only market; connecting a gigawatt; SB 6 clause by clause; the wires rate case; who pays for the substation) · `study:southern-company` (the elected commission; certifying a decade; the large-load rule; four channels; who picks the battery) · `study:xcel-energy` (what a resource plan decides; the approval sequence; procuring outside the plan; a tariff in three drafts; what a minimum demand charge buys) · `study:entergy` (the formula rate plan; buying for one campus; anatomy of an order that names the customer; who pays when the customer pays) · `study:burns-mcdonnell` (the study ladder from the requester's side — referenced; taught in `the-fence-line`) · `concepts:profiler-concepts` (IRP, CPCN, rate case, revenue requirement, large-load tariff, minimum demand charge, cost allocation, formula rate plan)
- **outline:** `the-monopoly-bargain` (prose — a service obligation for an allowed return; why the commission, not the customer, is the buyer of last resort) · `plan-file-prove-recover` (timeline — IRP → certificate → RFP → contract → rate case → recovery: who decides at each step and how long it takes) · `three-documents-three-questions` (table — the plan, the certificate, the tariff: what each decides, who signs it, what a seller can influence) · `the-large-load-tariff` (table — Dominion · AEP · Oncor/SB 6 · Georgia · Xcel: minimum demand, term, collateral, exit fee, who pays for the substation) · `who-picks-the-battery` (callout — utility self-build against PPA against the customer's own supply; where the OEM is chosen and by whom) · `ercot-is-the-exception` (callout — a wires-only utility in an energy-only market; what changes for a seller) · `where-it-fails` (callout — a bid that arrives outside the plan; a tariff draft that moves; a docket that names your customer; a cost-allocation fight that stalls the substation) · `drill` · `check-yourself`
- **why:** Utilities are the largest storage buyer class and, until this lesson, their machinery was visible only to contributors. After it an analyst can read a resource-plan docket, say which of the three documents a storage RFP falls out of, and tell a developer whether the utility or the customer is the desk that signs. `utility-procurement-meets-ai-load` (guidance) then teaches the playbook on top of it.

#### `the-certification-stack` — Listing, Test Method, Code
- **short:** UN 38.3 to NFPA 855: what each standard covers, whether it is a listing, a test method or a code, who demands it — and the fire record that wrote the rules.
- **group:** Market Access & Bankability · **gate:** `guidance` (contributor+)
- **inputs:** `guidance:bess-bankability-2026-08` (the certification stack table; UL 9540A and NFPA 855-2026 precisely; the fire record and how incidents price) · `concepts:profiler-concepts` (UL 9540A, NFPA 855, thermal runaway)
- **outline:** `three-different-things` (prose — a listing, a test method and an installation code are demanded by three different authorities) · `the-stack` (table — standard · covers · nature · who demands) · `9540a-precisely` (prose) · `the-fire-record` (timeline — McMicken 2019 → Moss Landing Jan 2025 → the setback bill dies → NFPA 855-2026 adoption; the failure rate per GWh falling) · `how-incidents-price` (callout — why underwriters distinguished legacy indoor NMC from modern outdoor LFP) · `drill` · `check-yourself`
- **why:** `spec-sheet-decoded` taught the vocabulary; this teaches the file. A contributor who has done it can hand a fire marshal, an insurer and a lender each the document they are actually asking for, and can tell a buyer why Moss Landing is a different risk object from the product being quoted.
- **v05.07r inputs:** + `study:dnv` (`fire-testing-and-the-witness`, `recommended-practice-versus-standard`, `three-documents-that-are-not-the-same`) as a public corroborating input; gate unchanged — the module is the spine.

#### `what-bankable-means` — The Independent Engineer's Report
- **short:** Lenders close on an IE report, not a brochure. What it tests, the contract skeleton it expects, the scorecards it consults, and the RFP diligence checklist that follows from all three.
- **group:** Market Access & Bankability · **gate:** `guidance` (contributor+)
- **inputs:** `guidance:bess-bankability-2026-08` (what bankable concretely means; the counterparty file; the RFP diligence checklist; sales implications) · `concepts:profiler-concepts` (bankability, augmentation, degradation)
- **outline:** `the-ie-report` (prose — revenue model against real capability; two-cycles-a-day against a one-cycle warranty) · `the-contract-skeleton` (table — commissioning guarantee · energy-retention warranty · availability guarantee through the LTSA · liquidated damages: who gives it, on what condition) · `the-scorecards` (prose — the Tier 1 list as a financeability screen; independent lab scorecards; factory audits and where defects now live) · `the-rfp-checklist` (table — the diligence items, grouped) · `where-it-fails` (callout — a warranty without the LTSA; a curve two points optimistic; a supplier that cannot survive a prudence review) · `drill` · `check-yourself`
- **why:** "Bankable" is the word that decides regulated-market sales and the word sellers use most loosely. After this lesson the reader can say what it means to the one person whose opinion counts, and can pre-empt the IE's questions in the proposal.
- **v05.07r inputs:** + `study:dnv` (`what-bankable-means-and-who-decides`, `inside-the-ie-report`, `degradation-is-where-the-money-leaks`, `reliance-who-may-trust-the-report`, `how-big-a-battery-financing-is`) · `study:sargent-lundy` (`why-the-owner-needs-its-own-engineer`, `the-technical-due-diligence-sequence`) — the IE seat is public now (G10); gate unchanged because the counterparty file and the RFP checklist are the module's.

#### `the-china-policy-stack` — Four Levers and One Order
- **short:** FEOC, MACR, §154 and the tariff stack as one machine; the timeline to 2031; the compliant lanes that remain; and what EO 14420 does and does not say.
- **group:** Market Access & Bankability · **gate:** `guidance` (contributor+)
- **inputs:** `guidance:china-policy-stack-2026-08` (the four levers; the MACR ladder; entity tests and the paper trail; the timeline; the interaction map; compliant pathways; red lines) · `guidance:eo14420-bulk-power-2026-08` (what the order is; covered equipment; the dated gates; the four definitions; what it does not say) · `concepts:profiler-concepts` (FEOC, NDAA §154, Section 301 tariffs, AD/CVD, 45X, ITC)
- **outline:** `four-levers` (table — the four machines side by side) · `the-macr-ladder` (bars) · `the-timeline` (timeline — 2023 to 2031) · `eo-14420` (callout — scope, gates, the four definitions, and what the order does not say) · `compliant-lanes` (proscons) · `red-lines` (callout — for listed-supplier sales teams) · `drill` · `check-yourself`
- **why:** The policy fence decides which US sales are possible before any technical conversation starts. The reader leaves able to walk a developer through "can I buy this cell in 2026 and keep my credit" without improvising — and knowing the red lines that end careers.
- **v05.07r inputs:** + `study:gotion` · `study:rept` (C12: named in NDAA §154(b) against captured by the incorporation prong; the material-assistance cost ratio as the operative test; the three widely repeated false claims about Gotion) · `profile:sungrow` (v9 — the FCC Covered List action, EO 14420 and the EU in `policyExposure[]`) · `profile:huawei-digital-power` · `profile:power-electronics` (the FCC's 28 July 2026 manufacturing-location test) as public corroboration; gate unchanged.

#### `utility-procurement-meets-ai-load` — The Gatekeeper
- **short:** When the regulated utility is the gatekeeper for AI load: the four-move playbook, the five channels storage enters through, who signs the PO, and ERCOT against the regulated states.
- **group:** Market Access & Bankability · **gate:** `guidance` (contributor+)
- **inputs:** `guidance:utility-aidc-procurement-2026-08` (the gatekeeper thesis; the playbook; Texas; the large-load tariffs; the Southeast machine; the five channels; the buyer map; ERCOT vs regulated; concrete awards; the dated gates) · `concepts:profiler-concepts` (ERCOT, PJM, interconnection queue, PPA)
- **outline:** `the-gatekeeper` (prose) · `the-four-moves` (table) · `five-channels` (proscons — storage RFPs · all-source RFPs · self-build supply agreements · ERCOT merchant · ride-through buffering) · `who-signs-the-po` (callout — regulated chain vs self-build vs PPA; the two-lane motion) · `ercot-versus-regulated` (table) · `the-dated-gates` (timeline) · `drill` · `check-yourself`
- **why:** The complement to `where-bess-plugs-in`: that lesson is the campus-side view of where storage attaches, this is the utility-side view. Together they are the map of every US storage purchase that AI load is driving, and the reader can say which channel a given opportunity is in and therefore who decides and how fast.
- **v05.07r scope:** the machinery is now taught publicly in `how-a-utility-buys` (position 5; 4 at v05.07r); this lesson keeps the playbook, the five channels, the buyer map, the concrete awards and the dated gates, and composes over the public lesson rather than restating it. + the six `study:` utility guides as corroborating public inputs (gate unchanged — the module still folds it to contributor+). Authored after §7 row 2.

## 4 · Teaching order

Registry order is teaching order. `clStudyNext_` walks `clTracks_()` in order and each track's `lessons[]` in order, skipping what the tier cannot read, and returns the first unfinished section — so the two lists below *are* the curriculum's path, not a suggestion. `clLessons_()` order only governs the index grouping; keep it aligned with the track order anyway so the index reads as the path does.

**`clTracks_()` order** (lane order, foundations first): `bess-foundations` → `electrical-foundations` → `aidc-grid-to-chip` → `aidc-campus` → `market-access` → *(S0, v05.09r — the fourth lane, §10.5)* `value-chain-makers` → `value-chain-builders` → `value-chain-buyers-and-backers`.

**Why this order and not lane-by-lane strictness.** A reader who follows the pointer literally does BESS first, then the electrical floor, then the AIDC chain. That is deliberate: the developer's stated goal is to sell *storage* into this market, so the product comes first and the electrical floor is met just before the track that most needs it. A reader who wants the AIDC lane first can open any track — the pointer only says what is next on the default path, and `prereqs[]` on tracks 3–5 will suggest the floor without blocking.

**Two ordering rules the authoring sessions must respect:**

1. **Within a track, the equipment walk is in physical order** — grid → fence → substation → building → rack → die — and the market track goes buyer → project → contract → file → policy → procurement. Do not reorder for gate convenience (e.g. moving guidance lessons to the end): the study-next pointer already skips withheld lessons, so an analyst walking `aidc-grid-to-chip` lands on `where-the-chain-breaks` straight after `the-800-vdc-shift` and loses nothing.
2. **Existing ids keep their position semantics.** `the-aidc-power-chain` and `heat-is-the-constraint` change track, not id; their sections and progress keys are untouched. The retired track id `aidc-power-primer` is not reused.

**Insertion versus append.** The unattended pipeline may only *append* to a registry or a track's `lessons[]` (P5). A developer authoring session is not bound by P5 and should place each new lesson at its position above, re-sequencing `clLessons_()` and `lessons[]` in the same commit — then run `check-classroom-pipeline.py --base origin/main` and expect P5 findings on that commit as the known cost of a curriculum reorder (the contract's §7 selftest must still pass; P5 is a diff assertion on pipeline commits, not a schema rule). Once the curriculum is in place, later pipeline runs append after the last lesson of the track whose `short` covers the new module, exactly as the C2c rule says.

**State of the registries at v05.07r.** `clTracks_()` holds `bess-foundations` → `aidc-grid-to-chip` → `aidc-campus`, and `aidc-power-primer` is already retired (its two lessons sit at their positions above), so of the track writes §8 item 9 describes only two remain: creating `electrical-foundations` (with `four-machines`, §7 row 1, inserted second in `clTracks_()`) and `market-access` (with `how-a-utility-buys`, §7 row 2, appended last) — plus the `prereqs[]` §2.2 promises (`aidc-grid-to-chip` and `aidc-campus` → `electrical-foundations`; `market-access` → `bess-foundations`, `aidc-grid-to-chip`), which can only be written once `electrical-foundations` exists. **Phase 4 authoring order (§7) is not track order**: a lesson lands at its position above whenever its session runs, re-sequencing `lessons[]` as the paragraph before this one allows, and the four lessons added at v05.07r take positions 5–6 of track 3 (4–5 at v05.07r), 4 of track 4 and 5 of track 5 (4 at v05.07r) without displacing any built lesson's id or progress — the two S0 insertions (track 3 position 2, track 5 position 4) shift them by one, and the three Value Chain tracks are created by S1 after `market-access`, never by a Phase 4 session.

## 5 · The failure-point map (grid to chip)

This is the spec for `where-the-chain-breaks` and the checklist every equipment lesson's `where-it-fails` section draws its row from. It is built from the study guides only (public), and each row names the guide that carries it so the authoring session pins what it actually uses. Four causes recur, and the lesson says so: **current squared becomes heat**, **direct current has no zero crossing**, **heat flows through a series path**, and **human hands are in the loop**.

| # | Stage (grid → chip) | Common failure | Why it fails there | Who owns it | Carried by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Interconnection | The date moves: study re-runs, queue re-pricing, an audit that pauses a batch | The study models the grid as it will be, and the grid keeps changing; the requester funds upgrades it does not control | Developer · utility | `study:burns-mcdonnell`, `study:stack-infrastructure` |
| 2 | Substation transformer | Bushing failure; moisture in paper insulation; tap-changer wear; a slot that slips | Bushings carry a conductor through a grounded wall — the highest-stress interface in the tank; paper that was not dried enough ages fast; the unit is custom and the queue is people-limited | OEM · owner's engineer | `study:hitachi-energy`, `study:ge-vernova`, `study:mortenson` |
| 3 | MV switchgear and protection | Miscoordination (a downstream fault opens a main); relay settings never re-checked after a load change; arc-flash energy | Time-current curves converge at high fault current; protection is a networked application now and drifts like software | Electrical contractor · EPC | `study:abb`, `study:eaton` |
| 4 | On-site generation | Turbines at part load or in hot thin air; air permits that lag the shell; fuel supply; a slot reservation that slips | A large turbine is efficient only at full load; the permit is for what comes out of the stack, not for the building | Power partner · developer | `study:kiewit`, `study:wartsila`, `study:xai`, `study:vantage` |
| 5 | Transfer switch and generator paralleling | Open transition with no stored energy; generators that do not synchronise; load that is not shed in priority | An open transition deliberately breaks before it makes; paralleling is a control problem with a human-written sequence | Electrical contractor · operator | `study:eaton`, `study:rosendin` |
| 6 | UPS | Load exposed on bypass while a monolithic unit waits for a specialist; eco-mode detection that is fast for the typical fault and not for every fault; modules parked below their efficiency sweet spot | Hold-up time is 10–20 ms and a monolith's repair time is hours; redundancy taxes efficiency | Operator · OEM | `study:eaton`, `study:huawei-digital-power`, `study:liteon` |
| 7 | Distribution and busway | Harmonic heating of transformers and neutrals; power-factor penalties; a tap-off added without a coordination re-check | Chopped current reflects distortion back into the building; distortion is heat doing no work | Electrical contractor · operator | `study:eaton`, `study:sinexcel` |
| 8 | Rack whip and PDU | Termination quality; A/B feeds that share an upstream point | Thousands of terminations are proven only in commissioning; redundancy that meets upstream is not redundancy | Electrical contractor · commissioning agent | `study:rosendin`, `study:hitt` |
| 9 | Server power supply | Inrush on a live swap; a failed unit dragging the shared bus; two supplies each at half load | ORing and precharge are engineered, not given; efficiency curves peak near full load | OEM · operator | `study:liteon` |
| 10 | 48 V bus and the last centimetre | A bus that behaves like a heater; a thousand amps with no thermal margin at the regulator | Loss scales with current squared; the final conversion is millimetres from the silicon | Server OEM | `study:delta-electronics` |
| 11 | DC distribution (800 VDC) | A sustained series arc; a stage deleted without a plan for its fault duty; a stock PSU never rated for DC | A DC arc has no zero crossing and must be engineered out; every stage removed makes the survivor's job harder | OEM · electrical contractor | `study:zhonhen`, `study:abb`, `study:delta-electronics` |
| 12 | The load itself | Tens of megawatts swinging in milliseconds, periodically, exciting resonances below line frequency | Training steps are rhythmic; the grid has never served a load of this shape at this scale | Operator · utility · buffer vendor | `study:eaton`, `study:megmeet` |
| 13 | The thermal chain (cooling) | One exchanger's approach pinched; recirculation for want of blanking panels; a sequence of operations that heats and cools at once | Heat flows in series and the worst stage limits the path; the sequence is invisible and decisive | Mechanical contractor · operator | `study:vertiv`, `study:schneider-electric` |
| 14 | Commissioning | Integrated tests skipped or run on temporary power; "rated for" read as "operating at" | Level 5 is the theatre of failure — what was not pulled under load was not proven | Commissioning agent · GC | `study:hitt`, `study:switch` |

Rows 1–12 are the power chain the developer asked for; rows 13–14 are the two failures that most often *present* as power failures and are not. The capstone's quiz is scenario-based ("the hall browned out for 40 ms and one row rebooted — which row of this table?"), which is the drillable form of the skill.

## 6 · Gap register — what the corpus cannot stamp yet

Things worth teaching that no stamp can currently carry, and what Profiler would need first. Ordered by leverage — the cheapest additions that unlock the most are at the top. "Unlocks" names the lesson or section that becomes possible, or becomes *analyst-visible* where today it would be guidance-gated.

*(Phase 3, 2026-09-07, v05.07r: this register was **not** re-run — `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §6 step 1 was discharged by X3 at v05.01r and that run stands. What Phase 3 did with it: the four lessons the **Unlocks** column of G2, G3, G5 and G8 names were admitted to the curriculum — §2.3 and §7 — and G6 remains the only open row, blocking none of them.)*

**Status is checked, not remembered.** Every row's status below was re-verified against the corpus on **2026-09-03 (v04.39r)** — dossier and study-guide files by slug, section titles and keyword coverage inside the guides that a row names, and the module ids in `guidanceDocs_()` — and **re-run in full at the close of Phase A of `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` on 2026-09-03 (v04.44r)**, after the four anchor sessions (Caterpillar v04.41r, Piller v04.42r, Dominion Energy v04.43r, Vicor v04.44r) — and **re-run in full again at the close of Phase B on 2026-09-05 (v04.78r)**, after the twenty-nine Phase B companies (B1–B2 at v04.45r–v04.46r, F1–F8 at v04.58r–v04.77r) — and **re-run in full again on 2026-09-07 (v05.01r) by X3, the Phase X integrity close-out**, against the completed **154-profile / 154-guide / 1,210-concept** corpus. **That X3 run is also Phase D step 1**: `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §6 opens with “re-run every register check and date it”, which is the same action, so Phase D begins at step 2 (authoring) rather than repeating it — recorded here and in that plan's §6 and §9.4 so the overlap is not re-derived. **Result: no row moved.** G1–G5 and G7–G10 remain closed and were re-verified by slug against the live corpus rather than read from this table; G6 remains **Open** (eight guidance modules exist and none is a large-load-interconnection subject — it is blocked on a developer-supplied document, not on a model); G11 and G12 are unchanged. *(G6, 2026-09-13, v05.43r: **the register now has no open row.** G6 closed on the session that built its module; the check was re-run against `guidanceDocs_()` rather than read from this table, and no other row was touched.)* *(S0, 2026-09-07, v05.09r: the "blocked on a developer-supplied document" wording here and in the G6 row's trail is superseded — the developer accepted a research-synthesis module; see the row's S0 note. The trail is append-only and kept as written.)* Each row carries its dated result. The column exists because it was missing: G1 had been closed since v04.31r and the register still read as open work with a *do this first* note attached, which produced a wrong recommendation in a later session. **A row is not closed until someone re-runs its check and dates it here.** `Partial` means the row's premise has moved but its stated asks have not been done; `Deferred` and `By design` are the register's own judgments that a row should not be commissioned, not work waiting to happen. **Re-run in full again on 2026-09-13 (v05.41r) at the close of S3 of `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7** — the fifth full re-run, against the 174-profile / 174-guide / 1,460-concept corpus, by the session that landed the last S3 pair (V4, Mitra Chem · Cornex); result and per-row notes in the trail below. **Standings — read this before the trail below (updated 2026-09-05, v04.78r, at the close of Phase B; re-confirmed unchanged 2026-09-13, v05.41r, at the close of S3 — see the S3 paragraph at the end of the trail).** The full re-run that produced this state was taken at v04.78r against the corpus rather than against the rows' own text — files by slug, section ids inside the guides each row names, and the module ids `guidanceDocs_()` returns — after all twenty-nine Phase B companies had landed (B1–B2 at v04.45r–v04.46r, F1–F8 at v04.58r–v04.77r). **Eight of the twelve are now closed**: **G1** (v04.31r), **G2** (v04.53r), **G3** (v04.51r), **G4** (v04.55r), **G5** (v04.53r), **G7** (v04.58r), **G8** (v04.52r), **G9** (v04.51r) — and **G10**, closed at this re-run (v04.78r) on the register's own definition of *Partial*: its one stated ask was satisfied twice by F1's two `advisor` dossiers, and the insurance seat it also mentioned was never an ask of this row. One is Open — **G6**. **G11** and **G12** stand as the register judges them, re-affirmed rather than re-checked, because neither turns on what the corpus holds.

**What is actually left, sorted by whether anything is scheduled to do it.** The useful split is not Open against Partial — it is whether a row has somewhere to come from.

- **Scheduled — a coverage-plan row exists and will close it.** **Nothing, as of v04.78r.** G7 and G10 were the two rows in this bucket; both closed when their ledger row (B10, regrouped as F1) ran at v04.58r, and no open row is now waiting on a session in `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §8.
- **Structural — no ledger row can produce it.** **G6** needs the guidance module "Interconnection for large loads". §8 of the coverage plan contains no guidance-module row anywhere, so no Profiler session — scheduled or otherwise — will ever move it. **G6 is now the whole of what this register still asks anyone to commission**, and it is one guidance session *(S0, v05.09r: no longer blocked on a document — one Opus 5 xhigh research-synthesis session, scheduled after S0 in `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.3)*.

**So the commission-first recommendation, restated against the state above: G6, and nothing else.** G4 was the other structural row and it closed at v04.55r when its guidance module landed; before that the recommendation read "G4 then G6, and nothing else", and before *that* the v04.39r paragraph below put G2 and G3 at the top. Every other row is closed or standing as a judgment; since v04.78r nothing is waiting on a ledger session any more. **G6 splits unevenly, and the v04.55r standings got this wrong by reading the row instead of the corpus** — the exact failure this block exists to prevent, corrected at v04.56r. That text said G6's sources "exist in the corpus only as fragments"; they do not. The **state and utility half is now well covered publicly**: `dominion-energy` teaches the large-load tariff, the load-interconnection study sequence and cost-causation against socialization; `aep` teaches the tariff as an instrument, eight tariffs against four dials, and what a tariff does to a queue; `oncor` teaches SB 6 clause by clause and who pays for the substation; `southern-company` teaches Georgia's January 2025 large-load rule; `xcel-energy` teaches the same tariff in three state drafts and what an 80 percent minimum demand charge buys; `burns-mcdonnell` teaches the queue that gates the buildout; and `stack-infrastructure`, `aligned`, `switch` and `amazon` teach queue-gaming, power banking and the co-location cost-shifting fight from the requester's side. Six utility guides landed at v04.53r *after* the row's last substantive re-check, which is why its text is stale. **What is genuinely missing is the federal layer**: the string "Order 2023" appears **nowhere** in the corpus, and study-fee regimes exist as no one's subject. So a G6 session's original research should concentrate on the federal interconnection rules and the fee regimes, and should compose against the state-side guides rather than restate them.

**The six paragraphs below are the dated re-check trail that produced this state.** Each is kept exactly as written and each was true on its own date; none of them is current guidance on its own, and the standings above supersede all six. The trail is append-only — a paragraph is never rewritten to match a later state, because that would make this section lie about its own history.

**What this register said to commission first (written 2026-09-03 at v04.39r, before Phase A).** With G1 closed, **G2 and G3 are the top of the list**: they are the two that turn the AIDC equipment walk from "taught through the electrical contractor's eyes" into "taught with the OEMs in the room", which is what a seller needs. Both were confirmed fully open on 2026-09-03 — no gen-set OEM dossier of any kind exists, Vertiv's guide contains no UPS content at all, Schneider's has none either, and rotary or flywheel UPS has no home in the corpus. **G9 is the one this curriculum has now hit in practice rather than in theory**: two lessons written in September 2026 had to state its thinness in their own text rather than teach around it, which is the strongest evidence any row here carries. G5 remains the expensive one that matters most for the *team* case: without utility dossiers the entire regulated-procurement story is invisible below contributor, and that is the story an analyst on a sales team would most need.

**Where the register stands after Phase A (re-run 2026-09-03, v04.44r).** The four anchors moved four rows from Open to Partial — G2, G3, G5 and G9 — and each of the four lessons they were commissioned for (`backup-generation`, `the-ups-room`, `how-a-utility-buys`, `inside-the-rack` / `the-800-vdc-shift`) now has a public study guide to pin as its primary source. None of the four is closed: each row's second half is scheduled rather than done — the second gen-set OEM (C1), the Vertiv and Schneider UPS revisions (unscheduled), the remaining four utilities (B1–B2), and the Narada guide (Phase C backfill). G4, G6, G7, G8 and G10 were re-checked and did not move; G11 and G12 stand as the register judges them. The next re-run is owed at the close of Phase B.

**Targeted re-run of G3, G4 and G9 (2026-09-04, v04.51r).** The §8 guide-revision session — `vertiv`, `schneider-electric` and `siemens-energy` revised, `narada` backfilled, one data-only push — was run against these three rows specifically and each was re-checked against the corpus rather than against memory. **G3 closes. G9 closes.** **G4 does not**, and the reason is structural rather than a matter of scheduling: its third ask is a guidance module that appears nowhere in `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md`'s ledger, so no amount of Profiler work will move it. Rows G2, G5, G6, G7, G8, G10, G11 and G12 were **not** re-run in this pass and keep their Phase-A dates; the full re-run is still owed at the close of Phase B.

**Targeted re-run of G7 and G8 (2026-09-04, v04.52r).** Phase C11 of `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` — `oklo`, `trane-technologies` and `mccarthy`, three dossiers and three study guides in one push — was run against these two rows, and each was re-checked against the corpus rather than against memory. **G8 closes**: the row asked for one SMR-vendor dossier and a study guide on what is actually new about SMRs, and both landed in the same session. **G7 moves to Partial at one of two**: Trane Technologies is the chiller OEM the row names, the CDU specialist is CoolIT at §8 row B10, and no session scheduled before that one can close it. The distinction against G4 is worth holding on to, because both rows now read as “one ask left” and they are not the same kind of ask: G7's remainder has a ledger row and a model assigned to it, so it is **scheduled**; G4's remainder appears nowhere in the ledger at all, so it is **structural** and needs a guidance session commissioned separately. `mccarthy` moved no row and was not expected to — §6 carries no general-contractor gap, the corpus already holding nine contractor guides, and the C11 brief scoped that guide to teach against them rather than over them. Rows G2, G5, G6, G10, G11 and G12 keep their Phase-A dates and G4 keeps its v04.51r date; none of the six was re-run in this pass, and the full re-run is still owed at the close of Phase B.

**Targeted re-run of G4 (2026-09-04, v04.55r).** The guidance session commissioned for G4's third ask ran, and the row was re-checked in full rather than having its third ask asserted done. **All three asks re-verified against the corpus: G4 closes.** Ask 1 — `siemens-energy.study.json` carries 9 Grid Technologies sections in an 18-section guide (landed v04.51r, re-confirmed here). Ask 2 — `powell-industries.profile.json` exists at profileVersion 2 under `supplier` with a study guide beside it (landed v04.48r, re-confirmed here). Ask 3 — `guidanceDocs_()` now returns **eight** modules, the new one being `grid-equipment-shortage-2026-09` in the **The AI Data-Center Wave** lane, registered immediately after `utility-aidc-procurement-2026-08` because it is that module's supply-side counterpart. **Two corrections belong in this trail.** First, the v04.51r paragraph's implication that this module needed a source document from the developer was wrong: only two of the eight modules have an ingested source under `industry-guidance/sources/`, and the other six declare what they are in a `**Provenance:**` line — this one is research-based synthesis over public primary sources (DOE's July 2024 report to Congress, EPRI's August 2023 bushing white paper, IEC 60076-2 and 60076-3, the Wood Mackenzie and PTR lead-time series, OEM capacity disclosures) plus already-verified internal material. Second, the v04.52r paragraph's distinction between G7 (**scheduled**) and G4 (**structural**) was exactly right and is the reason this session existed at all: a structural row needs someone to commission it, and nothing in `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §8 ever would have. **No §8 row was added for this work and none should be** — that would convert the register's one remaining structural ask into a scheduled one on paper without making it so in fact. Rows G1, G2, G3, G5, G6, G7, G8, G9, G10, G11 and G12 were **not** re-run in this pass and keep their existing dates; the standings block above was rewritten in the same commit, because it named G4 as one of the two structural rows and went stale the moment this module landed. **The register's commissioning backlog is now G6 alone.**

**Where the register stands after Phase B (re-run 2026-09-05, v04.78r).** All twelve rows were re-run against the corpus, not against their own text — dossier and guide files by slug, section ids inside the guides each row names, and the eight module ids `guidanceDocs_()` returns — after the last Phase B session (F8, v04.77r) had landed and every §8 B-row carried a version in both the Dossier and Guide columns. **One status changed: G10 closes.** Its stated ask — a module revision *or* one independent-engineer dossier — had been satisfied twice since F1 (`dnv`, `sargent-lundy`), and the v04.58r hold rested on the words *insurance* and *brokers* in the gap description rather than on any ask; by the register's own rule (*Partial* = premise moved, stated asks not done) that is a closed row with a residual, and the residual — the insurance seat around a financing is still taught nowhere, no insurer or broker slug exists, "delay-in-start-up" appears in no guide — is recorded in the row for the developer to raise as a new row if it is wanted. **Eight rows re-confirmed closed with no change** (G1, G2, G3, G4, G5, G7, G8, G9); G8 is now over-satisfied twice over, `x-energy` having landed at v04.73r beside `oklo`. **G6 stays Open and is the only row the register still asks anyone to commission**: no interconnection module among the eight, "Order 2023" still absent from the whole corpus, and study-fee regimes touched in two utility guides and one module but nobody's subject; Phase B contained no session that could have moved it and none was expected to. **Where the register stands after S3 (re-run 2026-09-13, v05.41r).** All twelve rows were re-run against the corpus, not against their own text — dossier and guide files by slug, section content inside the guides, the concepts registry, and the eight module ids `guidanceDocs_()` returns — after the last S3 session (V4, Mitra Chem · Cornex) had landed and every §8 Phase E row of `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` carried a version in both its Dossier and Guide columns (ten of ten sessions). **No status changed.** Per row: **G1** closed — 1,460 concepts, `check-profiler-study.py` 0 errors / 0 warnings across 174 guides. **G2** closed — `caterpillar`, `cummins`, `rolls-royce-power-systems` dossier + guide. **G3** closed — `piller`, `mitsubishi-electric`, `vertiv`, `schneider-electric` dossier + guide. **G4** closed — `siemens-energy`, `powell-industries`, and `grid-equipment-shortage-2026-09` among the eight modules. **G5** closed — `dominion-energy`, `southern-company`, `entergy`, `oncor`, `aep`, `xcel-energy`. **G6 stays Open** — the eight module ids are unchanged (bess-bankability, bess-tech-fundamentals, china-policy-stack, eo14420-bulk-power, grid-equipment-shortage, nvidia-800vdc, power-infra-aidc, utility-aidc-procurement); no interconnection module, and 'Order 2023' appears in no guide. **G7** closed — `trane-technologies`, `coolit`. **G8** closed — `oklo`, `x-energy`. **G9** closed — `vicor`, `infineon`, `narada`, `samsung-sdi` with guides. **G10** closed, and its residual now closed — see the row. **G11** deferred — 'water right' in four guides (`albemarle`, `cipher-mining`, `kiewit`, `tract`), all in passing; no public source beyond that. **G12** by design, unchanged by construction. This re-run is the closing condition of S3 in `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.3 ('register re-run for G10'); it was run by the session that landed V4 and not by the session that wrote the criterion, per that plan's Phase 0 rule. **The register's commissioning backlog is still G6 alone.** The next full re-run is owed when S2 has authored its first landscape modules, since those are the first content that could move G6's unlock (`the-fence-line`) or add a public source to G11.

## 7 · The cut line — re-planned 2026-09-07 (v05.07r)

Twenty-five new lessons was a curriculum, not a session; at the re-plan it is twenty-nine, ten built and **twenty-four remaining** — and at S0 (v05.09r) thirty-one, ten built and **twenty-six remaining**: rows 25 and 26 below. `reading-the-graph` is authored inside S1 as the Value Chain lane's opener (§10.5) and is **not** a Phase 4 row; the nineteen segment lessons are generated, not authored (§10.4). The first five were built as cut on 2026-09-02. Everything below them was re-ranked on **2026-09-07 (v05.07r, Phase 3 of `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md`)** against the corpus in §1, on the same three things as the original cut — what the developer asked for, **what the corpus now supports deepest** (the §6 closures that landed after the cut), and **what yields drillable understanding fastest** — plus the dependencies a re-ordered list has to respect: a track cannot be created empty, the capstone follows the stages it maps, and a lesson that hands material to another is authored after it. Each Phase 4 session (Opus 5 xhigh, one lesson, the contract's discipline in spirit) takes the **next unbuilt row**, reads its spec in §3 and every source named there, and pins dates off the documents it fetches (§8 item 2) — the stamps below are the refs an authoring session starts from, never the pins, and a session pins only what it actually drew on.

### First five — built (cut 2026-09-02; all built by 2026-09-03)

| # | Lesson | Track · position | Gate | Why first (2026-09-02) | Built |
|---|--------|------------------|------|------------------------|-------|
| 1 | `the-fence-line` | `aidc-grid-to-chip` · 1 | tracks | The missing front half of the chain the developer asked for; five deep public guides; every later AIDC lesson assumes it | 2026-09-02 |
| 2 | `bridge-power` | `aidc-grid-to-chip` · 2 | tracks | The largest untaught cluster in the corpus and the place a BESS most plausibly attaches to an AI campus | 2026-09-02 |
| 3 | `the-800-vdc-shift` | `aidc-grid-to-chip` · 9 *(was 6, then 8 at v05.07r)* | tracks | The topic every customer conversation drifts to; Zhonhen and Megmeet carry the whole public thesis | 2026-09-03 |
| 4 | `the-control-stack` | `bess-foundations` · 4 | tracks | The one hole in the existing BESS track a developer's asset manager would find in the first meeting | 2026-09-03 |
| 5 | `where-bess-plugs-in` | `aidc-campus` · 5 *(was 4)* | guidance | The payoff: after three AIDC lessons and one BESS lesson, this says where the product fits | 2026-09-03 |

Building them created `aidc-grid-to-chip` (with the existing `the-aidc-power-chain` at position 3), extended `bess-foundations` to four, created `aidc-campus` with `heat-is-the-constraint`, and retired `aidc-power-primer`. Every one of the five carries a `where-it-fails` section.

### The remaining twenty-six — Phase 4 order (re-ranked 2026-09-07, v05.07r; rows 25–26 added at S0, v05.09r)

One row per session; take the lowest-numbered unbuilt row. Position is the lesson's place in its track (§4), not its place in this list.

1. **`four-machines`** — `electrical-foundations` · 1 · tracks. *Stamps:* `study:sinexcel` · `study:sungrow` · `study:delta-electronics` · `study:vicor` · `study:infineon` · `study:power-electronics` · `concepts:profiler-concepts`. *Why first:* the floor every other lesson assumes (P = V·I, I²R, the four machines) and pure mechanism, so the highest drill value per hour of authoring; Vicor and Infineon (A4, C3) made it the most over-supplied unbuilt lesson; its session creates `electrical-foundations` and writes the `prereqs[]` on tracks 3 and 4. **Built 2026-09-13 (v05.53r)** — seven sections at the specified ids and kinds (`power-is-v-times-i`, `the-four-machines`, `switching-not-linear`, `three-ways-to-ruin-a-wave`, `where-it-fails`, `drill`, `check-yourself`), gate `tracks`, all seven stamps public and each date read off the fetched file (`study:sinexcel`@2026-08-08 · `study:sungrow`@2026-08-08 · `study:delta-electronics`@2026-08-21 · `study:vicor`@2026-09-03 · `study:infineon`@2026-09-04 · `study:power-electronics`@2026-09-06 · `concepts:profiler-concepts`@2026-09-13); `reviewBy` 2027-03-13 on the ~6-month default, the lesson having no dated gate of its own. The same commit created `electrical-foundations` at **`clTracks_()` position 2** per §4 and §8 item 9 — not appended, which is the expected `check-classroom-pipeline.py` P5 finding for a developer session — and wrote the `prereqs[]` §2.2 has promised since v05.07r: `aidc-grid-to-chip` gains `["electrical-foundations"]` and `aidc-campus` becomes `["electrical-foundations", "aidc-grid-to-chip"]`, its existing pointer kept. **Verified on the walk, not just in the registry:** `clStudyNext_` run against the real region returns `electrical-foundations → four-machines → power-is-v-times-i` for an account that has finished `bess-foundations`. The `short` §3.2 specifies is 184 characters and the card budget is 160, so it is carried with its second sentence tightened — the same trim `the-800-vdc-shift` made against its own §3.3 spec; the track's `short` is verbatim, tracks having no length check. Registry after: **31 lessons, 7 tracks**.
2. **`how-a-utility-buys`** — `market-access` · 5 *(4 at v05.07r)* · tracks. *Stamps:* `study:dominion-energy` · `study:aep` · `study:oncor` · `study:southern-company` · `study:xcel-energy` · `study:entergy` · `study:burns-mcdonnell` · `concepts:profiler-concepts`. *Why:* the largest closure (G5 — six 15–17-section guides, the deepest new cluster in §1), the first *public* utility lesson (a story analysts could not see at all), the largest storage buyer class; its session creates `market-access`. **Built 2026-09-13 (v05.54r)** — nine sections at the specified ids and kinds (`the-monopoly-bargain` prose, `plan-file-prove-recover` timeline, `three-documents-three-questions` table, `the-large-load-tariff` table, `who-picks-the-battery` callout, `ercot-is-the-exception` callout, `where-it-fails` callout, `drill`, `check-yourself`), gate `tracks`, all eight stamps public and each date read off the fetched file (`study:dominion-energy` · `study:aep` · `study:oncor` · `study:southern-company` · `study:xcel-energy` · `study:entergy` all @2026-09-03 · `study:burns-mcdonnell`@2026-08-21 · `concepts:profiler-concepts`@2026-09-13); `reviewBy` **2026-12-31**, set from the lesson's own nearest dated gate — the SB 6 cost-allocation rule changes the statute orders by the end of 2026, which is nearer than the GS-5 class's 2027-01-01 effective date. `study:burns-mcdonnell` is stamped because `where-it-fails` genuinely draws the study ladder (feasibility → system impact → facilities) and the load-versus-generation distinction from it; the §3.5 note that it is "taught in `the-fence-line`" remains true of the *requester-side* depth, which this lesson does not repeat. The same commit created `market-access` at **`clTracks_()` position 5** per §4 — the expected P5 finding — with §3.5's verbatim `short` and the `prereqs[]` §2.2 promises for track 5 (`bess-foundations`, `aidc-grid-to-chip`). **Verified on the walk:** `clStudyNext_` against the real region returns `market-access → how-a-utility-buys → the-monopoly-bargain` for an account that has finished the first four tracks. The `short` §3.5 gives runs past the 160-character card budget and is carried trimmed to 151, as `four-machines` and `the-800-vdc-shift` were. That is the intended behaviour for a newly published lesson and it is exactly what P5 exists to make visible. **Two notes for later authors.** §8 item 6 asserts that no outline runs two `prose` sections back to back; this outline does (`block-load-and-the-swinging-hall` then `paralleling-and-load-shed`), and the spec was followed rather than the assertion — the ids and order are load-bearing, item 6's claim is simply inaccurate for this row. 4. **`the-ups-room`** — `aidc-grid-to-chip` · 6 *(5 at v05.07r)* · tracks. *Stamps:* `study:piller` · `study:vertiv` · `study:schneider-electric` · `study:mitsubishi-electric` · `study:rehlko` · `study:eaton` · `study:samsung-sdi` · `study:narada` · `concepts:profiler-concepts`. *Why:* G3 closed (Piller plus two revised spines); the in-hall battery socket both built AIDC lessons describe from outside; must exist before `redundancy-by-the-numbers` so that lesson can shed the UPS ladder. **Built 2026-09-13 (v05.57r)** — nine sections at the specified ids and kinds (`seconds-or-minutes` prose, `three-ways-to-build-one` proscons, `the-ladder-and-the-datasheet` table, `the-battery-under-each` table, `the-battery-that-earns` callout, `the-shelf-that-threatens-the-room` callout, `where-it-fails` callout, `drill`, `check-yourself`), gate `tracks`, all nine stamps public and each date read off the fetched file (`study:piller`@2026-09-03 · `study:mitsubishi-electric`@2026-09-03 · `study:vertiv` · `study:schneider-electric` · `study:rehlko` · `study:narada` all @2026-09-04 · `study:eaton` · `study:samsung-sdi` both @2026-08-21 · `concepts:profiler-concepts`@2026-09-13); `reviewBy` 2027-03-13 on the ~6-month default, because **nothing in the fetched material carries an upcoming dated gate** — the standards this room cites (IEC 62040-3, NFPA 855, UL 1973, UL 9540A, IEC 62619, IEEE 519, NFPA 110) are named without effective dates in these nine sources, and the only dates present are past events. **Inserted at `lessons[]` position 5 of `aidc-grid-to-chip`** — its §4 position 6 among the *built* lessons, after `backup-generation` and ahead of `the-800-vdc-shift` — the expected P5 finding for a developer session, and again a real consequence rather than checker noise: `clStudyNext_` run against the real region now points an account that had finished all five previously-built lessons of that track back at `the-ups-room`, and forward to `the-800-vdc-shift` once it is done. The `short` §3.3 specifies runs past the 160-character card budget and is carried trimmed to 159, as rows 1–3 and `the-800-vdc-shift` all were. **One outline note:** §8 item 6's kind-variety bar is met (six kinds across nine sections) and the outline runs three `callout` sections in a row, which item 6 does not prohibit — it names only consecutive `prose`. Registry after: **34 lessons, 8 tracks**.

   **Three corrections of drift this row found and fixed, none of them new work:** (a) the §4 status column above still read `§7 row N` for rows 1–3 although all three are built — all four cells now read `built`; (b) `aidc-grid-to-chip`'s running `short` ("So far: …") was two lessons behind, never updated when `backup-generation` landed at v05.55r — it now names the ten-second race and the room that covers the seconds the engines cannot; (c) **a rendering defect the checkers cannot see, found in a screenshot and only partly fixable here.** A `proscons` card's `t` and `meta` reach the DOM through `clEl()`'s `textContent`, not `clFmt` — so a `{{term}}`, `**bold**` or `*italic*` in either renders its literal punctuation to the reader. This lesson's nine occurrences were removed before commit and the terms re-tooltipped in the `adv` bullets of the same section. **Nine identical occurrences are already shipped in four other lessons** — `bridge-power` (3 metas), `the-control-stack` (2 `t`, 1 meta), `where-bess-plugs-in` (2 `t`) and one `*square*` in `the-800-vdc-shift`'s `glossary[].d` — and they are deliberately **not** touched here: fixing another row's lesson literal would put a `check-classroom-pipeline.py` P8 finding on a commit whose expected signature is P1 + P5, and the wording change owes no `revisions[].changed[]` entry (G4 — meaning does not move), so it needs its own run. The mirror of the v05.56r markup check — an error on markup tokens in the `textContent`-only fields (`proscons` `cards[].t`/`meta`, `timeline` `lanes` values, `bars` `items[].label`/`sub`, section `title`/`read`, and the lesson's `title`, `short`, `tiles[]`, `glossary[]`) — is specified in §7.15 and belongs in the same run, because adding it before those nine are fixed would break the pristine-HEAD error baseline every Classroom session checks against.
5. **`the-transformer-and-the-substation`** — `electrical-foundations` · 3 · tracks. *Stamps:* `study:mortenson` · `study:hitachi-energy` · `study:ge-vernova` · `study:abb` · `study:siemens-energy` · `study:powell-industries` · `study:mitsubishi-electric` · `study:invenergy` · `concepts:profiler-concepts`. *Why:* the most-cited bottleneck in both markets and every BESS project's step-up sits in the same queue; G4 turned "thin on vendors beyond three" into three more; the HVDC section now has three sources.

   **Built — v05.61r, 2026-09-14.** Seven sections at the ids §3.2 fixes, in its order — `changing-clothes` (prose) · `inside-the-tank` (prose) · `why-forty-months` (timeline) · `the-biggest-lines-run-dc` (callout) · `where-it-fails` (table) · `drill` · `check-yourself` — on **nine public stamps**, each date read off the document fetched this run against a freshly-fetched `origin/main`: `study:mortenson@2026-08-21` · `study:hitachi-energy@2026-08-21` · `study:ge-vernova@2026-08-21` · `study:abb@2026-08-21` · `study:siemens-energy@2026-09-04` · `study:powell-industries@2026-09-03` · `study:mitsubishi-electric@2026-09-03` · `study:invenergy@2026-09-05` · `concepts:profiler-concepts@2026-09-13` (the registry's last commit date on `origin/main`). Every one of the nine contributed; none was dropped. The stamp folds to **`tracks`** and `guidance:grid-equipment-shortage-2026-09` is **not** pinned, exactly as §3.2 requires — the supply-side module stays the contributor deep-link rather than a gate-raiser.

   **This is the first Phase 4 row that appends rather than inserts**, and the checker signature says so: `electrical-foundations` holds only `four-machines` today (position 2, `string-versus-central`, is row 15), so the §4 position 3 *is* an append among the built lessons and the run reported **P1 and P8 only — no P5 and no P3**. `clStudyNext_` run against the real region proves the lesson is on the walk three ways: **fresh** it returns `bess-foundations → cell-to-container → what-a-cell-is` (the walk starts at the top of the curriculum, as it must); with **every other readable lesson completed** it returns `electrical-foundations → the-transformer-and-the-substation → changing-clothes`; with **this one completed too** it returns `null`, everything readable being done. A fourth run — `bess-foundations` plus `four-machines` finished — returns the new lesson, which is the electrical walk itself confirming position 2 of 2. Registry after: **35 lessons, 8 tracks**.

   **The `short` §3.2 specifies is 199 characters against a 160-character card budget**, so it is carried at 158 with the clause order changed — the same trim rows 1–4 and `the-800-vdc-shift` all made. **The §8 item 6 disagreement is recorded rather than acted on**, per the standing rule that §3's ids, kinds and order are the contract: the spec's outline opens with **two `prose` sections back to back** (`changing-clothes`, `inside-the-tank`), which item 6 says no lesson should do. Item 6's other half is met — five kinds across seven sections. The two prose sections are genuinely different jobs (the ladder outside the tank, then the machine inside it) and splitting either to satisfy the rule would have moved a section id that progress and drill ids key on. **Item 6 is the thing to revise if anyone wants this resolved, not the spec.**

   **One layout observation the screenshots surfaced and the checkers cannot see:** the `where-it-fails` table is four columns, and the engine's auto layout gives the first column roughly the width of its longest word, so `Harmonic and overload heating` renders one word per line. The shipped `four-machines` table wraps the same way at two lines, so this is the renderer's normal output for a four-column table rather than something this content introduced — but a `min-width` on the first column of `.cl-tbl` would fix it corpus-wide, and that is a **page** change (`Classroom.html`, a `w` bump) rather than a lesson one, so it is recorded here instead of taken.
6. **`who-buys-storage`** — `market-access` · 1 · tracks. *Stamps:* `profile:plus-power` · `profile:nextera-energy-resources` · `profile:arevon` · `profile:jupiter-power` · `study:constellation-energy` · `study:equinix` · `study:vantage` · `study:mortenson` · `study:turner-construction` · `study:stack-infrastructure` · `study:dominion-energy` · `profile:brookfield` · `profile:blackstone` · `profile:mgx` · `study:excelsior-energy-capital` · `study:dnv` · `study:sargent-lundy` · the B3–B6 and C5–C6 buyer guides named in §3.5 · `concepts:profiler-concepts` (pin only the guides a row is drawn from). *Why:* eleven classes now, three of them new; the desk map that opens `market-access` in reading order and every later market lesson assumes; the Phase B and C buyer guides are its material.

   **Built — v05.67r, 2026-09-14.** Seven sections at the ids §3.5 fixes, in its order, with the **id-permanence exception §3.5's own v05.07r scope line instructs** taken: the title is *Eleven Buyer Classes* and `eight-classes` is `the-classes`, because the roster gained `utility`, `investor` and `advisor` after the outline was written. The other six ids are followed exactly — `the-classes` (table) · `the-ipp` (prose) · `epc-versus-gc` (proscons) · `the-landlord-tiers` (prose) · `where-it-fails` (callout) · `drill` · `check-yourself`. The eleven classes are the registry's own `categories` array read on the day, and the `other` category is excluded because no company holds it. `reviewBy` **2027-03-14** on the ~6-month default: the only dated gate anywhere in the fetched material is the safe-harbour window's close on 2025-12-31, which has passed, so there is nothing nearer to take.

   **The stamp is nineteen of the thirty-one candidates, and the twelve omissions are the record worth keeping.** All 31 were fetched from a freshly-fetched `origin/main`, identity-matched on `slug`, and dated off the fetched body. **Pinned (19):** `profile:plus-power@2026-09-06` · `profile:nextera-energy-resources@2026-09-05` · `profile:arevon@2026-09-07` · `profile:jupiter-power@2026-09-06` · `profile:brookfield@2026-09-06` · `profile:blackstone@2026-09-06` · `profile:mgx@2026-09-06` · `study:constellation-energy@2026-08-21` · `study:equinix@2026-08-21` · `study:vantage@2026-08-21` · `study:mortenson@2026-08-21` · `study:turner-construction@2026-08-21` · `study:stack-infrastructure@2026-08-22` · `study:dominion-energy@2026-09-03` · `study:dnv@2026-09-04` · `study:sargent-lundy@2026-09-04` · `study:excelsior-energy-capital@2026-09-05` · `study:spearmint-energy@2026-09-05` · `concepts:profiler-concepts@2026-09-13`. **Fetched, read and not pinned (12):** `study:aypa-power` · `study:intersect-power` · `study:invenergy` · `study:gridstor` · `study:available-power` · `study:esvolta` · `study:strata-clean-energy` · `study:hunt-energy-network` · `study:engie-north-america` · `study:aes-clean-energy` · `study:rwe-clean-energy` · `study:clearway-energy`. **The reason is one reason, not twelve:** every one of them teaches an *instrument* or a *lifecycle stage* rather than a buyer class — contract against hedge against merchant, the conversion ladder, tax-credit monetisation, the yieldco, develop-transfer, repowering — and §3.5 assigns that material to rows 10 and 11 (`how-a-storage-project-happens`, `contracts-and-revenue`), which name most of the same guides. Two of the nine B3–B6 developer guides *were* pinned because they answer a class question rather than an instrument one: `study:spearmint-energy` for the developer class's single defining decision (sell the project or keep it, and the market in which unbuilt projects change hands), and `study:excelsior-energy-capital` for the investor class and the supply framework behind the supplier and integrator rows. **Pinning all 31 would have been the easier record and the worse one** — §3.5 says pin only what a row draws from, and thirteen of these put nothing in the finished lesson.

   **Signature: P1 and P5, no P3, no P8** — as predicted. `who-buys-storage` went in at `market-access` **`lessons[]` position 1**, ahead of `how-a-utility-buys`, which is an insert at the head rather than an append, so P5 fires and is accepted for a developer session. No `GATE_SYMBOLS` member moved, so `gateDigest` was correctly left alone; no other lesson's literal was opened. **`clStudyNext_` run against the real PROJECT region proves the walk three ways:** *fresh* it returns `bess-foundations → cell-to-container → what-a-cell-is`; with **every other readable lesson completed** it returns `market-access → who-buys-storage → the-classes`; with **this one completed too** it returns `null`. And because this row inserts, a fourth run shows **whose pointer moved**: an account that had finished the first four tracks plus `how-a-utility-buys` — which was `market-access`'s only lesson and therefore its completed track — is now pointed back at `who-buys-storage → the-classes`. That is the intended consequence of publishing a lesson at position 1 and exactly what P5 exists to make visible. Registry after: **36 lessons, 8 tracks**.

   **A finding no previous Phase 4 row could surface: registering a lesson makes its `READ_NEXT` segment due, and that is not damage.** `build-classroom-segments.py --check` went 0 due → **1 due** (`storage-developers-and-ipps`, `sections differing: read-next`, no pin moved) the moment this lesson was registered, because the generator's `read-next` renders an unregistered lesson as *"Eight Buyer Classes" (planned)* from `PLANNED_TITLES` and a registered one by its live title. **Nothing was moved that should not have been** — the diff is one string in one section, and the dry-run confirms it. It was **not regenerated**, per §10.9's ownership rule and the row's own brief: `segment-*` literals are S2's. Rows 1–5 all created the same staleness invisibly, because the whole roster was already due until the v05.62r sweep; **this is simply the first Phase 4 row to run against a clean 0-due baseline**, so it is the first that could see it. The consequence for the next session is that **1 due is this row's expected residue, not a new defect**, and it clears when S2 reaches `storage-developers-and-ipps`. Four other unbuilt rows are named in the same list (`how-a-storage-project-happens`, `contracts-and-revenue`, `reading-the-numbers`), so that segment will go due again three more times before S2 gets to it.

   **The five-column table rendered cleanly, which retires half of the open `.cl-tbl` question.** `the-classes` is five columns by eleven rows — the widest table any mechanism lesson has carried — and at a 1400px viewport no column wrapped one word per line. `Classroom.html` was not touched and no page version moved. The `min-width` question stays **recorded, not taken**: the row-5 observation was about a four-column table whose first column held a long single word, and this table's first column holds short class names, so this row is evidence about column *count* being harmless rather than evidence the defect is gone.
7. **`inside-the-rack`** — `aidc-grid-to-chip` · 8 *(7 at v05.07r)* · tracks. *Stamps:* `study:delta-electronics` · `study:liteon` · `study:lambda` · `study:nvidia` · `study:megmeet` · `study:vicor` · `study:infineon` · `study:flex` · `study:narada` · `concepts:profiler-concepts`. *Why:* G9 closed (Vicor 17 sections, Infineon, Flex, Narada) — the row the register said the curriculum had "hit in practice"; completes the walk beneath the built `the-800-vdc-shift`.

   **Built — v05.72r, 2026-09-14.** Eight sections at the ids §3.3 fixes, in its order, and **no id-permanence exception was available or needed** — unlike §3.5's entry, this spec carries no scope line overriding its own outline, so the ids are verbatim: `the-rack-is-the-machine` (prose) · `psu-to-die` (table) · `forty-eight-volts` (prose) · `hot-swap-is-engineered` (callout) · `the-periodic-swing` (prose) · `where-it-fails` (callout) · `drill` (6 cards) · `check-yourself` (5 items). Title *The Last Ten Metres*, gate `tracks`, four tiles, a six-term lesson glossary, `short` trimmed to **159** characters against the 160 budget (the §3.3 line is 175, the same trim rows 1–6 all made), `sales` on 3 of 8 sections. `reviewBy` **2027-03-14**.

   **All ten candidate stamps pinned — so this row's omission record is at section level, not source level.** Row 6's was twelve dropped sources with one shared reason; here every one of §3.3's ten inputs (six in the base line, four added by the v05.07r scope line when G9 closed) is load-bearing in a named section, so nothing was dropped and saying "none" would be the whole record. The useful version is one level down: **`study:flex`'s `the-last-hundred-metres` and `busway-or-conduit` are upstream of this lesson's fence** — the switchboard, the panel and the whip belong to `the-aidc-power-chain`, and the six-thousand-termination count is §5 row 8, which is row 8's — so flex is pinned on `reading-a-third-party-shelf` alone; **`study:vicor`'s `two-jobs-one-box`, `current-multiplication-and-the-sine-amplitude-idea` and `lateral-versus-vertical` are converter topology**, which no §3.3 section asks for, so their *conclusions* are carried (regulate upstream where the current is small; the last converter moves toward the die) without the topology; and **`study:liteon`'s `the-ocp-movement` and `study:nvidia`'s `cuda` and `fabless-and-foundry` are industry structure**, which is the segment lessons' material.

   **Signature: P1 and P5, no P3, no P8** — the third mid-track insertion and the third time this signature has held. `aidc-grid-to-chip` held six built lessons and §4's position 8 falls **between `the-ups-room` and `the-800-vdc-shift`**, so this is an insert rather than an append, P5 fires on `lessons[]` and is accepted for a developer session under §8 item 9. With row 6's 5 × 11 table and S2 session 2's 15-row and 50-row tables, the evidence now says the v05.57r defect was about a **first column holding one long word**, not about column or row count. Recorded-not-taken, with the hypothesis effectively retired.

   **The content contract held on material that is entirely hardware, and the test came out stronger than "no proper-noun answers".** No flashcard answer and no quiz answer is a proper noun — but also **no company or product name appears anywhere in the rendered body**. All eight public, so the stamp folds to `tracks` — **verified through the real serving path rather than assumed**: an analyst session answered `cop=lesson` with all eight sections and saw the lesson in its index with the track's `withheld` count at 0. **The roster was re-derived rather than copied**: §3.2's base list is five and its v05.07r amendment adds three, overlap zero, union eight, and §7's own list is eight — a fifth consecutive row on which the two agree.

   **EIGHT SECTIONS, NOT SEVEN — the §8 item 6 conflict resolved rather than picked.** §3.2's outline opens `the-spinning-machine` (prose) · `frequency-is-a-meter` (prose), which **§8 item 6 forbids in as many words**. §7.34's preferred route — convert the second to `bars` or `timeline` — was tested against the corpus and **failed on its own condition**: no input states a frequency-deviation series, and `study:power-electronics` records that the quantification is *in no datasheet, no release and no independent test result*, so a `bars` could only have been invented. The numbering is **corpus-visible rather than plan-only**: `study:vertiv` and `study:schneider-electric` cite rows 13 and 14 **by these numbers** in their own `where-it-fails` sections and `study:siemens-energy` cites rows 1, 2, 3, 11 and 12, so a renumbering would have broken three shipped guides.

   **The four causes were tallied, not asserted, and the tally is the lesson's sharpest fact.** Hands 6 (rows 2, 3, 5, 6, 8, 14) · current squared 5 (3, 6, 7, 9, 10) · no zero crossing 1 (11) · heat in series 1 (13) · **none of the four 3** (1, 4, 12); rows 3 and 6 carry two. **The commonest cause on the map is not physics**, and the most-discussed hazard in the chain — the DC arc — is its rarest. No fifth cause was invented: the three unassigned rows are marked as such and share a property the callout states — in each the equipment works exactly as specified and the failure is in the system around it. The string is valid JSON, the field is in `PLAIN_BY_KIND` so no micro-markup rule applies, and the character is present in the DOM: `cat -A` on the rendered text confirms `M-bM-^FM-^U` = U+2195. **It is a font-coverage defect, not a markup defect, and nothing in the toolchain can see one** — the fix is to carry the meaning in ASCII (`PUE up, WUE down`). (2) A two-preposition collision in the `two-ratios` `note` (*hold the landlord **to for** the life of the lease*).
