   **THE ROW INHERITED A DEBT AND CLOSED IT, AND (ff1) MADE THE CHECK MANDATORY RATHER THAN POLITE.** Two declarations named this lesson before it existed, and both were grepped word by word against the draft **before** the splice. `the-800-vdc-shift`'s `dc-safety-in-plain-terms` enumerates **five** things as belonging to *"a separate lesson at a stricter gate"* — how a DC fault behaves against an AC one, which grounding scheme a hall adopts **and what each costs it**, where the protection zones are drawn, the interlock that keeps hands off a live whip, and how quickly the device classes clear. **All five are carried, one per section**, and the enumeration maps onto §3's outline exactly, which is evidence the spec was written to honour it. `where-the-chain-breaks` declined *"the taxonomy of arc types and how each is detected and cleared"* in a row cell **and** in a drill card; the taxonomy is rows 2–5 of `ac-versus-dc-faults` and *detected* and *cleared* are two whole columns. **Both of that lesson's sentences also asserted the taxonomy was "held by nobody", which this commit makes false**, so both were corrected in the same commit under **one `revisions[]` entry naming both section ids** — `changed[] == the differs set`, which is why **P8 did not report**. The two header comments that recorded the gap (in `string-versus-central` and `where-the-chain-breaks`) were **closed with a dated line rather than rewritten**: a session's finding is a record, not a live claim.

What the grep did find became a paragraph: **`interlock` already means four other things in this curriculum** — a zone-selective wire between two breakers (`the-aidc-power-chain`, `where-the-chain-breaks`), a fire-alarm interlock (`redundancy-by-the-numbers`), a grid-interactive UPS reserve floor (`the-ups-room`), and a relay setting proved at a factory acceptance test (`the-transformer-and-the-substation`). None is this one, and a seller who conflates them will be wrong in a room. **A collision found by a reverse check became teaching material, which is a use of (t) no previous row has had.**

The `(planned)` leak went **27 → 28** across the **same nine** segments over 11 → 12 titles, and those nine are still byte-for-byte the nine `--check` reports as due, so (qq1)'s invariant survives the registration.

   **The track `short` moved off its "So far:" enumeration to the whole-track form §3.3 specifies**, as rows 18 and 19 did on completing their tracks, with one clause added that is this lesson's own — *say which regulator owns each step of the way*. That rewrite is also why the **analyst index payload moved at all** on a gated row: it went **43,071 → 42,708 bytes**, exactly **-363**, which is exactly the `short`'s own shrink, and swapping only that string into the base payload reproduces the head payload byte for byte. **The gated registration's own contribution to the analyst payload is +0** — so (nn5)/(pp5)/(qq7)'s byte-identity signal holds under decomposition even though the raw number moved, and a raw reading of it would have been a false alarm (rr2).


**Why the guidance lessons are still last.** The 2026-09-02 reason holds and one part of it sharpened: they are contributor-gated, quick to author (one module each — the outline is the module's own), and they inherit the modules' `reviewBy` dates, of which `bess-bankability`'s is 24 days from this re-plan. Building them first would still put the refresh pipeline's first real revisions on the lessons with the least drill value per hour; building rows 23–24 before the module is reviewed would put a red chip on a lesson the day it ships.

### Revisions, not rows — built lessons the closures deepen

Three built lessons could take inputs that did not exist when they were authored. None is a Phase 4 row, because Phase 4 authors lessons; each is a **developer-session revision** — adding public inputs to a public lesson moves no gate, and the pipeline cannot do it because deepening is novelty, not contradiction (G3). **`the-800-vdc-shift`** — the battery side G9's closure was commissioned for (`study:vicor` `the-battery-on-the-dc-bus`, `study:narada` `the-cell-behind-the-bbu`, `study:infineon`, `study:flex`). **`bridge-power`** — `study:mitsubishi-power` (the third turbine OEM its `the-turbine-queue` section names without a source), `study:fermi-america`, and a hand-off line to `backup-generation`. **`where-bess-plugs-in`** — `profile:on-energy` and `project:colossus` moved past their pins, and `study:narada` now sits beside `study:samsung-sdi` on the stationary niche. Do them after rows 3, 4 and 7 exist so the hand-offs have somewhere to point; each is a `revisions[]` entry with `changed[]` naming only the sections whose meaning moved.

### Not recommended — report-derived modules

Four reports exist and could stamp admin-only lessons ("the grid-scale BESS field", "the 800 VDC race", "§154-listed supplier risk", "named-project BESS attach"). Recommendation: **do not build modules from reports.** Three reasons. A report is point-in-time — its own schema carries `status` and the pipeline handles supersession by swapping refs (G8) — while a module is meant to hold for months. A report's content is company-ranked competitive judgment, which the content contract keeps out of flashcards ("never company trivia"), so a report lesson would drill exactly the wrong thing. And the admin already reads the report in Profiler's lens; a lesson wrapping it adds a progress tick and nothing taught. If the developer wants report material in the drill, the right vehicle is the **weekly briefing** — a dated feed is what a report is — and the pipeline already knows how to cite a report there.

**Re-affirmed 2026-09-07 (v05.07r).** The three reasons stand and the corpus moved *against* the idea: F10 and F11 in `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` record that two of the opportunity report's three "whitespace" campuses now have storage on the record and that all four reports were built at ~85–89 companies against 154 — a report-derived module built today would be stamped to an edition Phase 5 may retire. The briefing remains the vehicle.

### Also not recommended now

A "policy vocabulary" public lesson (FEOC, §154, 301, AD/CVD, 45X, ITC from the concepts registry alone). It is stampable and analyst-visible, but six definitions without the mechanics behind them is a glossary, and the glossary already exists as tooltips. The 2026-09-02 revisit condition was "if G5 lands and a public `how-a-utility-buys` lesson needs a public policy companion". **G5 landed and the answer is still no** (v05.07r): the registry now resolves some forty policy terms as tooltips rather than six, `how-a-utility-buys` needs the tariff and the docket rather than FEOC, and the mechanics stay where their sources are — contributor-gated in `the-china-policy-stack`, which now carries Gotion, REPT and Sungrow v9 as public corroboration. Also not scheduled: the three unlocked-but-unscheduled lessons in §2.3.

## 8 · Notes for the authoring sessions

Everything an authoring session needs that the schema and the classroom rules do not already say, or say somewhere a session under task pressure will not look.

1. **Model and session shape.** Per the standing decision (SESSION-CONTEXT, 2026-09-02): planning here on Fable 5.1; **authoring on Opus 5**, where the schema, the checkers and stamp discipline are the actual work. One lesson per session is the safe unit — the built lessons average ~290 lines of strict JSON each, and a session that authors two is a session that copies a section between them. At the v05.07r re-plan that is **twenty-four sessions**, and **twenty-six at S0** (v05.09r — rows 25–26), in the §7 order (Phase 4 of `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md`), each taking the next unbuilt row. The nineteen segment lessons are not sessions — they are generated (§10.4); `reading-the-graph` is S1's.
2. **Pins are read, never carried over from this plan.** The dates in §1 are what this planning session observed; an authoring session re-fetches every input and pins the date off the fetched document (`lastUpdated` in the profile or study JSON, `updated` in `guidanceDocs_()`, `built` in the graph, `git log -1 --format=%cs` for `profiler-projects.json` and `profiler-concepts.json`). G2 and G7 in `.claude/rules/classroom-app.md` bind developer sessions in spirit even though the contract binds only pipeline runs — a wrong pin is a silent freshness bug either way.
3. **`reviewBy` inheritance from guidance modules.** A lesson's `reviewBy` is its own nearest dated gate, and for a guidance-stamped lesson that is usually the module's — as of 2026-09-07: `bess-bankability` **2026-10-01** (24 days from the v05.07r re-plan), `nvidia-800vdc` 2026-11-30, `utility-aidc-procurement` and `power-infra-aidc` 2026-12-10, `eo14420` 2026-12-24 (`updated` 2026-09-01), `china-policy-stack` 2026-12-31, `bess-tech-fundamentals` 2027-02-24, `grid-equipment-shortage` 2027-03-31 (pinned by no lesson). Two consequences: `the-certification-stack` and `what-bankable-means` would show "⚠ review due" within weeks of being built — the reason they are §7 rows 23–24 and are **not to be authored before the bankability module's quarterly review lands** — and the quarterly guidance review must land before any guidance lesson or they go red together. Public lessons default to ~6 months from `updated`; `the-fence-line` and `bridge-power` took the nearest dated gate in their own material (`bridge-power` carries 2026-12-31), and the four lessons added at v05.07r should do the same where their material has one (a licence date in `clean-firm-power`, a tariff effective date in `how-a-utility-buys`).
4. **G1 is done — this note is now about how to keep it done.** G1's own list landed in v04.31r and a second increment of 37 terms in v04.38r; the registry stands at 112 entries. The mechanism the original note described still holds and is the reason none of this required touching a lesson: each lesson carries its glossary locally (the schema allows it) and registry entries are added afterwards without editing the lessons — `{{term}}` resolves lesson-first, so a local entry simply shadows a registry one and nothing breaks. **The standing practice for an authoring session:** define locally whatever the lesson needs, and check terms against the registry's *terms and aliases* — not its terms alone, since six candidates in v04.38r turned out to resolve already through an alias. Promote a local term to the registry when it is defined in two or more lessons, or when it appears at least five times in the public corpus or the guidance modules; leave lesson-specific framing local.
5. **Drill implications.** Twenty-four lessons at ~6 cards + ~5 quiz items add ~260 items to the drill's lesson pool. The study pool is **766** `sf:` items, not 802 — and it is smaller than the corpus: `clDrillStudyItems_` enumerates a guide's **top-level `flashcards[]` only** (`Classroom.gs`), while every guide authored from 2026-09 carries its cards inside a `flashcards` *section* (95 guides, 1,154 cards) that the pool never sees — including every closure guide §7 pins (Caterpillar, Piller, the six utilities, Vicor, Oklo…). **That is a Classroom (C4) heads-up for the developer, not a curriculum decision:** either the pool builder reads section cards too, or `lift-study-guides.py` lifts them to the top level; until one of those lands, the cards of the guides the new lessons are built from will not be drilled. `CL_DRILL_NEW_CAP` = 10 new items per day still means a reader meets a new lesson's items over roughly a week. Item ids are positional (`lc:<lessonId>:<sectionId>:<n>`), so a revision must **append** cards, never insert mid-array, unless the hash reset is intended. *(S0, v05.09r: the fix is decided and specified — §10.7, K1: the pool builder reads section cards under a new `ss:` item kind; no guide is lifted, no `sf:` id moves.)* *(K1 landed at v05.10r: the study pool is **1,920** items — 766 `sf:` + 1,154 `ss:` — Classroom GAS v01.17g, page v01.08w.)*
6. **Section-kind variety is a check, not a taste.** Every outline above mixes at least four kinds, and no lesson runs two `prose` sections back to back without a `table`, `callout`, `proscons`, `timeline` or `bars` between them. `bars` needs numbers the source actually states (the cell ladder, the rack-power ladder, downtime per nine, relative cell cost by duration) — never invented proportions; where the source gives a relationship without figures, use `table` instead. `ledger` is the guidance modules' claims ledger and is not planned for any lesson: a lesson's sourcing is its stamp and its provenance strip.
7. **The `sales` line.** The existing lessons put a one-line `sales` on roughly half their sections. Keep that ratio; a `sales` line on every section reads as a pitch deck.
8. **Two lessons touch each other's material by design and must not duplicate it.** `breakers-relays-and-faults` (track 2) cites `study:eaton` for fault-energy sizing only — selective coordination stays in `the-aidc-power-chain`. `contracts-and-revenue` (track 5) teaches instruments — revenue *shapes* stay in `duration-and-degradation`. `where-batteries-stop` (track 1) ends where `bridge-power` (track 3) begins; the engine table appears once, in track 1, and track 3 refers to it.
9. **Track edits are a developer-session write.** State at v05.07r: `aidc-grid-to-chip` and `aidc-campus` exist and `aidc-power-primer` is retired — that half was done with the first five. What remains: creating `electrical-foundations` (§7 row 1's session, with `four-machines`) and `market-access` (row 2's, with `how-a-utility-buys`), writing the `prereqs[]` §2.2 promises once `electrical-foundations` exists, and re-sequencing `clLessons_()` and each track's `lessons[]` as rows land at their §4 positions. All of it inside the content fence, one commit per session, with `gateDigest` unchanged (no gate symbol moves). Run `check-classroom-content.py` (zero errors), `check-classroom-pipeline.py --base origin/main` (expect P1/P5 findings on a reorder commit, expect **no** P3 finding), `--selftest` (zero failures), `node --check`, and `check-gas-inner-scripts.js`. Bump the GAS version once per commit and keep the public changelog line generic.
10. **The analyst view is the test.** After each commit, the acceptance check that matters is the one C1 established: `?as=analyst` (or a real analyst session) sees every public lesson, sees each guidance lesson only as a withheld count inside its track, and never sees `dc-fault-engineering`, `where-bess-plugs-in`, `spec-sheet-decoded` or `utility-procurement-meets-ai-load` in its index. *(Corrected 2026-09-17 at v06.38r. This sentence read "or the four guidance market lessons" from S0, when it was written against a planned track. **None of those four existed**: `the-certification-stack`, `what-bankable-means` and `the-china-policy-stack` are §7 rows 22–24 and register zero times, and `reading-the-numbers` is public. Row 21 is the **first** guidance lesson `market-access` has ever carried, so the corpus's gated set is the four named above and the sentence now names what exists. Re-check it as rows 22–24 land rather than trusting the count.)* The provenance gate is only as real as its last check against real content.
11. **What this plan does not decide.** ~~Whether to keep the id `aidc-power-primer`~~ *(moot — retired at the first-five build)*; ~~whether G5 is worth four profiler sessions before track 5's guidance lessons exist~~ *(moot — G5 landed at v04.53r with six utilities, and track 5 now opens on a public utility lesson)*; ~~whether the first five should be authored before or after G1~~ *(moot — G1 landed first, and all five are built)*. What it still does not decide, as of v05.07r: whether the three unlocked-but-unscheduled lessons in §2.3 are ever wanted, and ~~whether the study-pool gap in item 5 is fixed in the pool builder or in the lift script~~ *(decided at S0 — the pool builder, §10.7; and one of the three unscheduled lessons, the G6 lesson, entered §7 as row 26)*. Those are the developer's calls, and the recommendation on each is in the section that raises it. **As of v05.09r it also does not decide the taxonomy** — the nineteen segments and their memberships in `profiler-segments.json` are the developer's to review (S0's "done when"), and §10.11 lists where the application strained, `compute-and-the-rack` first. *(Reviewed 2026-09-07 PM, v05.11r: `compute-and-the-rack` is filled by Phase E group E7 — AMD, Supermicro — not folded; the other §10.11 strains stand as recorded.)*

## 10 · The segment layer — market structure, generated (designed 2026-09-07 at S0, v05.09r)

**Provenance.** The developer's review of Phase 3 (2026-09-07 PM) found the plan short one layer: thirty-four mechanism lessons teach how the machines and the contracts work, and nothing teaches **who sits where** — which companies make, build, buy and back each link of the chain, on what basis, and how that moves. That layer cannot be hand-authored at 154 companies and rising, and it should not be: every dossier already carries `ecosystemRole`, `productsAndServices`, `strategyRead`, `financials`, `relationships[]` and — for 126 of them — `policyExposure[]`. So the layer is **generated** from the registries and the dossiers and **regenerated when they move**, with a human-authored judgment layer on top. The decisions are recorded in `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.2 (three fences moved with approval: the content contract amended, landscapes at the contributor tier, segments as a registry layer); S0 designed what follows. **Nothing in this section is built at v05.09r** — `profiler-segments.json` exists, the generator, the tracks, the lane opener, the health script and the landscape modules do not (S1, S2, K1, K2 of the run order). *K1 (§10.7) landed at v05.10r. **S1's code half landed at v05.17r (2026-09-08)** — the generator (§10.4), the nineteen lessons, the three tracks (§10.5), the health script (§10.9), the content checker's segment assertion and the registry mirror; **S1's lesson half landed at v05.18r (2026-09-08)** — `reading-the-graph` (§10.5), the lane opener, at position 1 of `value-chain-makers`, which closes S1. S2 opened at v05.64r (2026-09-14) with `landscape-storage-integrators-and-containers-2026-09`, the first landscape module and the first guidance module in the fourth lane — **1 of 19; K2 still stands.** Its session also proved §10.6's tier from the real serving path (admin and contributor read it, an analyst is denied) and corrected §10.3's `read-next` row in place.*

### 10.1 · The five-layer model

| Layer | What it is | Unit · gate | Authored by | Refreshed when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1 · The company** | Dossier + study guide — one company's record and what it teaches | `<slug>` · public | Profiler Command (Opus / Fable per the model rule) | The earnings desk and the quarterly sweep |
| **2 · The mechanism** | The 37 hand-authored lessons of §3 and the lane opener — how the thing works, why the buyer asks | lesson · public or guidance | Phase 4 sessions (Opus 5 xhigh) | The C2 pipeline under G3 — contradiction, not novelty |
| **3 · The segment** | Nineteen generated lessons — who sits where in one link of the chain, what is bought there and on what, the numbers, the connections, what moved, the policy fence | `segment-<id>` · **public** (every input public) | `scripts/build-classroom-segments.py`, run in a developer session (S1) | Regenerated when a member dossier, the graph or the segment's membership moves |
| **4 · The landscape** | One human-authored judgment per segment — who dominates and on what basis, who threatens, each player's bet, the indicators, the seller's play | guidance module `landscape-<id>-YYYY-MM` · **contributor** | S2 sessions (Opus 5 xhigh), one per session, only above the floor | The quarterly guidance review; a revision when the segment lesson's `what-moved` contradicts a judgment |
| **5 · The report** | Point-in-time synthesis across dossiers with citations and a coverage block | `reports/<id>` · **admin** | Report Command (Opus 5 xhigh) | Never — superseded by a new edition (Phase 6) |

Two cross-cutting feeds ride the layers rather than sitting in them: the **briefing** (the pipeline's dated edition, any gate the inputs fold to) and the **drill** (every card and quiz item in layers 2–3, plus the opt-in roster deck of §10.8). *A third was designed 2026-09-18 and is not built: the **rehearsal** (C5's scenarios — one named counterparty from a layer-3 roster, rehearsed against layer-4's judgment, contributor-gated by construction, in no deck and no track; `C5-SALES-SIMULATIONS-DESIGN.md`, ledger in §11).* Content flows up the layers and never down the access ladder: a segment lesson is public because its every input is; a landscape is contributor-tier because judgment about named companies is what the 2026-08-29 content-scope rule kept out of public modules — §7.2 moved that fence to contributor, not to public; a report is admin because it ranks. The gate is the stamp's fold in every case (`CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md`), never a field.

**Why generated, and why a human layer above it.** Layer 3 states what the record states — the registry's roles, the dossiers' own product lines and figures, the graph's edges, the developments' dates — and drills structure (the amended content contract). It contains no judgment, so it can be regenerated without anyone re-reading nineteen lessons. Layer 4 is the judgment, and judgment is authored: it cites the dossiers it rests on in a claims ledger and carries a `reviewBy`. The seam between them is the reason the layer can scale with the corpus: S3 adds a dossier, the Profiler Command assigns its segment, the next generator run rewrites one lesson's player table, and the landscape is revised only when the new player changes a judgment.

### 10.2 · The floor rule

**No landscape module is authored for a segment until it holds at least three members with a dossier, including at least one `incumbent` and one `challenger`** (`INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.2; the rule is repeated as `floorRule` in `profiler-segments.json` so a consumer can apply it without this plan). Adjacent members count toward the three and never toward the roles. The segment **lesson** is generated regardless — a thin segment gets a thin, honest lesson ("two members on record") and the health script (§10.9) reports the gap; only the judgment waits. A landscape written below the floor would rank a field the corpus cannot see, which is exactly the report-derived-module failure §7 refuses.

**The floor at S0 (2026-09-07), from `profiler-segments.json` — 283 memberships across all 154 dossiers, none unassigned:**

| # | Segment | Members | Inc · Chal · Adj | Floor | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `cells-and-chemistry` | 16 | 4 · 8 · 4 | yes | Upstream materials arrive as adjacent members (S3 batch 4) |
| 2 | `storage-integrators-and-containers` | 29 | 10 · 8 · 11 | yes | The largest supply segment |
| 3 | `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` | 19 | 9 · 2 · 8 | yes | |
| 4 | `grid-equipment` | 13 | 5 · 1 · 7 | yes | Transmission developers arrive as adjacent (S3 batch 5) |
| 5 | `in-hall-power` | 26 | 10 · 7 · 9 | yes | Includes the UPS and BBU battery line (G9) |
| 6 | `bridge-and-on-site-generation` | 17 | 8 · 5 · 4 | yes | |
| 7 | `clean-firm-and-nuclear` | 16 | 3 · 2 · 11 | yes | Eleven adjacent — the utilities' fleets and the EPCs' nuclear arms |
| 8 | `cooling` | 10 | 4 · 1 · 5 | yes | |
| 9 | `compute-and-the-rack` | 2 | 1 · 0 · 1 | **no** | NVIDIA plus Flex as a system builder; §7.2 counted it above the floor and the dossiers do not. **Developer's decision 2026-09-07 PM: fill it — AMD and Supermicro, Phase E group E7** (§10.11; coverage plan §10) |
| 10 | `epc-and-construction` | 19 | 16 · 2 · 1 | yes | Sixteen incumbents: the corpus covers the top of every ranking it cites |
| 11 | `storage-developers-and-ipps` | 31 | 19 · 8 · 4 | yes | The largest segment; the principal buyer class |
