Sixteen at the floor, three below. **S2's order** (`INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.3 — integrators, cells, developers and IPPs, utilities, power conversion, AIDC landlords, in-hall power, grid equipment, bridge generation, clean firm, hyperscalers, neoclouds, capital, EPC/GC, cooling, compute, software, insurance, assurance) is consistent with this table: the first fifteen are at the floor today, the last four wait on S3 or the developer.

### 10.3 · The segment-lesson template

One lesson per segment, **schema v1, no shape change** — an ordinary `clLesson<Name>_()` literal inside the content fence, distinguishable only by its id prefix and by the fact that a script wrote it. Everything below is fixed so the generator is deterministic and a regeneration diffs cleanly.

- **Identity.** `id` = `segment-<segment id>` (e.g. `segment-cells-and-chemistry`) — permanent, because progress and the roster deck key on it; the segment id is itself permanent (PROFILER-SCHEMA.md → Segments registry). Function name `clLessonSegment<CamelId>_()`. `type` = `module`. `title` = the segment's `name`. `short` = the first sentence of its `definition`, cut at 160 characters at a word boundary. **`group` = `The Value Chain`** — a fourth lane, added because none of the three guidance lanes fits a segment (the schema's own rule: add a lane only when none fits; recorded here as that decision).
- **Dates.** `updated` = the generation date. `reviewBy` = the earliest **future** `effectiveDate` among the members' `policyExposure[]` entries (the fence's own dated gate), else `updated` + 6 months — the guidance discipline's default for slow-moving fundamentals.
- **The stamp** — every member `profile:<slug>` with `date` = that profile's `lastUpdated` **read off the fetched file** (G2), plus `graph:profiler-graph` with `date` = the graph's `built`, plus `concepts:profiler-concepts` with `date` = the file's last commit date on the base revision (PROFILER-SCHEMA.md → Registry revision signals). Every input is `public`, so the stamp folds to **`tracks`** — analyst-visible. No `study:`, no `guidance:`, no `report:` ever enters a segment lesson's stamp: the numbers and the fence are read from the profiles, and the judgment lives in the landscape. A `note` per input records what the lesson took ("member — incumbent" / "edges among members" / "term resolution").
- **Tiles** (up to four): members on record · incumbents · challengers · curated edges among members.
- **No lesson-local glossary.** `{{term}}` tooltips resolve against the concepts registry (1,210 terms); the generator warns on any term it emits that the registry lacks, exactly as `check-classroom-content.py` does.
- **Sections — ten, fixed ids, this order.** A section with nothing on record is still emitted with a single stated line ("No member carries a normalized figure for this period"), never omitted: ids are permanent and P5 never removes one.

| # | Section id | Kind | Generated from | What it says |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `the-segment` | prose | `definition` | What the segment makes or does; one paragraph, the registry's words |
| 2 | `where-it-sits` | table | `position`, `tier`, the segments at `position ± 1`, and the demand segments that buy from it | Upstream · this segment · downstream, and who the buyers are |
| 3 | `what-is-bought-and-on-what` | table | `buyingCriteria[]` | One row per criterion — the criterion and, where a mechanism lesson teaches it, the lesson id |
| 4 | `the-players` | table | `members[]` grouped incumbent → challenger → adjacent | Company · **dossier slug** (the identity the content checker's segment assertion and the roster deck key on — added at S1 because the Classroom renderer has no chip syntax; a clickable dossier chip is a renderer feature for K2 or C3) · role · the `basis` line. **The roster deck's source (§10.8)** |
| 5 | `the-numbers` | table | Each member's `financials.periods[]` **normalized KPI overlay only** — `revenue` (`usdMillions`, `fxBasis`), `gwh-shipped`, `backlog-gwh`, `mw-energized`, `mw-contracted` (`qty`) for the latest annual period, with `periodEnd` | The comparable figures the dossiers already carry, and only those; a member with no overlay row is listed as such. Never a figure the prose does not contain (the overlay's own rule) |
| 6 | `who-is-connected` | table | `profiler-graph.json` edges where both ends are members, then edges from members to the segments at ± 1 — curated edges first with `type` · `status` · `scale` · `via`, derived-only edges as a count | The relationship map of the segment, exactly as the graph states it |
| 7 | `what-moved` | timeline | Members' `recentDevelopments[]`, newest first, capped at twelve, each item `date` · headline · the member | The last twelve months of the segment on the record; the pins say when it was read |
| 8 | `the-fence` | table | Members' `policyExposure[]` — regime × status × the members exposed, `effectiveDate` where stated | Which policy regimes bear on this segment and on whom; the source of `reviewBy` |
| 9 | `read-next` | callout | A fixed map inside the generator: segment → the §3 mechanism lessons that teach its technology and its market; plus the landscape module id when one exists — **measured 2026-09-14 at S2 session 1, the first time this branch had a module to name: the sentence is emitted unconditionally and every tier reads it, including an analyst.** The parenthetical this row previously carried ("the renderer applies the reader's gate; a tier that cannot read it sees nothing") described a per-sentence gate the generator's template and the `cl*` renderer do not have and never had. It is not an access defect and needs no fix: what leaks is a module **id**, the sentence says in its own words that the module is for the tiers holding guidance access, and the module itself is denied at the server — verified from the real `handleGuidanceOp_` path, where an analyst gets `ROLE_DENIED` on both `gop=index` and `gop=doc`. Recorded here rather than silently corrected in the generator, because a future session reading the old parenthetical would have 'fixed' a renderer that was never broken; plus the members' study guides | Where to go from here |
| 10 | `check-yourself` | quiz | Five structural items generated from the registry: which segment a member sits in; which role the registry gives it; which criterion a buyer here buys on; which segment is upstream; which two members the graph connects and how | The amended content contract's structure drill — never a figure for its own sake |

No `flashcards` section: the roster deck (§10.8) enumerates its cards from `the-players` rows, so a segment lesson adds nothing to the mechanism deck and adds to the roster deck only when an account opts in.

- **Revisions.** The first generation carries no `revisions[]` entry. Every regeneration that changes anything appends exactly one — `{ "date": <generation date>, "note": "regenerated: <the inputs that moved, as ref old→new>", "changed": [<the section ids whose generated JSON differs>] }` — computed by **diffing the sections as P8 does** (parsed JSON, base → head), so `changed[]` is the differs set and nothing else, and the freshness delta reaches every account that completed a section. `updated` advances; pins advance only on the inputs that moved and only to the dates read off them.

### 10.4 · The generator — `scripts/build-classroom-segments.py` (S1, Fable 5.1 High; built v05.17r) — **BACKLOG CLEARED v06.50r (2026-09-18): `--check` reads 0**

- **Inputs:** `profiler-segments.json`, `profiler-companies.json`, every member's `<slug>.profile.json`, `profiler-graph.json`, `profiler-concepts.json`, and the base revision's commit date for the concepts file. **Output:** the nineteen `clLessonSegment*_()` literals in `Classroom.gs`, inside the content fence — a new literal appended after the last existing literal and before the registries, a regenerated one **replaced in place, byte for byte where nothing moved** — plus the ids appended to `clLessons_()` and to the three Value Chain tracks' `lessons[]` in chain order (first run only).
- **Stable ids, fixed sections, deterministic output.** Same inputs → the same bytes: sorted members, sorted edges, stable JSON key order, no timestamps but `updated`. That is what makes "regenerate only what moved" a diff rather than a rewrite.
- **Regenerate only segments whose members moved.** A segment is **due** when any of: a member profile's `lastUpdated` is later than its pin; the graph's `built` is later than its pin; the concepts file's commit date is later than its pin; the segment's member set or any role in the registry differs from the lesson's `the-players`; the registry's `definition` or `buyingCriteria` differ from sections 1–3. `--check` lists due segments and exits 1 if any (the health script calls it); `--segment <id>` regenerates one; `--all` forces all nineteen; `--today` sets the generation date for a reproducible run. Nothing due → nothing written, and the run says so.
- **⚠️ MEASURED 2026-09-09 (v05.23r): the pin design makes every segment lesson due after every dossier session, so "regenerate only what moved" currently degenerates to "regenerate all nineteen".** `--check` reports **19 of 19 due** — but the causes are very unequal: **3** differ in **no section at all** (only the pins moved), **11** differ in exactly one section (`where-it-sits`, the cross-segment context line that shifts whenever any segment's membership changes), and only **5** differ substantively (`cooling`, `compute-and-the-rack`, `assurance`, `software-and-optimization`, `insurance-and-risk-transfer`). The mechanism is that every lesson pins `concepts:profiler-concepts` and `graph:profiler-graph`, and **every dossier session moves both** — S3 P2 alone added 44 concepts and rebuilt the graph. So the "due" signal is dominated by two inputs that always move, and it no longer discriminates between a lesson whose players changed and one whose bytes are identical. **Two things to decide before the next regeneration run:** whether the concepts and graph pins should be coarser (or compared by content rather than by date) so the signal means something again, and whether a run that rewrites three lessons byte-for-byte is worth the commit. Neither is urgent; both get worse with every S3 session.
- **Ownership of a regeneration run — clarified 2026-09-09 after two sessions recorded it wrongly.** The v05.20r and v05.21r changelogs both state that regenerating a segment lesson is "a developer/S1 job", and v05.22r repeated it. **That is a misreading of the bullet below**, which says the developer *or the S1/S3 session* may run it. S1 is closed, S2 authors landscape modules (a different artefact), and the quarterly clock only *reports* staleness — so on the "developer/S1" reading nobody owns it and the backlog is permanent. The correct reading is that **any S3 session may run it**, and the reason to run it as its own developer session instead is mechanical rather than jurisdictional: a nineteen-lesson regeneration breaches `check-classroom-pipeline.py`'s P10 caps (as S1's own run did, recorded and accepted), so it wants its own commit, its own GAS version bump and its own checker pass rather than riding along with a dossier push.
- **Where a regeneration run belongs in the programme — answered 2026-09-09.** Per segment, **inside that segment's S2 session**, not as a scheduled big-bang. §10.6 requires the segment lesson's `read-next` to deep-link to the landscape module, so the lesson **must** be regenerated after its module exists — which makes S2 the natural and in fact obligatory owner, one `--segment <id>` run per session, well inside P10's caps. That leaves the standing 19-due backlog, and it does not need clearing first — **but the split above was measured 2026-09-09, before S3 closed, and it went badly stale.** **RE-MEASURED 2026-09-14 (v05.62r)** — the 2026-09-09 figures of "5 substantive, 14 bookkeeping" were wrong by then: it was **12 substantive, 7 bookkeeping**. Of the 19, **1** differed in no section at all (`clean-firm-and-nuclear` — only pins moved), **6** differed only in the cross-segment context sections (`where-it-sits` / `who-is-connected`, plus `read-next` for `bridge-and-on-site-generation`), and **12** differed in `the-players` — which is the assertion `check-classroom-content.py` enforces, so those twelve were failing it at two errors each: **the standing 24-error baseline every Classroom commit was diffing against**. **20 distinct companies were absent from the segment lesson that should have listed them** (`albemarle`, `amd`, `aon`, `calb`, `cornex`, `csa-group`, `great-power`, `grid-united`, `gridmatic`, `habitat-energy`, `heron-power`, `intertek`, `kwh-analytics`, `marsh-mclennan`, `mitra-chem`, `novonix`, `pattern-energy`, `stem`, `supermicro`, `ul-solutions`), plus 3 dossiers revised since their pins; `insurance-and-risk-transfer` carried **no player rows at all**. **Cleared 2026-09-14 in the single developer run this bullet allows** (`--all`, v05.62r): all nineteen regenerated, the content checker taken from **24 errors to 0**, and `--check` back to 0 due. **The lesson for the next re-measure: do not trust a recorded split — run `--check` and read the `sections differing` list, because the "due" causes drift much faster than the count does.**
- **THE SECOND DEVELOPER REGENERATION RUN — v06.50r, 2026-09-18, and `--check` reads 0 for the first time since the generator was built at v05.17r.** One `--all --today 2026-09-18`, briefed as `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.58. The nine due segments were rewritten and the other ten were not: `--all` marks all nineteen `forced`, but the write loop skips `forced and not secs and not moved`, and no section builder reads `self.today`, so **9 lessons were written, not 19** — (rr18). All nine differed on `read-next`, six of them also on `what-is-bought-and-on-what`, and **`the-players` was in none of them**, which is why the run moved **0 of the roster deck's 314 cards** (id set, hash multiset and every id→hash pair identical base to head — (rr20)). **`inputs moved: none` on all nine and `no pin moved` nine times in the run log**, so every revision note reads *"regenerated: registry or graph content moved with no pin change"*; `updated` advanced on nine and `reviewBy` on only four — the four on the `updated + 6 months` default, the other five pinned to a future policy `effectiveDate`. **The `(planned)` leak went 28 → 0** across those same nine segments over 12 titles, closing (qq1)/(xi) by clearing rather than by measuring. Checker signature: **P10 as forecast** (9 revised against a cap of 3 — the second time this bullet's own allowance has breached it), **P12 until the version bump**, **no P2, no P8** (the generator's `changed[]` matched the real differs set on all nine, verified outside the checker — (rr19)), and **no P3**: `gateDigest` was left exactly as K2 set it. Content checker unchanged at **56 / 8 / 192, 0 errors / 0 warnings**, module assertion 28.
- **What this run settles about the two staleness numbers, because the brief conflated them — (rr17).** `check-classroom-curriculum.py --strict` prints **"N stale pin(s) on the hand-authored lessons"** four lines above the generator's own `--check` output, and its loop `continue`s on every `segment-` id. **A regeneration pass cannot move the stale-pin count and never could**: 28 before, 28 after, `--check` 9 → 0. When a brief or a handover names "stale pins" as a regeneration's target, it is naming the wrong number.
- **A developer-session tool, not a pipeline one.** The Routine never runs it: a run that regenerated nineteen lessons would fail P10's caps and, more to the point, a regeneration is not a G3 contradiction — it is the record restated. The developer (or the S1/S3 session) runs it inside a session, then does what every content commit does: bumps the GAS version, writes the generic changelog line ("Curriculum updated"), runs `check-classroom-content.py`, `check-classroom-pipeline.py --base origin/main` (expect P1 on the developer files and P5 on the first run's registry inserts; **never P3** — the generator touches no gate symbol, so `gateDigest` is unchanged), `--selftest`, `node --check` and `check-gas-inner-scripts.js`. The pipeline treats `segment-*` lessons as **frozen**: it never revises one, and a run whose pin table finds a segment lesson's inputs moved reports it under `Segment lessons due for regeneration` rather than acting (`classroom-app.md`, the content-contract section).
- **What it never does.** No prose beyond the registry's own sentences and the dossiers' own headlines; no ranking beyond the registry's role; no `strategyRead` (that is analysis, and analysis is the landscape's); no `study:` or report input; no field the overlay does not carry; no invented figure; no new section id, ever.
- **Verification it carries itself:** every `profile:` ref resolved by opening the file and matching `slug` (G7); every emitted `{{term}}` checked against the registry; the ten section ids present in order; the-players rows equal to the registry's member set; JSON strictness (double-quoted keys, no trailing commas). Then the two Classroom checkers judge the commit as they judge any.

### 10.5 · The Value Chain lane — three tracks and the opener

A **fourth lane**, `The Value Chain`, in both apps' `group` vocabulary (Classroom lessons and tracks; the guidance hub for the landscape modules). Three tracks, **contiguous position ranges** so each reads as one stretch of the chain, all public, prerequisites advisory as always:

| Track id | Title | Lessons (chain order) | Prereqs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `value-chain-makers` | The Value Chain I — Who Makes It | `reading-the-graph` → `segment-cells-and-chemistry` → `segment-storage-integrators-and-containers` → `segment-power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` | none — `reading-the-graph` is the floor |
| `value-chain-builders` | The Value Chain II — Who Builds and Powers It | `segment-grid-equipment` → `segment-in-hall-power` → `segment-bridge-and-on-site-generation` → `segment-clean-firm-and-nuclear` → `segment-cooling` → `segment-compute-and-the-rack` → `segment-epc-and-construction` | `value-chain-makers` |
| `value-chain-buyers-and-backers` | The Value Chain III — Who Buys It and Who Backs It | `segment-storage-developers-and-ipps` → `segment-aidc-developers-and-landlords` → `segment-hyperscalers-and-ai-labs` → `segment-neoclouds` → `segment-utilities` → `segment-capital` → `segment-assurance` → `segment-software-and-optimization` → `segment-insurance-and-risk-transfer` | `value-chain-makers` |

Four, seven and nine lessons — every track under the twelve §2.2 refuses. **`clTracks_()` order:** the three follow `market-access`, so the study-next pointer reaches the chain after the mechanism curriculum; a reader who wants structure first opens the lane. Track `short` lines: I — "Finish this and you can read the relationship graph, and say who makes the cell, the container and the converter and on what each is bought." II — "…say who connects it, powers it, cools it and builds it, and who sets the calendar." III — "…say who buys it, who backs it, who assures it, who optimises it and who insures it — and which desk signs."

**`reading-the-graph` — the lane opener, authored inside S1 (Opus 5 xhigh), the one hand-authored lesson in the lane. Built v05.18r (2026-09-08); the id, the gate and the nine sections below are now fixed for life.** Public — the stamp is all-public and folds to `tracks`; `group` The Value Chain; inputs `graph:profiler-graph`@2026-09-08 (the file's `built`) · `concepts:profiler-concepts`@2026-09-07 (the file's last commit date on `origin/main`) · four `profile:` refs as the worked example, each at its own dossier's `lastUpdated` — `profile:fluidstack` / `profile:hut-8` / `profile:terawulf`@2026-09-06 and `profile:google`@2026-09-07, the fourth added because the backstop's own typings are what make the chain's disagreement legible. It teaches what an edge is (curated against derived; the evidence sentences; `last`), the seven relationship types and their inverses (`customer`↔`supplier`, `investor`↔`portfolio`; `partner`, `competitor` and `other` self-inverse), the chips (`status`, `since`, `scale`, `via`, `project`, `source`), inbound against outbound, the `#network` explorer and the Relationships tab — **stated honestly as contributor+ surfaces, with the segment lessons' `who-is-connected` tables named as the analyst's door onto the same edges** — and, because the segment lessons assume it, what a segment is, what the three roles mean (the registry's `roles` definitions quoted verbatim), how to read a player table and a `who-is-connected` section, and that a role and an edge type are different axes. **Sections as built, nine, fixed ids, this order:** `what-an-edge-is` · `seven-types-two-directions` · `reading-the-chips` · `one-chain-three-typings` (the worked example — the credit-substitution chain typed **supplier** by the tenant, **customer** by a landlord and **competitor** between the two landlords, with the backstop typed **partner** by Google and **investor** by TeraWulf) · `the-explorer` · `segments-and-roles` · `where-it-fails` (a cross-mention read as a deal; a `partner` taken at face value; one counterparty read as "the customer" when tenant of record and end user are different companies; `status` read as current) · `drill` (8 cards) · `check-yourself` (5 items). A ten-entry `glossary[]` carries the graph vocabulary the concepts registry lacks (`relationship graph`, `edge`, `curated edge`, `derived edge`, `cross-mention`, `inverse pair`, `segment`, `incumbent`, `challenger`, `adjacent`); no dated gate applies, so `reviewBy` is `updated` + 6 months (2027-03-08). Inserted at **position 1** of `value-chain-makers` — a reorder, so the pipeline checker's P5 reports it; accepted as a developer-session write because the track is a position-ordered reading list and no account held progress on it.

### 10.6 · The landscape module (S2, Opus 5 xhigh, one segment per session, only above the floor)

- **Shape and home.** A **guidance module** — the `industry guidance:` machinery (`.claude/rules/industry-guidance.md`): the analysis markdown at `repository-information/industry-guidance/landscape-<segment-id>-analysis.md` as the source of truth, and the module JSON in `guidanceDocs_()` — in `Profiler.gs` until C3, in `Classroom.gs` after it (C3 runs before S2 passes about six modules for exactly this reason, §7.4). Id `landscape-<segment-id>-YYYY-MM`; lane **`The Value Chain`**; tier **contributor** (`GUIDANCE_ROLES` unchanged — the §7.2 decision put judgment at contributor, not admin). The `**Provenance:**` line reads "Corpus synthesis over the segment's member dossiers at the versions in the claims ledger; no ingested document" — the G4 precedent, six of eight modules being research-based.
- **The content-scope exception.** The 2026-08-29 directive keeps single-company analysis out of guidance modules; a landscape names and ranks the segment's covered companies by construction. The exception is the developer's (2026-09-07, §7.2 decision 2), recorded in `industry-guidance.md` at S0 as the one module class that does — and it is still guidance to a *group*: the seller reading it, about the players it sells against and to.
- **Sections** (guidance vocabulary): `who-dominates-and-on-what-basis` (prose — the incumbents, and the third-party or dossier basis for each) · `who-threatens` (prose — the challengers and the route each is attacking on) · `each-players-bet` (table — one row per incumbent and challenger: the bet, drawn from `strategyRead[]` and labeled as analysis, never blended with fact) · `the-indicators` (table — what to watch, dated where the record dates it) · `the-sellers-play` (callout — for a BESS or AIDC-power seller, per §10.10's two paths) · `claims-ledger` (kind `ledger` — **every claim → `profile:<slug>` at its `profileVersion`, and the dossier field it rests on**; dossiers already cite sources, so the ledger cites dossiers) · `what-the-record-does-not-say` (callout) · `drill` · `check-yourself` (judgment questions at the group level).
- **`reviewBy`** = the nearest dated gate among the members' `policyExposure[]` and the developments the module leans on, else six months from `updated`. **Admin extras** — the ranking a report makes — reach admins only through a report's `guidanceOverlays[]` anchored on the module's sections (the Admin lens), which is why Phases 5–6 precede S2: a landscape must not anchor on an edition Phase 5 retires.
- **The Classroom side.** Pre-C3 the segment lesson's `read-next` deep-links to the module for tiers holding `guidance`; post-C3 the module renders in Classroom under C3's own checklist. No Classroom lesson wraps a landscape — the module *is* the contributor's layer-4 read, and wrapping it would add a progress tick and nothing taught (§7's argument against report modules, applied here).
- **Order:** S2 runs the segments in the `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.3 order and skips any segment below the floor on the day.
- **Built so far — 19 of 19 — S2 CLOSED (updated 2026-09-17, v06.45r).** `landscape-storage-integrators-and-containers-2026-09` (v05.64r, 2026-09-14), the first; `landscape-cells-and-chemistry-2026-09` (v05.70r, 2026-09-14), the second; `landscape-storage-developers-and-ipps-2026-09` (v05.74r, 2026-09-14), the third; `landscape-utilities-2026-09` (v05.78r, 2026-09-14), the fourth; `landscape-power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon-2026-09` (v05.83r, 2026-09-15), the fifth; `landscape-aidc-developers-and-landlords-2026-09` (v05.88r, 2026-09-15), the sixth; `landscape-in-hall-power-2026-09` (v05.93r, 2026-09-15), the seventh; `landscape-grid-equipment-2026-09` (v05.97r, 2026-09-15), the eighth; `landscape-bridge-and-on-site-generation-2026-09` (v06.03r, 2026-09-15), the ninth; `landscape-clean-firm-and-nuclear-2026-09` (v06.07r, 2026-09-16), the tenth; `landscape-hyperscalers-and-ai-labs-2026-09` (v06.11r, 2026-09-16), the eleventh; `landscape-neoclouds-2026-09` (v06.15r, 2026-09-16), the twelfth; `landscape-capital-2026-09` (v06.24r, 2026-09-16), the thirteenth; `landscape-epc-and-construction-2026-09` (v06.28r, 2026-09-16), the fourteenth; `landscape-cooling-2026-09` (v06.32r, 2026-09-17), the fifteenth; `landscape-compute-and-the-rack-2026-09` (v06.36r, 2026-09-17), the sixteenth; `landscape-software-and-optimization-2026-09` (v06.40r, 2026-09-17), the seventeenth; `landscape-insurance-and-risk-transfer-2026-09` (v06.43r, 2026-09-17), the eighteenth; and `landscape-assurance-2026-09` (v06.45r, 2026-09-17), **the nineteenth and last — the module that closes S2**. Three things the first settled that the next eighteen inherited: **(a)** the nine section ids above are used verbatim as the module's section ids, so progress ticks and drill items key on the same names in every landscape; **(b)** backticks render **literally** through `clFmt` — it resolves `**bold**`, `*italic*` and `{{term}}` and nothing else — so a ledger's source column is written as plain text (`profile:sungrow @ v9 — ecosystemRole`), and the backticked form belongs only in the analysis markdown; **(c)** the registry count in `scripts/check-classroom-content.py`'s gate truth table is hard-coded on purpose and must be bumped in the same commit that registers a module (9 → 10 here).
- **What session 2 added to that list (v05.70r).** **(d)** `reviewBy` is read, not sorted: the nearest dated gate can sit **inside a `policyExposure[]` entry's `exposure` prose** rather than in its `effectiveDate` field, because one entry can carry a two-step schedule with only the first step in the field. `cells-and-chemistry` resolved to **2027-01-01** on an export-rebate cliff stated in prose by two members, where a sort of the fence returns 2027-02 and the generator returns 2027-10-01 — a ten-month spread across three defensible readings, and the reason §10.6 says *gate* rather than *effectiveDate*. **(e)** The Scraper seed of step 9 is **conditional and was correctly skipped here**: `topic-bess-technology` already owns the cell and chemistry layer, which session 1's own seed comment says in as many words, so no `Scraper.gs` edit was made. Check the roster before adding; a near-duplicate seed double-counts an article in the digest's topic band. **(f)** A landscape's prose **names the players** — the developer-approved exception exists for exactly that, and an anonymised threat section ("one challenger…") is weaker guidance, not safer guidance.
- **What session 4 added (v05.78r).** **(j)** A landscape can land on a segment an **existing guidance module already partly covers**, and when it does, the split is written **before** the module is. `utility-aidc-procurement-2026-08` owns the procurement **process** (the four moves, the tariff terms, the five BESS channels, the buyer map, the calendar); `landscape-utilities-2026-09` owns the **parties** (the instrument each franchise built, the bets, the disintermediation routes, and the four members the playbook never covers). The landscape deliberately carries **no** tariff table, **no** channel list and **no** buyer map, names the other module by title in its opening paragraph, and closes `check-yourself` on the distinction. The test that a split is genuine: the one number both modules cite does a **different job** in each. **(k)** `reviewBy` read-don't-sort can return a date **nearer** than the sort, not merely a different one — `utilities` resolved to **2026-10-01** out of one member's `policyExposure[]` prose (an Alabama threshold statute) while the only future `effectiveDate` among 76 entries was 2027-01-01, **seventeen days after `updated`**. So this is the first module to ship inside its own 30-day review horizon: `clReviewChip` renders it gold immediately and `check-classroom-curriculum.py` reports 1 item due while still exiting 0, because review dates never call `strict()`. Both are the rule working; a six-month default would have been a date field telling a lie the module's own prose contradicts. **(l)** **Read the registry's `notes` before sizing a threat section.** Here it says in as many words that regulated franchises have no challenger by construction and that competitive retail suppliers carry the role — so `challenger` is a **structural placeholder**, the two carrying it outrank four of the six incumbents by revenue, and the real threat is disintermediation carried by the *adjacents*. A threat section proportioned to the previous module would have missed all of it. **(m)** **The `each-players-bet` table shrinks as honestly as it grows** — eight rows here against session 3's twenty-seven, because six franchises and two retailers is the entire ranked roster. Section proportions follow the segment, never the last module.

- **What session 3 added (v05.74r).** **(g)** `reviewBy` may have **nothing to sort at all**: `storage-developers-and-ipps` carries 142 `policyExposure[]` entries, 96 with an `effectiveDate`, and **not one of those 96 is in the future** — so the generator's rule falls through to the six-month default and only a hand-authored module can find the real gate, which here is a **cost ratio indexed to the calendar year** stated in two members' prose. Session 2's finding was that a sort returns the *wrong* answer; this is the case where it returns *none*. **(h)** The `each-players-bet` table scales: twenty-seven rows rendered clean at 1400px, so §10.6's one-row-per-incumbent-and-challenger rule needs no cap, and on a segment whose distinguishing fact is the **size** of its incumbent set, trimming the table would destroy the thing the module is about. **(i)** The Scraper seed of step 9 is **conditional both ways, and the check must run against every existing term, not every existing seed label**: this session's answer was yes, but its first draft duplicated `topic-storage-offtake`'s contract vocabulary and had to be re-scoped to the **ownership** layer — the residue that no seed scored.

- **What session 5 added (v05.83r).** **(n)** **The split rule scales to a TOTAL overlap, and the test still works.** Session 4's case was a module covering a *process* that ran over its segment; here `nvidia-800vdc-2026-08` covers the **architecture of the exact layer** `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` is defined as — the segment's own one-sentence definition reads as that module's table of contents. The defensible line is **architecture versus parties**: that module says what the voltage change is, why it happens and what it deletes, and is *deliberately vendor-blind* (its own guardrails section says it names no power-equipment vendors); this one says who stands where along it. §7.23's test — the one number both cite doing a different job in each — held on **~2029, the next-generation SST launch**: a *roadmap gate* there (what can I build, and when) and a *position clock* here (who is exposed, and for how long). **Write the omissions down.** Six things the other module owns are enumerated in the new function's header comment and in §2 of the analysis file, precisely so a later revision cannot import them and collapse the split. **(o)** **A segment can have more than one overlapping module, and the second kind overlaps a *criterion* rather than the layer.** Two policy modules — `eo14420-bulk-power-2026-08` and `china-policy-stack-2026-08` — own the manufacturing-location buying criterion, so this is the first landscape with **three** registered modules bearing on its segment. The line there is **instrument versus sorting**: those modules define the origin test; the landscape says only which members sit which side of it, and why that re-sorts the roster in a way the role labels do not predict. **(p)** **A landscape whose own `segment-*` lesson is ALREADY due cannot raise the due count** — and four consecutive briefs carried the opposite model. Sessions 1–4 each saw N → N+1 → N because their own segment was *not* in the standing due set; session 5's was the first of eight, so registering the module changed its `read-next` without changing its membership and the measurement was **8 → 8 → 7**. Predict from whether your own segment is already due, not from the previous session's arithmetic. **(q)** **State a brief's prior as a prior.** §7.25 said the Scraper-seed answer was "likely no" because existing terms already scored the layer; run against **every term** rather than every seed label (per (i)), all **390** across both arrays, the wide-bandgap silicon layer and the last centimetre scored **zero** — two of the five sub-layers the segment's own definition names, where one of its nine incumbents lives entirely and where two of its six buying criteria sit. The seed was added and re-scoped once before landing. **(r)** **`reviewBy` read-don't-sort produced a third distinct failure mode for the sort, and a rejection worth copying**: the nearest gate (2026-11-27) beat a *genuine* competing gate twenty-seven days later, which was rejected not for distance but because it is **already another module's own `reviewBy`** — taking it would have duplicated that module's clock instead of setting this one's. A gate a module watches but does not own belongs in `the-indicators`, not in `reviewBy`.

- **What session 6 added (v05.88r).** **(s)** **A landscape can have to split against a public LESSON, and that split crosses an ACCESS TIER rather than only a subject.** `aidc-developers-and-landlords` has **four** neighbours, the most any landscape has faced: three registered modules (`large-load-interconnection-2026-09` above the fence, `utility-aidc-procurement-2026-08` on the process, `power-infra-aidc-2026-08` on the chain) and — new — the Phase 4 row 9 mechanism lesson `the-campus-as-a-power-project`, which is **public** and built on nine of this segment's own members, with row 10's `how-a-storage-project-happens` touching two more. The line is one sentence and it generalises: **the lesson teaches the test, and the module applies it to named parties.** So the module states where a named challenger's "1.8 GW delivered or under development" headline actually sits (about 62 MW operating) without restating the five-rung ladder that makes the reading possible, and names the four companies in one lease chain without re-teaching that landlord, tenant of record, credit support and end user are routinely four different parties. **It matters more than a module split because the gates differ**: material drifting from the module into the lesson's territory is material an analyst can already read, and material drifting the other way is a gate raised on something already public. **(t)** **The neighbour can pre-declare the handoff, and this one did.** One of that lesson's four tiles reads *0 league tables — no ranking of these landlords exists in this corpus and this lesson does not invent one*, which is layer 4's job by construction (§10.1). **A landscape should check its public neighbours' tiles and callouts for exactly this before drafting**: a declined judgment upstream is the cleanest possible statement of where the boundary runs. §7.23's test held on the **pre-leased share falling 64 % → 54 % while capacity underway rose 82 %** — an *arithmetic exercise* in the lesson (convert a percentage into exposure) and a *solitary market gauge* in the module (the only speculative-build number the segment has, because eighteen of thirty members publish nothing). **Nine omissions across all four neighbours** are enumerated in the function's header comment and in §2 of the analysis file. **(u)** **The `reviewBy` rejection class can fire on the NEAREST candidate, and on more than one candidate.** Session 5's (r) rejected a gate twenty-seven days *behind* the one taken; here the **genuine nearest** gate — 10 December 2026, carried by three members' prose — is already the `reviewBy` of **two** registered modules, so it went to `the-indicators` as row 1 rather than becoming a third module ringing the same bell. A second candidate was rejected **twice over** (one day after `updated`, *and* squarely a neighbour module's subject). The date taken, 2026-12-15, was read out of policy **prose** while that entry's `effectiveDate` holds the first step — the pattern (d) records — where a sort of the fence would have returned **2027-12-31**, a tariff expiry, because exactly two of 84 dated entries across 107 lie in the future and both are the same schedule. **(v)** **A brief's Scraper-seed prior can be HALF right, which is a third outcome beyond (i)'s yes and (e)'s no.** §7.27 named five candidate gaps; run against all **392** terms per (i), **two were already covered** — the co-location statutes and the buildout shape — while the segment's own *commercial* layer scored zero across the board (`powered land`, `land entitlement`, `build-to-suit`, `data center ABS`, `tenant credit`, `take-or-pay lease`, `bitcoin miner`), including two of its six buying criteria. **And a new reason to leave a zero-scoring term out**: `special contract` and `energy service agreement` both score zero and were dropped on **split grounds rather than duplication grounds**, because they are `utility-aidc-procurement-2026-08`'s instruments and seeding them here would blur in the digest exactly the line the module draws in the curriculum. **(w)** **The netting rule of (p) held on its second run, and the row-10 deepening did NOT fire.** `--check` went **8 → 8 → 7** with this segment already sixth of the eight, and it differed in `read-next` alone both before and after. So deepening is a real possibility rather than a regular one: **predict the membership delta and the per-segment section delta, and record which halves actually fired.**

- **What session 7 added (v05.93r).** **(x1)** **ELEVEN neighbours is the new record, six of them PUBLIC lessons, and the organising finding is that ALL SIX of the segment's buying criteria have their mechanism owned by one of them.** `in-hall-power` sits where the public AIDC-power curriculum was built: criteria 1 and 3 belong to `the-ups-room`, 2 to that lesson and `the-aidc-power-chain`, 4 to that lesson and `bess-tech-fundamentals-2026-08`, 5 to `nvidia-800vdc-2026-08` and `the-800-vdc-shift`, 6 to `grid-equipment-shortage-2026-09`. **So the module owns none of the six tests and only says who sits where on each** — which is the sharpest statement of layer 4's job any landscape has had to make. **Nineteen** omissions across the eleven are enumerated in the header comment and in §2e of the analysis file, against session 6's nine. The corollary is worth keeping: a crowded neighbourhood does not make a landscape thin, because **none of the eleven names a vendor in a ranking** — the six lessons anonymise by construction and the two nearest modules are deliberately vendor-blind. **(x2)** **THE FIRST LANDSCAPE-AGAINST-LANDSCAPE SPLIT, and the line is a ROLE INVERSION rather than a subject.** Eight members sit in both `in-hall-power` and `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon`, and **five carry a different role in each** — `schneider-electric`, `eaton` and `abb` are incumbent here and adjacent there; `delta-electronics` is adjacent here and incumbent there; `heron-power` is adjacent here and challenger there. **The registry draws the split itself**, so no arbitration was needed: that module ranks the conversion layer, this one ranks the room. §7.23's test held on **`late 2027`**, one member's mass-production date — an *arrival risk on a challenger's own business* there, a *reprieve clock on an installed product category* here. **When the neighbour is another landscape, look at the roles before looking at the subjects.** **(x3)** **Session 6's (t) paid three times over, and one pre-declaration supplied a glossary term.** Three of the six public neighbours pre-declare the handoff in their own text: `the-ups-room` closes a callout on a paragraph headed *"The honest limit, and where this hands off"* and states that what is in play is **the marginal megawatt in new AI halls** — adopted verbatim as a module glossary entry; `redundancy-by-the-numbers` states that it *"deliberately does not describe the machine being deleted"*; and `where-bess-plugs-in` answers **"No"** on this segment's socket and then says **"Keep watching it anyway"**, so the module's storage-seller path is written as *what the watching looks like* rather than as competing advice. **Read the public neighbours' callouts before drafting, not only their tiles.** **(x4)** **`reviewBy` read-don't-sort produced a fifth distinct failure mode and a new sub-field.** 81 `policyExposure[]` entries, 29 dated, **exactly three in the future**, and the nearest is an adjacent member's **on-highway engine model year** — a sort would have clocked an in-hall-power module on a truck. The date taken, **2026-10-01**, came out of `policyExposure[0].`**`mitigation`**, a field no previous session has found a gate in, on an entry carrying **no `effectiveDate` at all**. Five rejections in writing, including a CFO retirement **on the same calendar day** — the trap of taking the nearest *date* rather than the nearest *gate*. The module ships inside its own 30-day horizon, so `check-classroom-curriculum.py` reports **2 items due** while still exiting 0: **(k) firing a second time.** **(x5)** **(w) was satisfied on both halves and the deepening did not fire, so the rule now reads 2-for-3.** `--check` went **10 → 10 → 9** with this segment already the first of the ten, differing in `read-next` **and** `what-is-bought-and-on-what` before and clearing both on regeneration. Row 11 predicted a deepening that fired; sessions 6 and 7 predicted none and none fired. **Deepening is a real possibility rather than a regular one — keep predicting both halves and keep recording which fired.** **(x6)** **The Scraper-seed answer was yes, and the gap was a whole half of the segment rather than a residue.** Against all **399** terms per (i), most of the vocabulary was already covered — by **five different segment seeds**, which is why the label-level glance the rule forbids would have returned the wrong answer — but the **rotary machine, the store under it and the packaging axis scored zero across the board**, which is buying criteria 2, 3 and 4. Four terms were dropped as superstrings of existing ones and **three on split grounds** per (v).

- **What session 8 added (v05.97r).** **(y1)** **THE FIRST SEGMENT WITH A BUILT LANDSCAPE ON BOTH SIDES OF IT IN THE CHAIN, AND BOTH SPLITS ARE ROLE INVERSIONS THAT POINT IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS.** Session 7's (x2) found the first landscape-against-landscape split and a role inversion; `grid-equipment` sits at position 4 between two built landscapes and inverts against both. Eight members are shared with `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` upstream and **five carry a different role** — three of this segment's five incumbents (`hitachi-energy`, `ge-vernova`, `abb`) are *adjacent* there, while `zhonhen` is adjacent here and incumbent there and `flex` adjacent here and challenger there. Six are shared with `in-hall-power` downstream and **four invert the other way** — `eaton`, `schneider-electric` and `zhonhen` are adjacent here and *incumbent* there. **The direction is the finding**: the members a landscape ranks highest are downgraded upstream and the members it calls adjacent are upgraded downstream, because **each landscape ranks the members whose centre of gravity sits at its own chain position**. Two members are incumbent on both sides of the fence line and on neither side of the conversion split (`abb`, `mitsubishi-electric`). **When a landscape has a neighbour on each side, read the roles for their direction and not only for the line they draw.** **(y2)** **§10.6 (t) FIRED ON A MODULE RATHER THAN A LESSON.** `grid-equipment-shortage-2026-09` is a module about this exact segment's scarcity — its title is this segment's first buying criterion — and its own buyer callout pre-declares the handoff: *"Reserve the slot early… in a shortage the queue position is the thing being bought, not the transformer."* It teaches the instrument and names no holder. §7.23's test held on the **transformer lead time**: a *market condition* there (a dated series with a measurement basis, produced by four independent constraints, taught vendor-blind) and **one named vendor's own quoted wait travelling with that vendor's own reservation instrument** here. **Check a guidance neighbour's callouts for a pre-declaration, not only a public lesson's.** **(y3)** **THE FIRST LANDSCAPE WRITTEN AGAINST A HALF-UNBUILT CURRICULUM, and the module names the gap rather than filling it.** `READ_NEXT` names four mechanism lessons for this segment and **only two are built**; session 7's segment had all six of its buying criteria taught by a neighbour, and here **two of the six have no complete owner** (criterion 2 has none at all; criterion 4's protection lesson is Phase 4 row 13, unbuilt). The module states the gap, points at the planned lesson and says who sits where — **a landscape that starts teaching a criterion because nobody else does stops being a landscape.** **Thirty-five omissions across six neighbours** are enumerated in the header comment and in §2e of the analysis file, against session 7's nineteen across eleven: fewer neighbours, deeper overlap per neighbour. **(y4)** **A ONE-CHALLENGER THREAT SECTION IS WRITABLE, AND THE REGISTRY'S `notes` IS WHAT MAKES IT WRITABLE — (l), firing a second time.** The bench is one named party, the thinnest any landscape has faced. The note says the **adjacent role here was reserved and then filled with buyers**, so the nine adjacents split into **six suppliers who carry the real routes and three buyers who are the demand**, and the section is proportioned to that rather than padded. Two further facts carried the section: the single challenger is **the only one of the fifteen that belongs to no other segment**, and it **buys its interrupting elements from four of the five incumbents it is listed against** — so what it carries is a route (schedule) rather than a displacement. The module's own judgment is that **the threat is capacity entering the segment from outside it rather than share moving inside it**, and that criterion 3 is the sorting mechanism because every entering route is a domestic-manufacturing route. **(y5)** **`reviewBy` read-don't-sort produced session 3's (g) case a SECOND time — a sort that returns NOTHING AT ALL — and a new sub-field.** 41 `policyExposure[]` entries across fifteen members, 25 dated, **not one in the future**, and five members carrying no policy array at all; the generated segment lesson proves it independently by falling through to the generator's six-month default. The date taken, **2026-10-31**, was read out of `grid-united`'s own **`strategyRead[4]`** — a field no previous session has found a gate in — and **31 October rather than 1 October because a review that fires on the first of the month fires before the event it watches**, which also keeps the module outside its own 30-day horizon so the curriculum checker still reports 2 items due rather than 3. **Six** rejections in writing, including the bulk-power order's own `reviewBy` to the day, the shortage module's own review gate (**also rejected because the corpus states its date two different ways**), an incumbent's gas-turbine sell-out date (the right company on the wrong product line — session 7's truck-engine class in a new shape), and two on split grounds per (v). **(y6)** **(w) was satisfied on both halves, and the section half was DERIVED rather than guessed.** `--check` went **10 → 11 → 10**, sessions 1–4's shape because this segment was not in the standing due set — and the section half was read off the generator before the write: **`self.landscapes` is read at exactly one place in the code, inside `sec_read_next`**, so a landscape registration cannot touch any other section. Measured after the write: `read-next` alone, no pin moved, no deepening anywhere. **Row 12's lesson generalises — predict the section half from the mechanism, never from the prose.** **(y7)** **A brief's Scraper-seed prior was right in BOTH halves for the first time.** §7.31 predicted negative on the calendar vocabulary and positive on the machines. Against all **410** terms across both arrays, every word of criterion 1 is covered **five seeds over**, while the segment's **fifth product family** (the grid-stability machine, sold by four of its five incumbents) and the whole of **criterion 4** score zero. Nine terms seeded as `topic-landscape-grid-equipment`; four dropped as superstrings, four as too generic, one as a company name, and **three on split grounds** per (v).

- **What session 9 added (v06.03r).** **(z1)** **§10.6 (t) FIRED ON FOUR WHOLE SECTIONS OF TWO PUBLIC LESSONS, WHICH IS A DIFFERENT ORDER OF PRE-DECLARATION.** `bridge-and-on-site-generation` is **the first segment two of whose three `READ_NEXT` mechanism lessons are already built**, and between them `bridge-power` and `backup-generation` teach **five of its six buying criteria** — the four machine classes, the slot-reservation instrument, the standby/prime/continuous ladder, block load, and the whole permit ceiling from the hundred-hour emergency allowance to the synthetic minor — **naming not one vendor.** Every actor in those sections is *one supplier*, *one operator*, *three manufacturers*. Session 6's (t) fired on a **tile** that declined a ranking and session 8's (y2) on a **callout** that taught an instrument and named no holder; this is the mechanism curriculum reserving layer 4's whole job for layer 4 across four sections. Session 8's line needed no amendment — a market condition taught vendor-blind belongs in the lesson, one named vendor's own instrument belongs in the landscape — and §7.23's test held on **the slot itself**: a mechanism that explains why the escape routes exist there, a **balance-sheet position a buyer with no permit can own** here. **(z2)** **THE FINDING: A PUBLIC LESSON'S OWN CLASSIFICATION MAPPED ONE-FOR-ONE ONTO THE SEGMENT'S CHALLENGER BENCH.** `bridge-power` says in as many words that there are *exactly three ways out of a factory queue* and that every supplier archetype in it is one of them — change machine class, change where the machine comes from, build a machine nobody queues for — and names nobody. The registry's five challengers fill all three: `rolls-royce-power-systems` and `rehlko` on machine class, `proenergy` on provenance, `bloom-energy` and `mainspring-energy` on machines nobody queues for. **So session 6's (s) generalises**: from *the lesson teaches the test and the module applies it to named parties* to **the lesson teaches the CATEGORIES and the module FILLS them**. The threat section therefore does not re-derive the routes; it says who is on each, at what size, and which the record can measure. **(z3)** **SIX BUILT LANDSCAPE NEIGHBOURS IS A RECORD, AND THE INVERSION RATE IS BIMODAL — which is a finer instrument than session 8's direction.** Shared members and inversions: `in-hall-power` **6 / 6 (100 %)**, `storage-developers-and-ipps` 3 / 3, `storage-integrators-and-containers` 6 / 4, `utilities` 2 / 1, `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` 2 / 1, `grid-equipment` **3 / 0 (0 %)**. The two extremes sit either side of this segment in the chain and the reason is legible: `grid-equipment` shares the members whose centre of gravity is genuinely in **both** places (GE Vernova and Siemens Energy sell turbines *and* transformers from one book, so the registry is right twice; Eaton assembles in both), while `in-hall-power` shares the members whose machines **cross the fence line** (Caterpillar and Cummins are the standby fleet inside the hall and the prime fleet outside it; Piller and Eaton are the room's incumbents and this plant's accessories). **The rate measures whether a shared member's business CROSSES the boundary or STRADDLES it** — 0 % when it straddles, 100 % when it crosses — and it is available to every future landscape with two or more built neighbours. **Twenty-one omissions** across seven neighbours are enumerated in the header comment and in §2g of the analysis file. **(z4)** **`reviewBy` read-don't-sort produced session 3's (g) case a THIRD time, and the one future date in the fence is a TRAP rather than an absence.** 60 `policyExposure[]` entries across seventeen members, 24 dated, and **exactly one in the future** — `cummins`'s `2027-01`, whose own entry says it affects the **on-highway** platforms rather than the data-centre gen-sets. So unlike sessions 3 and 8, a mechanical sort here returns *something*, and what it returns would clock a bridge-generation module **on a truck engine**: session 7's truck trap in the one shape that would actually have fooled a sort. The date taken, **2026-12-31**, was read out of `caterpillar`'s own `strategyRead[5]` indicator list and **corroborated by four other members converging on end-2026** — the first time a landscape's gate has rested on a *convergence* rather than on one member's sentence. **(z5)** **THE FIRST DELIBERATE ACCEPTANCE OF A CALENDAR DAY ANOTHER MODULE ALREADY CARRIES, and the distinction is worth keeping.** `china-policy-stack-2026-08` also holds `reviewBy` 2026-12-31. (r) and (u) reject a candidate that is **already another module's own gate** — two modules ringing the *same bell*. This is a different bell on the same day, and the alternative (shifting to 30 December) would mean **fabricating a date the corpus does not state**, which §5.2's spirit forbids more strongly than a shared day offends (r). Recorded rather than quietly absorbed. **(z6)** **(w) was satisfied on both halves and the section half was derived rather than guessed, for the second time.** `--check` went **10 → 11 → 10** — sessions 1–4's shape, because this segment was not in the standing due set — and `read-next` alone before and after, no pin moved, no deepening. Row 13's deepening did not import, exactly as §7.33 predicted: it fired because a *lesson* id sat in both a segment's `READ_NEXT` and in `CRITERION_LEXICON`, which a landscape registration never does. **(z7)** **A BRIEF'S OPEN ITEM WAS DECIDED RATHER THAN DEFERRED A THIRD TIME, and the original ground did not survive inspection.** §7.32 (vi) recorded `section 232` scoring zero across all Scraper terms, left out by session 8 as *a tariff regime belonging with the policy seeds*. **No policy seed owns it**: `topic-china-policy` holds `tariff` and `Section 301` but is China-scoped, and 232 is a steel-and-aluminium-derivatives statute biting Germany, France and Japan — four members here carry that exposure and two name the regime in their own `policyExposure[]`. It stays out on a **sharpened** ground — it is a **cross-segment** regime and a landscape session may only add `topic-landscape-<segment>`, so seeding it here would mis-band every other segment's tariff article — with the recommendation recorded that it wants a policy-level home. **When a brief hands forward an undecided item, decide it and say why, even when the answer is the same.** The seed itself was a **half-and-half** (v): the machine and rating vocabulary is covered five seeds over, the packaging layer is `seg-sidecar-power`'s, and **four of the six buying criteria score zero across all 444 terms** — twelve terms seeded, four of them criterion 3.

- **What session 10 added (v06.07r).** **(aa1) THE SPLIT WAS DRAWN BY A LESSON THAT DOES NOT EXIST YET — a shape no previous landscape has met.** `contracts-and-revenue` (Phase 4 row 11, **public**, built, stamped on all three of this segment's incumbents) carries a section `clean-firm-and-the-restart` whose **opening sentence hands the physics off by name**: *"The physics — why a reactor follows load badly, what a small modular reactor changes, what clean firm admits — belongs to Clean Firm Power in the campus track, and is deliberately not repeated here."* **`clean-firm-power` is §7 row 16 and unbuilt.** So a built public lesson pre-declared a handoff **to a lesson that does not exist**, and the material it declined is held by nobody. Session 6's (s) generalises a second time — session 9's (z2) took it from *the lesson teaches the test* to *the lesson teaches the categories and the module fills them*; this adds a **third posture: say the test is unowned and decline to own it**, which (y3) demands and which is hardest to obey when the missing lesson is the segment's own subject. §7.23's test held on **the uprate attached to a supply agreement** — an *arithmetic worked example* of additionality there, naming nobody, and a *position marker* here that sorts the three incumbents into three answers to one objection. **Twenty-two omissions across eight neighbours** (four built landscapes, two guidance modules, two `READ_NEXT` lessons of which one is unbuilt) are enumerated in the header comment and in §2d of the analysis file. **(aa2) AN EMPTY `notes` IS ANSWERABLE BY MEASUREMENT, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS A NEW INSTRUMENT.** (l) and (y4) both need a registry note to explain a lopsided roster; this segment has none, so the 69 % adjacent share was read off the memberships instead: **eleven of the eleven adjacents are ranked — incumbent or challenger — in another segment, eleven out of eleven with no exception**, and **the two challengers are the segment's only pure plays**, the only two of sixteen belonging to no other segment. 19 shared memberships across seven neighbours and **14 inversions (74 %), the highest rate any landscape has measured** — but the rate is not the finding. Every genuinely on-subject gate is then either **month- or quarter-level only** or lands on **a day another module already holds**: the nearest licensing gate (a final safety evaluation targeted *November 2026*) is a **tell rather than a gate** by its own dossier's framing, and its month-end is `nvidia-800vdc-2026-08`'s 2026-11-30; the real gate (a construction permit decision guided to *Q1 2027*) has its quarter-end on `grid-equipment-shortage-2026-09`'s 2027-03-31. **So 2026-12-31 is the third module to carry that calendar day.** (z5) permits a shared day when the alternative is fabrication; **here the alternative was a *differently* shared day, which is not better** — recorded rather than absorbed, and the bell is different from both (a licensing filing deadline, not a tariff expiry and not a fuel-and-permit convergence). With §7.34's false `CRITERION_LEXICON` claim (corrected by row 14) this is now a pattern rather than an incident: **never take a measurement from a brief; open the file.** **(aa5) THE NETTING RULE (v) HELD FOR A FOURTH TIME AND WAS TRACED TO ITS CAUSE FOR THE FIRST TIME.** `--check` went **10 → 10 → 9**, `read-next` alone at every step, the section half **derived** from the generator before the write (`self.landscapes` is read at exactly one place, inside `sec_read_next`) and confirmed verbatim by the run (`changed: read-next; no pin moved`). **But the segment was already due for a reason nothing to do with this module**: `the-campus-as-a-power-project` was built *after* that segment's last regeneration, so `lesson_ref` had been rendering a **built** lesson as `(planned)`. Registering the landscape then added a second change to the same section, and the two netted. - **What the v06.20r revision added — (bb1) A PHASE 4 ROW THAT BUILDS A LESSON AN EXISTING LANDSCAPE DECLARED UNBUILT MUST REVISE THAT LANDSCAPE IN THE SAME COMMIT.** Session 10's `landscape-clean-firm-and-nuclear-2026-09` was written around (aa1) — a built public lesson pre-declaring a handoff to `clean-firm-power`, which was §7 row 16 and unbuilt — and **Phase 4 row 16 built that lesson the same EST day** (v06.13r). Row 16 **stated the inverted split correctly in its own record** (*the contributor landscape owns the parties, this public lesson owns the physics, and no company is ranked in it*) and still left the module's prose asserting the lesson did not exist. **The asymmetry is the argument for a rule rather than a habit**: the generated `segment-*` lesson caught the same event **mechanically** — `lesson_ref` stopped rendering `(planned)` and `--check` reported the segment due in *two* sections — while a hand-authored guidance module has **no detector at all**, no checker reads its prose for claims about what exists, and its `reviewBy` was **2026-12-31**, three and a half months out. Five sections were revised at v06.20r with one `revisions[]` entry naming exactly those ids; the split inverted to the **ordinary S2 shape** (two public lessons own the tests, the contributor module owns the parties), verified by measuring that the new lesson is eight sections, stamped on **eight of the segment's sixteen members**, and **names not one company**. **Five of the six buying criteria now have a built public owner against five having none**, and the module names the one that is still unowned (criterion 6, the sponsor's balance sheet) instead of keeping a blanket claim. **A corollary worth carrying: the session-10 third posture — *say the test is unowned and decline to own it* — was correct for exactly one day and stays on the record for the next landscape that meets an unbuilt `READ_NEXT` entry.**

One member's Gold is attested only in **another member's** dossier, not its own.
- **(cc2) THE FIRST INTERMEDIATE ROLE-INVERSION RATE, AND THE DECOMPOSITION IS FINER THAN (z3)'s.** Exactly **one** built landscape shares any member with this one (`aidc-developers-and-landlords`; the other ten share none) — four shared, **two inverted, 50 %**, against session 9's **(z3)** bimodal 0 % / 100 %. The accurate line is that the two holding the same role in both **sell campus capacity to others as a business**, while of the two that invert one **builds only for its own contracted load** and the other **owns almost nothing** — two different reasons for one label, neither of them size. **So the rate sorts on whether a campus position is a business SOLD or a book merely HELD**, which is a sharper cut than the segment's own third buying criterion along the same axis. (z3) is not violated; an intermediate rate is what it predicts when a segment holds both kinds of member.

In session 8 that was a real curriculum gap and in sessions 10 and 11 the mapped lessons appeared in no `CRITERION_LEXICON` entry. **Here every one of the five criteria DOES hit a lexicon key — and not one of the matching lessons is in this segment's `READ_NEXT`.** The mapped set and the keyword-matching set are **disjoint**: `READ_NEXT` names three campus-and-power lessons while the criteria are written in commercial vocabulary owned elsewhere (`how-a-storage-project-happens`, `what-bankable-means`, `the-china-policy-stack`, `reading-the-numbers`, `the-cooling-plant-and-water`/`heat-is-the-constraint`/`inside-the-rack`). **So a dash row can be a keyword artefact rather than a curriculum hole**, and one of the five hits is a false positive (`the-china-policy-stack`'s `ownership` is manufacturing ownership, not power ownership). **Scope is unchanged** — the module teaches none of the five — but the diagnosis a landscape publishes about its own dash row must now name which of the three mechanisms produced it.

Eight terms scored zero and were taken, two of them (`neocloud`, `merchant cloud`) **reserved for this seed by name** in session 11's own comment. **Two were dropped as near-duplicates an exact-match score does not catch** — `time to power` against session 11's `speed to power`, `contracted compute` against its `compute commitment` — which is the check **(i)** demands and the one that nearly failed here. **And a FIFTH drop reason: BLOCKED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** The segment's largest bear-case variable, GPU residual value and the useful-life assumption, **cannot be seeded at all**: every precise form is a superstring of an existing term and every short form mis-bands into battery degradation. Signature **P1, P2**; content checker **0/0 at 46 lessons**, module assertion **20 → 21**. **The micro-markup check found nothing for the second consecutive session and the screenshots caught FOUR defects instead** — the (cc2) over-reach, **literal backticks rendered in two quiz questions** (**(b)**, seventeen sessions on), and two arithmetic slips in the module's own prose (*six of seven rows* against five; *three members* against four).

- **What session 13 added (v06.24r).** **(dd1) THE FIRST SEGMENT WHOSE INCUMBENTS DO NOT SHARE A BASIS, AND THE GRAPH — NOT THE JUDGMENT — SETTLES WHICH OF THEM COMPETE.** `capital` is **8 members, 4 · 1 · 3**, the inverse of session 12's roster, and the registry's four `basis` lines name **four different instruments** (perpetual capital; operational integration; the finite-life fund; sovereign capital) rather than four sizes. Every previous plural incumbency could be sorted; this one cannot, and one member's own file says why — **no publisher ranks data-centre owners by capacity or value**, its own four scale figures *"measure four different things and do not crosswalk"*, and the two rankings that do exist disagree (eighth by five-year infrastructure fundraising in one, first by assets under management in another). So the module declines the ranking and **tests the rivalry against the graph instead**: 13 edges among the eight, 8 curated, **13 typings** — `competitor` **6**, `partner` 5, `other` 1, `portfolio` 1 — and the six competitor typings are **three pairs typed in both directions**, a complete triangle among the three asset managers. **The sovereign incumbent is typed `competitor` with nobody**, appearing only as a historical transaction counterparty and an active portfolio holder, with **no curated typing at all** to the other two incumbents; the single challenger likewise. The published judgment is therefore **three competitors and a counterparty, not four rivals** — and **two of the three competitor edges are the same transaction seen from both sides**, one firm buying the platform another was obliged to sell. **The rule to carry: when a roster's bases are instruments rather than magnitudes, count the typed edges before writing a contest.**
- **(dd2) THE FIRST CORPUS-INTERNAL CROSS-ATTESTATION: TWO MEMBERS STATING THE SAME CUT ABOUT EACH OTHER, INDEPENDENTLY, IN ALMOST THE SAME WORDS.** Blackstone's `strategyRead[0]`: *"Macquarie, running the closest comparable platform in Aligned out of finite-life infrastructure funds, exited at eight years. Same asset class, opposite structural obligation."* Macquarie's `strategyRead[0]`: *"a closed-end fund must eventually return capital, which is precisely the obligation Blackstone's perpetual vehicles do not carry over QTS. Same asset class, opposite structural clock."* **Neither file cites the other and both carry `lastUpdated` 2026-09-06.** Session 12's **(cc1)** found a shared basis supplied by a **third party**; this is two of the corpus's own analyses converging unprompted on one axis, which is stronger evidence for a cut than any module assertion. The axis is **the clock — when must this money leave** — and it is what `who-dominates-and-on-what-basis` sorts on. **Generalisable: before inventing an axis for an unrankable roster, grep the members' own strategy sections for a comparison they already make about each other.**
- **(dd3) THE ADJACENT BENCH RUNS THE OPPOSITE WAY FROM EVERY PREVIOUS LANDSCAPE'S, AND THE INVERSION RATE DECOMPOSES ON A NEW LINE.** **All four incumbents are pure plays** — none belongs to any other segment, the first time a segment's whole incumbency has been exclusive. Meanwhile **all three adjacents are ranked in exactly one other segment** (`aidc-developers-and-landlords`) and **all three invert** into `adjacent` here: **3 of 4 shared members inverted (75 %)**, bimodal per **(z3)** at **3/3 (100 %)** and **0/1 (0 %)**, with only **two** of the twelve built landscapes sharing any member at all. But the direction is what matters: these are **operators that learned to sell capital**, not investors that bought operations — a landlord running a USD 3.25 bn third-party fund on 20 % of the equity and keeping the mandate, a landlord that invented the data-centre securitization in 2018, and a broker-dealer that owns a campus. Session 10's **(aa2)** read a 100 %-ranked adjacent bench as *a product other industries make*; **here it is the same measurement with the arrow reversed — the adjacents are selling the incumbents' own instrument back into the segment**, so the threat is aimed at the **fee** rather than the asset. **And it is already priced**: the one that went furthest, issuing on its own credit with no sponsor behind an unrated tenant, paid **9.875 %** against **6.192 %** for a rated and enhanced comparable — about **375 bp** for having no balance sheet. **The non-inverting member is the challenger**, which holds `challenger` in both places because capital is its only business; so the rate sorts on whether capital is the member's **business** or its **second product**.
- **(dd4) FOUR MECHANISMS IN FIVE CRITERION ROWS, AND THE BRIEF'S OPEN QUESTION ANSWERED AGAINST IT.** Measured programmatically against the parsed `CRITERION_LEXICON` and `READ_NEXT`: criterion 1 maps to a lesson **in `READ_NEXT` and built**; criterion 2 to one **in `READ_NEXT` and unbuilt**; criteria 3 and 4 hit **no lexicon key at all**, so **(y3)**'s real curriculum gap; criterion 5 hits a **built** lesson **not** in this segment's `READ_NEXT`, so **(cc3)**'s disjoint-set artefact. **This is the first segment where the diagnosis differs row by row rather than segment-wide.** §7.41 asked whether *mapped but unbuilt* is a fourth mechanism: **it is not — it is a variant of the first**, because rows 1 and 2 pass the same `lid in eligible` guard and reach the same `lesson_ref()`, differing only at the registry lookup that appends `(planned)`. **A dash row is what the reader sees when the INTERSECTION fails; rows 1 and 2 are not dash rows at all.** Two further mechanics worth keeping: the row-2 **false positive** (the lexicon key `engine` matching *engineer* and mapping to `bridge-power`) **never reaches the page**, because the eligibility guard drops it — the guard working, against session 12's false positive that surfaced; and criterion 1's cell had been rendering a **built** lesson as `(planned)`, which is **(aa5)**'s cause for a **fourth** segment and why `capital` was already due.
- **(dd5) `reviewBy` PRODUCED AN EIGHTH FAILURE MODE — A TAUTOLOGY RATHER THAN A TRAP — AND (bb4) FIRED ON THE SAME `policyExposure` ENTRY THE GENERATOR USED.** 27 entries across eight members, 22 dated, **exactly one in the future**: a Section 232 tariff expiry at **2027-12-31**. It is rejected three ways — **later than the six-month default**; **off-subject** (a transformer-and-switchgear customs duty clocking a capital module, **(x4)**'s class, and **(z7)** already settled that Section 232 is cross-segment with no segment home); and **already this segment's own generated lesson's `reviewBy`**, set by the generator from **that exact entry**. Session 9's **(z4)** called the one future date a *trap*; here the mechanical route **cannot produce a second opinion at all**, because the sort and the generator read one field. Taken instead: **2026-11-06**, a half-year result whose financial year closed **before** the eight-year platform exit settled, so the gain lands in that period and no earlier — and **no return figure has ever been published** for the segment's defining transaction. Corroborated by the prior year's result falling on 2025-11-06 and by that dossier's own warning that *"an inferred quarterly date for this company would be wrong"*. **Six rejections in writing**, including **2026-10-14** — the strongest rival and the only one a dossier calls *"the first hard date"* — rejected on **subject** (the entities voting are the listed affiliates that dossier's own first judgment excludes as not its subject) and kept as an indicator row per **(u)**; and **2026-11-07**, a chief-executive handover **one day after the date taken**, which is the pair a naive scan gets wrong. The module ships **outside** its 30-day horizon, so the curriculum checker still reports **3** items due.
- **(dd6) THE SCRAPER CHECK WAS THE HIGHEST-YIELD S2 HAS RUN AND THE BRIEF'S PRIOR WAS WRONG ABOUT ITS MECHANISM — A FOURTH OUTCOME BEYOND (i), (e) AND (v).** §7.41 forecast that finance vocabulary *"will collide with the broadest terms already in the roster"* and predicted a **low yield**. Against all **462** distinct terms across both arrays per **(bb5)** (38 seeds / 238 terms plus 29 lenses / 249 terms; 454 + 8 reconciles exactly), **sixty candidates were run and fifty-seven scored ZERO.** There was **no collision to have**: the roster is an equipment, power, project, site and policy vocabulary containing **no finance seed at all**, and the only two exact matches were placed by S2 sessions covering *other* segments from the financing side. So the drops are almost all **editorial** — 21 too generic, 4 on split grounds (3 of them to `topic-bess-bankability`, which is **(v)** applied for the first time to a registered **module's** subject rather than a sibling landscape's), 3 near-duplicates per **(cc6)**, 2 duplicates, 1 superstring, 1 unlikely to appear in article text. **Ten terms taken.** **And a SECOND STANDING RESERVATION DEBT was identified**: session 12 dropped six terms *"on split grounds to `topic-aidc-landlords`"*, and **none of the six is in that seed while all six still score zero**, because an S2 session may only add `topic-landscape-<segment>` and that seed has not been edited since session 6. **A term ASSIGNED to another seed's territory is not a term SEEDED there** — the pattern `restart` established (now four sessions unseeded) is general, and two of the six bear directly on this segment's own criteria and were still left alone. **(dd7) A NAME COLLISION IS A NEIGHBOUR RELATION NO CHECKER CAN SEE.** `reading-the-graph`, the lane opener, names **not one** of the eight and does not mention this segment, so **(cc4)**'s instrument returned nothing — but the neighbour scan found `nvidia-800vdc-2026-08` carrying a **glossary entry** defining **MGX** as NVIDIA's modular rack form factor, while one of this segment's incumbents **is** MGX, the sovereign fund. Both modules sit at the contributor tier. The module therefore never uses `{{MGX}}` as a term and **names the collision once in its own prose**. **Check the neighbours for the member's NAME, not only for its subject.** **(aa5) held for a seventh time** and this is the **first S2 session to clear its own already-due segment**: `--check` **8 → 8 → 7**, all three numbers called in advance, `read-next` **and** `what-is-bought-and-on-what` clearing together. Signature **P1, P2**; content checker **0/0 at 47 lessons**, module assertion **21 → 22**. **The micro-markup check found nothing for the third consecutive session and the screenshots again caught what it cannot** — three defects: an **unverified corpus-wide superlative** (*"the widest any landscape has measured"*, about a ratio no prior landscape has measured) and **two arithmetic slips**, one of them a sentence shape carried over from the previous module without recounting (*five of eight rows* against six).

- **What session 14 added (v06.28r).** **(ee1) A SIXTEEN-FIRM RANKING WAS DECLINED AND THE ONE THE RECORD SUPPORTS WAS PUBLISHED WITH BOTH CONDITIONS THE CORPUS ATTACHES — the inverse of (dd1) rather than a repeat of it.** `capital`'s four incumbents named four instruments and **no** publishable ranking existed; here **sixteen** incumbents carry four *kinds* of claim (third-party ranks on named scales, GW/MW volumes, dollar volumes, and five role claims with no number) and exactly one ranking survives: the **ENR 2026 Top 400 Contractors** table, which ranks **13 of 19** — 12 of the 16 incumbents plus one challenger. **Six members are absent, including the #1 and #2 US electrical contractors** (#1 at 13.5bn of electrical revenue, **3.7× the runner-up**), so the table is a ranking of *one of the segment's two markets*. **Condition one — name the list — is stated inside the corpus by a member about its own rank**: *"ENR publishes several revenue bases across different lists, so 'ENR rank' is meaningless without naming the list"*, written after a secondary source called it the *15th largest domestic builder* in the year the Top 400 put it at 20. The roster proves it twice more: one member is **#1 in Power design eleven consecutive years and #35 as a contractor**, another **#1 Hydrogen / #1 O&M / #6 Power / #14 design / #49 contractor**, and **two different members both carry '#6' on two different lists**. **Condition two — publish the movement, not the level** — because the Top 400 grew **11.8 %** while its telecom/data-centre category grew **86.4 %**: one table, two populations at seven times different speeds, which is why a member **fell #4 → #5 while adding USD 1.4bn**. So (dd1) generalises: *a segment whose incumbents do not share a basis may still have one publishable ranking, and the module's job is to name its list, its coverage and its movement column rather than to decline outright.*
- **(ee2) THE GRAPH SPLIT THE SEGMENT INTO TWO MARKETS AND THE CUT IS A NODE AND AN EDGE, NOT AN INTERPRETATION.** **53 edges among the nineteen, 45 curated, 57 typings — `competitor` 39, `partner` 16, `investor` 1, `other` 1, and ZERO untyped curated pairs** — **35 competitor edges across 20.5 % of all 171 possible pairs**, against `capital`'s 13 edges and 6 competitor typings among eight. The competitor subgraph is **one connected component of 18**, so a first reading says one market; **cut one node and one edge and it falls into 6 + 10 + 1**.
