The **six-firm data-hall GC market** (`dpr`, `hitt`, `holder-construction`, `mccarthy`, `turner-construction`, `whiting-turner`) carries **12 of its 15 possible pairs** typed `competitor`; the **ten-firm power-and-energy EPC market** (`bechtel`, `black-veatch`, `burns-mcdonnell`, `kiewit`, `mastec`, `primoris`, `quanta-services`, `rosendin`, `solv-energy`, `strata-clean-energy`) carries **15 of 45**. The seams are **`mortenson`**, the only member of nineteen typed `competitor` on **both** sides (3 GC-side, 4 power-side) and whose own file says why — *"the only US contractor at scale holding both a hyperscale data-center building franchise and a fleet-scale power-EPC franchise"* — and the single edge **`mccarthy`↔`strata-clean-energy`**. `blattner` hangs off the second by **one** edge because its link to its own parent is typed **`investor`/`other`**, the graph getting a wholly-owned arm right. **The one publishable ranking runs ACROSS the boundary** — #1 and #5 in the data-hall market, #2/#4/#6 in the power market — **so the table compares firms the graph says do not compete.** And **`samsung-ct` has ZERO edges of any kind**, the only member with degree zero: a registered challenger the graph cannot see challenging anybody, which §4b states rather than sizes. **The new instrument: when a role-homogeneous roster's competitor subgraph has a cut vertex and one bridge edge, the segment is two markets and any single ranking of it is a category error.**
- **(ee3) THE BENCH INVERTS DOWNWARD 6 OF 6 — (aa2)'s MIRROR, AND A HIGHER RATE THAN ANY LANDSCAPE HAS MEASURED.** **8 of 19 shared, 11 memberships across 6 neighbours (5 with a built landscape), 10 inversions — 91 %**, past (aa2)'s 74 % — and **9 downward, 1 upward, 1 equal**. The rate is not the finding: **every one of the six shared incumbents is `adjacent` in every other segment it appears in, 6 of 6 with no exception** (`bechtel`, `black-veatch`, `burns-mcdonnell`, `kiewit`, `quanta-services`, `rosendin`). (aa2) reads an all-ranked *adjacent* bench as evidence the segment is a **product** other industries make; this is its mirror: **when a segment's INCUMBENTS are demoted to `adjacent` everywhere else, the segment is the SERVICES LAYER the rest of the chain borrows a builder from.** The lone upward inversion confirms rather than weakens it — `strata-clean-energy` is a **developer** with a self-perform EPC arm, ranked where it develops (`challenger` in `storage-developers-and-ipps`) and demoted where it builds (`adjacent` here) — so **the direction tracks where the balance sheet sits, not where the crews are.** Available to any landscape with two or more built neighbours, and it pairs with (z3)'s crosses-versus-straddles rate as a second axis.
- **(ee4) A FOURTH MECHANISM FOR A DASH ROW, AND (dd4)'s ROW-BY-ROW DIAGNOSIS EXTENDED RATHER THAN REPEATED.** Measured programmatically against the parsed `CRITERION_LEXICON` and `READ_NEXT`: **three of six criteria resolve, three dash, and the three dashes have three different causes.** Criterion 6 (`TRIR`, repeat-relationship share) hits **no lexicon key at all** and is **(y3)**'s real gap in its most complete form — across all 48 registered lessons `TRIR`, `recordable`, `incident rate`, `experience modification` and `surety` each appear **zero** times, so nothing in the curriculum teaches contractor safety metrics. Criterion 5's `open-book` half is **(cc3)** exactly: the only lesson carrying the term is `who-buys-storage`, outside this segment's `READ_NEXT`. **But criterion 4's material IS taught, by a lesson that IS in this segment's `READ_NEXT`, and that lesson IS built** — `how-a-storage-project-happens` teaches `percentage-of-completion` as a `{{term}}` and the **performance bond** past the damages cap, and across the corpus `percentage-of-completion` appears in **four** lessons and `performance bond` in exactly **one**, both sets including it. The row still dashes because `CRITERION_LEXICON`'s entry for that lesson is `("epc","schedule","construction","commissioning","labor","labour","craft")` and criterion 4 contains none of the seven. **The intersection did not fail; the keyword table did** — a *lexicon coverage failure*, the first dash row in fourteen landscapes whose cause sits in **neither** the curriculum nor the segment. (dd4) settled that *mapped-but-unbuilt* is a variant of the first mechanism because both pass the same guard; this one **passes the guard's eligibility test and never reaches the keyword loop**, so it is genuinely fourth. **It is also the only one of the four that a generator edit could fix**, and it is recorded for a developer decision rather than taken.
- **(ee5) `reviewBy` TOOK THE SIX-MONTH DEFAULT FOR THE FIRST TIME IN FOURTEEN LANDSCAPES, AND THE SORT RETURNED AN EMPTY SET RATHER THAN A DISTANT ONE.** All thirteen previous landscapes found a read date; this one cannot, and the fence says why: **21 `policyExposure` entries across 19 members — 1.1 per member, the thinnest any landscape has met, with 10 members carrying none at all — 6 dated and ZERO in the future**, on a status distribution of **20 `in-effect` against 1 `announced`**. **A regime already in force has no future date to ring**, which is the structural difference between construction regimes and equipment ones and the fifth firing of session 3's **(g)**. It is the **empty-set** version against row 18's **distant-set** version, where four genuine future dates were all at least twenty-seven months out and the default was taken *despite* a non-empty result. The whole nineteen-dossier corpus holds **four** future day-level dates and all four are rejected in writing: **2026-09-21** (a securities lead-plaintiff deadline — off-subject, and it would put the module inside its own 30-day horizon and take the checker's due count to 4), **2026-11-02** (an earnings date the dossier itself calls *estimated* and *not company-scheduled*, with two sources a week apart — fabricating precision), **2027-12-31** (the OBBBA placed-in-service cliff — genuinely on-subject for the renewables half, but **later than the default** and already twice a `reviewBy`, so kept as **indicators row 6** per **(u)**) and **2028-04-30** (a buyback authorisation expiry). **Six further month- or window-level candidates rejected**, including **the next ENR Top 400 edition — the one thing that genuinely clocks this module's central claim, published annually on no announced day.** The judgment recorded: **a module whose central claim is a market BOUNDARY is not clocked by a policy effective date at all**, and inventing a day for an annual table would be worse than defaulting.
- **(ee6) THE SCRAPER ZERO RATE WAS ESSENTIALLY TOTAL, AND (dd6)'s MECHANISM REPEATED ONE LAYER DOWN.** Denominator re-measured against both arrays per **(bb5)**: **39 seeds / 248 terms and 29 lenses / 249 terms — 497 raw, 472 distinct**, reconciling with session 13's 462 plus its ten. **Forty-nine candidates scored; forty-five zero.** The four non-zero are not construction terms — `interconnection queue` (exact duplicate), `substation EPC` and `transmission EPC` (superstrings of held terms), `EPC` (the held `epc contract` is a superstring). (dd6) explained its own high zero rate by the absence of a **finance** seed; this is the same mechanism one layer down — **the roster has never held a CONSTRUCTION seed at all** — so every drop is **editorial** rather than duplication-driven. **Criterion 1 is covered by the segment one link up** (`days to energize` is the registry's wording, not the market's — session 13's ground for dropping `fund-to-fund` — and its market form `speed to power` is already seeded in `topic-landscape-hyperscalers-and-ai-labs`), which is **(v)**'s half-and-half. **Criterion 3 is BLOCKED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS for the third time**: the precise forms (`Level 5 commissioning`, `commissioning agent`) score zero and carry almost no article volume, the broad form `commissioning` also scores zero but is used of ships, plants, artworks and public buildings, so **no admissible term exists** and the criterion is recorded unseeded rather than served badly. Ten terms taken, audited for exact, substring and near-duplicate collision. **Both standing reservation debts re-checked and both still open**: `restart` scores zero across all 472 for a **fifth** session, and session 12's six terms *assigned* to `topic-aidc-landlords` are still in no seed and still score zero — **a second consecutive session confirming that a term assigned to another seed's territory is not a term seeded there.**
- **(ee7) THE SCREENSHOTS CAUGHT TWO DEFECTS FOR A SIXTH CONSECUTIVE SESSION, AND BOTH ARE THE RECOUNT CLASS RATHER THAN ROW 18's GLYPH CLASS.** Every checker passed 0/0 before either was found. **(i)** `the-indicators`' intro and its `sales` line both asserted *two rows carry a day* beside a table where **three** do — row 1 prints 2026-05-22, row 6 prints 2027-12-31 **and** 2026-07-04, row 10 prints 2026-04-30 — so the honest statement is **three rows, four days, exactly one still ahead**, and both sentences were rewritten. **(ii)** `what-the-record-does-not-say` opened *eleven of the nineteen are private and six of those publish no revenue, no backlog and no margin at all*; re-counting the `ownership` field gives **12 private, 5 public, 2 subsidiary**, and the triple-disclosure claim is not supportable — what is measurable is that **seven members' files carry an explicit *publishes no …* statement and four name backlog specifically.** Row 18's own countermeasure worked on the glyph class: **all four `tiles[].k` values were authored in pure ASCII** and the rendered DOM contained only `U+2014`, `U+00B7` and one `U+2190` belonging to the page's own back button. **The standing instruction sharpens: read the screenshots for glyphs, and RECOUNT every number the prose asserts about a table it sits beside — this session's two defects were both in that second class, and neither is visible in the JSON.** Render: **9/9 sections at contributor, 63,051 characters, zero page errors**, and the re-render after the fixes moved the count **62,894 → 63,051**, which is the stale-process evidence row 18 established. Through the real serving path: **analyst → `ROLE_DENIED`** (and 0/9 sections with *"Industry Guidance is available to the admin and contributor tiers"* at the page level), **viewer → `ROLE_DENIED`**, **token < 32 chars → `SESSION_EXPIRED`**, **admin and contributor → the full nine-section module.**

- **What Phase 4 row 20 added (v06.34r)** — the third non-S2 contribution to this list, and it is here for the same reason as row 19's: every finding is about the instruments §10.6 defines rather than about a landscape. **The (gg…) label is spent; the next session continues at (ii1).** **(hh1) THE FIRST GATED LESSON IN THE CORPUS, AND THE GATE TURNED THE ACCEPTANCE TEST INSIDE OUT.** Forty-nine hand-authored lessons existed and every one folded to `tracks`, so every previous row's render was a *content* test: did the eight sections say the right things. This one carries `guidance:nvidia-800vdc-2026-08` in its stamp, `clGateForProvenance_()` returns `guidance`, and the test that matters becomes the **denial**. It was measured rather than asserted, base against head, through the same `handleClassroomOp_` the page calls: an analyst's `aidc-grid-to-chip` returned **9 cards and `withheld` 0** at base and **9 cards and `withheld` 1** at head, `cop=lesson` answers `ROLE_DENIED`, the page renders **0 of 8 section nodes**, and the analyst index stays at **47 of 50** with **the lesson's title never entering the analyst DOM**. **The rule to carry: on a gated lesson the render proves the content for the tiers that may read it and the gate for the tier that may not, and only the second one is new evidence.** §8 item 10's standing acceptance check — *"never sees `dc-fault-engineering`"* — was written at S0 against a lesson that did not exist, and this is the first run that could execute it.
- **(hh2) A DECLARED HANDOFF CAN BE HONOURED IN SUBSTANCE AND FALSE IN ITS STATUS CLAUSE ON THE SAME DAY — (ff1)'s SECOND SHAPE, AND IT FIRES ON THE LANDING RATHER THAN ON THE DRIFT.** Row 19's (ff1) found a declaration pointing at a sibling that carried none of the material: stale by drift. Here both declarations pointed correctly and **both were satisfied word for word** — `the-800-vdc-shift` enumerates **five** items and all five are carried one per section, `where-the-chain-breaks` reserves the arc taxonomy and it is rows 2–5 of `ac-versus-dc-faults` with *detected* and *cleared* as whole columns. **What went wrong is the other half of the sentence.** Both of `where-the-chain-breaks`'s declarations added a *status* clause — *"that lesson is not yet built"*, *"the classification is currently held by nobody"* — and **those clauses became false at the instant the reserving lesson landed**. A handoff that names a destination stays true; a handoff that reports the destination's **state** expires the moment the state changes, and nothing in the toolchain can see it. **The rule to carry: write a handoff as a pointer, not as a status report — and when a row closes a reservation, grep the corpus for the reservation's own words and correct every status clause in the same commit.** Done here under **one `revisions[]` entry naming both section ids**, which is exactly the shape P8 asserts, so the correction cost no finding.
- **(hh3) THE REVERSE (t) CHECK RETURNED EMPTY, AND AN EMPTY RESULT IS A MEASUREMENT WORTH RECORDING — plus the collision it found became teaching material.** S2 session 15's **(gg3)** added a fourth (t) shape, the *silent* neighbour, and made checking for it mandatory. All forty-nine existing lessons were grepped for `HRMG`, `HRRG`, interlock, `SSCB`, insulation monitoring, `IMD`, `RCM`, tap can, protection zone, ungrounded and high-resistance: **nobody teaches any of the four grounding schemes, either monitoring device, the two protection zones, the connector interlock or the four deployment options**, so the fourth shape does not recur. Two adjacent mentions were read and dismissed on inspection rather than on the grep: `the-ups-room` names `SSCB`s as a *consequence* of moving the store into the rack, giving no device comparison and no clearing time, and `where-the-chain-breaks` names them in the same declining sentence that reserved the taxonomy. **What the grep did find is the finding: `interlock` already carries four other meanings in this curriculum** — a zone-selective wire between two breakers, a fire-alarm interlock, a grid-interactive UPS reserve floor, and a relay setting proved at a factory acceptance test — **and none of them is a connector interlock**. That became a paragraph of the lesson. **The rule to carry: a reverse (t) grep looks for pre-emption, but the same sweep is the cheapest vocabulary-collision detector the corpus has, and a collision a seller could trip on is worth more than the null result that found it.**
- **(hh4) A SOURCE'S TABLE WAS TURNED ONTO A DIFFERENT SPINE TO ANSWER THE QUESTION THE HANDOFF ASKED, AND THE DECISION WAS WRITTEN WHERE A READER CAN CHECK IT.** The module's Table 1 runs on a **topic** spine — fault current, fault energy, shock, protection devices, ground-fault detection, arcing — and answers *how does DC differ from AC*. The inherited handoff asked a different question: *what are the arc types, and how is each one detected and cleared*. A topic spine cannot answer that, because detection and clearing are properties of a **fault**, not of a topic. So the table was rebuilt on a fault spine — bolted short, parallel arc, series arc, first earth fault, second earth fault, shock — with *what sees it* and *what stops it* as columns, and **every topic of the original survives**: two as rows, two as the new columns, two in row one and the intro. **The rule to carry: when a lesson inherits a question its source's own table cannot answer, transposing the table is a legitimate authoring move — provided the `note` states the transposition, names where each original topic went, and does not claim the source's row labels as its own.** This row's first screenshot defect was exactly that over-claim, made and caught in the same session.
- **(hh5) `reviewBy` WAS INHERITED RATHER THAN READ — a first for Phase 4 — AND THE SORT THAT WOULD HAVE OVERRIDDEN IT RETURNED AN EMPTY SET.** Every Phase 4 row before this one either took the ~6-month default or read its own material for a dated gate; §3 fixes this row's clock in advance at `nvidia-800vdc-2026-08`'s **2026-11-30**, so the work was to *confirm* rather than to sort. The module was re-read on the day and still carries it. The sort ran anyway, because §8 item 3 says a nearer dated gate in the lesson's own material wins: across all six inputs **the only day-level date of any kind is the module's own `revisions[].date` of 2026-08-29**, which is past and housekeeping. Two rejections in writing — that date, and the quarter-level execution calendar (Q3 2026, Q3 2027, toward 2029), which is a genuine set of forward gates and gives no day. **Sharing a module's date is not the (r)/(u) violation it would be anywhere else**, because §3 prescribes the inheritance: a lesson built out of one module's field notes *is* as fresh as that module and no fresher. **The rule to carry: an inherited `reviewBy` is confirmed on the day and the confirmation is written down, and the nearest-gate sort is still run — an inheritance the spec fixes is a floor, not an exemption.**
- **(hh6) §10.6 (ff5) NOW HAS A SECOND LESSON ON ITS PERMISSIVE SIDE, AND THE ASYMMETRY WAS DELIBERATELY NOT "FIXED".** The track view withholds a gated lesson's **title** from an analyst — proved above — while the generator's `read-next` publishes that same title inside `segment-power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon`, which is **public**. Registering this lesson put a second title on the permissive side of that gap. Neither surface was touched: the generated literal is the generator's and a Phase 4 row regenerates nothing, and the track view's behaviour is the gate working as designed. **The rule to carry is (ff5)'s own, now with two instances rather than one: the fold protects a lesson's *body* everywhere and its *title* only where the gate is consulted, and the generator does not consult it. Whether that is a defect or an accepted cost is the developer's call, and it is now a call about a pattern rather than about one lesson.**
- **(hh7) THE SCREENSHOTS CAUGHT TWO DEFECTS FOR A NINTH CONSECUTIVE SESSION, AND BOTH ARE (ee7)/(ff6) CLASSES THAT NO CHECKER CAN REACH.** Every checker had returned 0/0 first. **(i)** The fault table's `note` asserted *"the row labels are the source's"* — false, because the source's labels are topics and these are fault modes; a provenance claim contradicted by the table it sits under. **(ii)** The closing callout said *"because it is the section above's whole point"* about the grounding scheme, when the section **above** it is the clearing-time bars and grounding is three sections earlier — a positional promise the adjacent content refutes. The same `note` also carried an unverifiable promise (*"every topic in the original is still here"*), rewritten as an enumeration the reader can check row by row. **Two countermeasures held and are worth keeping.** The micro-markup simulation ran **before** the splice over both plain-field families this row puts in play — `proscons` cards' `t`/`meta` **and** a `bars` item's `label`/`sub`, which no recent lesson had together — and came back **0/0 with every one of the ten local glossary entries used**, so session 15's unused-entry class did not recur. And **the literal was written 100 % ASCII**, which retires row 18's glyph-that-lies class by construction rather than by vigilance: the rendered DOM's only non-ASCII characters are the page's own `·`, `—` and back-arrow. **The rule to carry: ASCII-only content is a cheaper defence than reading every glyph, and it costs nothing a lesson needs.** Render at contributor **25,556 characters, 8 of 8 sections, zero page errors**; the PROJECT region moved **3,459,716 → 3,523,030 bytes** and both meters moved again across the fix round.
- **What session 15 added (v06.32r).** **(gg1) A BRIEF'S CENTRAL HYPOTHESIS WAS TESTED AGAINST THE GRAPH AND REJECTED — the first time an S2 session has published the refutation as its opening section.** §7.45 proposed that `cooling` is *"two markets wearing one name"* and asked that the reading be tested before publishing. It was, and it fails: **26 edges among the eleven, 23 carrying a curated typing, 36 of 39 typings reading `competitor`, 21 distinct rival pairs of 55 possible (38 %)** — and the competitor subgraph is **ONE connected component of ten**, whose only cut vertex (`coolit`) detaches a single degree-1 leaf (`supermicro`). Restricting to edges whose own text mentions thermal content still leaves **one component of nine**. Session 14's **(ee2)** showed what a two-market roster looks like in the same instrument — cut one node and one edge and it fell into **6 + 10 + 1**; nothing here cuts. **The rule to carry: a brief's structural hypothesis is a hypothesis, and the graph is the instrument that settles it either way. Publishing the refutation is better guidance than quietly publishing something else.**
- **(gg2) WHAT REPLACED IT IS STRONGER, AND (dd2)'s CROSS-ATTESTATION FIRED ON A RANKING FOR THE FIRST TIME.** Two independent rankings of this exact segment run in **opposite directions at both ends**: ABI Research (30 January 2025, thermal-management providers) places **Vertiv 1st** and **Trane 5th**; Global Market Insights (August 2026, data-centre chillers, a USD 2.6bn 2025 market) places **Trane 2nd at 18.7 %** and **Vertiv 5th at 8.1 %**. Both members' dossiers give the same reason **independently, in almost the same words, neither citing the other** — *"because the two companies are strong at different points in the same chain."* Session 13's **(dd2)** found two files converging unprompted on a structural **clock**; this is the same instrument returning a **ranking axis**, which is stronger evidence for a cut than any module assertion. **The published judgment is therefore that the segment is ONE CHAIN MEASURED AT TWO POINTS**, and it satisfies session 14's **(ee1)** name-the-list condition in a new shape: (ee1)'s problem was one publisher running several lists, this one is two publishers measuring different **places**.
- **(gg3) §10.6 (t) FIRES IN A FOURTH SHAPE — THE NEIGHBOUR IS SILENT, AND IT IS SILENT WHILE STANDING ON THE SEGMENT'S OWN MEMBERS.** Session 6's (t) was a pre-declared handoff honoured; row 18's was an undeclared pre-emption; row 19's **(ff1)** was a declared handoff naming the wrong sibling. **All three presuppose that the neighbour said something or took something.** Measured here across both `READ_NEXT` literals: `heat-is-the-constraint` and `the-cooling-plant-and-water` are both public, between them own **all six** of the segment's buying criteria, and name **0 of 11** members in prose. The six apparent matches are **every one a `provenance.inputs[].ref`** — a stamp, not a sentence — and `the-cooling-plant-and-water` is **stamped on five of this segment's own eleven members** while naming none of them. So the boundary is **enacted rather than declared**, and it is visible only as a **measurement of what was not written**: the check has to be a name count, not a search for a handoff sentence.
- **(gg4) (aa2) AND (ee3) BOTH FIRE, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS, ON ONE ROSTER — which no landscape has met — AND THE INVERSION SORTS PERFECTLY BY ROLE.** 9 of 11 shared, **22 memberships elsewhere, 17 inversions (77 %)**. By the role held here: **incumbents (4 members, 6 memberships) 0 up / 4 down / 2 equal; the challenger (1, 5) 1 up / 4 down; adjacents (6, 11) 8 up / 0 DOWN / 3 equal.** So **all nine promotions elsewhere belong to members this segment does not rank at the top, and all eight demotions belong to members it does.** (aa2) reads an all-ranked adjacent bench as *a product other industries make* and fires at **6 of 6**; (ee3) reads demoted incumbents as *the services layer the chain borrows from* and fires on four of six. They are not in conflict — they are two halves of one statement that **(gg2)** explains: a segment whose own rank depends on the measuring point will invert against every neighbour, because a neighbour measures somewhere else by definition. **The two members that cannot invert are the proof**: `trane-technologies` and `coolit` belong to no other segment, are the only two pure plays, sit at opposite ends of the loop, and **the graph carries no edge between them of any kind**.
- **(gg5) A NUMERIC LADDER THAT LOOKS LIKE A RANKING AND IS NOT, AND THE DEFEATER IS A UNIT MISMATCH RATHER THAN A COVERAGE GAP.** Eight of the eleven publish a CDU capacity, from 1.8 MW to 14 MW. It is not a rank order for two reasons: **the top rung is an assembly of 2.5 MW blocks validated to fourteen while the rest are single units**, and the ladder **contradicts all three independent assessments at once**, placing an `adjacent` member (Delta, 3 MW) above three of the four incumbents — a member none of the three publishers names. (ee1) established that a roster may still have one publishable ranking; this is the complementary case, **a number available for most of the roster that must be published with its defeater attached**. The honest use is as evidence of **convergence** on one machine.
- **(gg6) `reviewBy` — (bb4) AND (dd5) FIRED TOGETHER AND FORCED THE EARLIEST CLOCK ANY MODULE CARRIES.** 34 `policyExposure` entries across eleven members, 17 dated, **exactly one in the future**: 2026-11-09, a Section 301 exclusion expiry that is **genuinely on-subject** (the expiring exclusions name rotary compressors within defined wattage ranges). Unusual — **(x4)**, **(z4)** and **(aa3)** all met a sort whose one answer was off-subject. It is rejected anyway on **(bb4)**'s ground: it **is already `segment-cooling`'s own generated `reviewBy`**, set by the generator from that exact field. That is **(dd5)**'s tautology in a **new sub-shape** — (dd5)'s date was off-subject *and* the generator's, so two grounds agreed; here the date is **on-subject and still unusable**, so reading is the only way out. Reading returns **2026-09-28**, a next-generation CDU launch stated **seven separate times in one dossier**, the seventh inside a field headed **INDICATORS TO WATCH** that names the test the module's own ladder rests on. **Nine rejections in writing.** Taken deliberately **inside** the 30-day horizon, so the curriculum checker reports **4** items due rather than 3 — **(k)** for a fourth time, by design, and the earliest `reviewBy` in the corpus at eleven days.
- **(gg7) THE FIRST SEGMENT WHOSE CRITERION TABLE PRINTS NO DASH AT ALL, AND THE SUPERLATIVE WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** Run the generator's own `READ_NEXT` ∩ `CRITERION_LEXICON` intersection across **all nineteen** segments: `cooling` resolves **6 of 6** and is the **only** one with zero dash rows; the next best is `cells-and-chemistry` at five of six, and three segments resolve none. Row 18 cleared the last of them the day before. Against session 8's **(y3)** two-of-six unowned, session 10's five and session 11's **(bb2)** six-of-six, this is the opposite limit — **the module owns none of the six tests and only says who sits where on each**, which is the sharpest form of layer 4's job the curriculum has been able to support. **(dd7)**'s name-collision scan and **(cc4)**'s lane-opener instrument both returned **nothing** and are reported as disabled per **(bb1)**.
- **(gg8) THE BETS TABLE CARRIED ELEVEN ROWS RATHER THAN THE FIVE §10.6 SPECIFIES, and the ground is a measurement.** §10.6 says one row per incumbent and challenger. On a roster where **six of eleven are somebody else's incumbent and every one of them is promoted elsewhere**, a five-row table omits the module's own finding. Session 4's **(m)** established that section proportions follow the segment; this is that rule **adding** rows rather than removing them, and it is recorded so a later session does not read it as drift. The rows run incumbent → challenger → adjacent with a middle column naming **which end of the chain each member arrived from**.
- **(gg9) THE SCRAPER GAP WAS THE SEGMENT'S ARITHMETIC, NOT A RESIDUE, AND ONE HALF-SEEDED METRIC PAIR IS THE SHARPEST INSTANCE YET OF WHY (i) FORBIDS THE LABEL-LEVEL GLANCE.** A `seg-cooling` lens already exists — so the label says covered — and its **eight terms are all equipment nouns**. Scored against all **482** distinct terms across both arrays per **(bb5)** (40 seeds / 258 terms plus 29 lenses / 249 terms; 472 + 10 reconciles exactly), what scores zero is **four of the six buying criteria**: the temperature contract, the water ratio, the part-load curve and the refrigerant calendar. **The segment's own pair of ratios is half-seeded — `pue` is an exact held term and `WUE` scores zero** — so the digest bands the efficiency half of this segment and not the water half, which is the half the registry's own definition leads with. **Twelve terms taken**; a **new drop class recorded — ROSTER CONSISTENCY**: `water usage effectiveness` was dropped because the paired metric is seeded as the bare acronym with no expansion, and seeding both forms here would double-count against a convention the roster already set. **The seven-seed shortfall is confirmed and is the developer's**: seven `topic-landscape-*` seeds against fourteen built landscapes before this session, counted as **quoted `key:` literals** because a loose grep returns thirteen. **Both standing reservation debts re-checked and both still open** — `restart` scores zero for a **seventh** session, and session 12's six terms assigned to `topic-aidc-landlords` are still in no seed for a **third**. **(aa4) caught itself here**: a first pass checked session **6**'s seven landlord terms by mistake, found all seven held, and nearly recorded a false state change.
- **(gg10) THE SCREENSHOTS CAUGHT FOUR DEFECTS FOR AN EIGHTH CONSECUTIVE SESSION, AND ALL FOUR ARE THE CLASSES (ee7) AND (ff6) NAMED.** Every checker passed 0/0 before any was found. **(i)** *"Eleven firms … are now all rated in megawatts of the same machine"* beside a ladder in which **eight** publish a rating and three sell no merchant unit — the recount class. **(ii)** The bets table's intro promised rows **grouped by which end of the chain the member arrived from** while the rows ran in **role** order with the arrival end in a column — **(ff6)**'s promise-versus-content class, on the table's own structure rather than on a count. **(iii)** *"All eleven of these firms sell thermal management for a living"* contradicted the same module's statement two sections earlier that one member's platform is *"never sold to anybody"*. **(iv)** *"Two **incumbents** carry no policy exposure entry"* where one of the two is the **challenger** — a role error against the module's own roster table. The countermeasures held: **all four `tiles[].k` in pure ASCII**, and the rendered DOM carried only `U+2014`, `U+00B7` and the page's own back-arrow. Render at **contributor: 9 of 9 sections, 50,330 characters, zero page errors**, and the count moved **49,973 → 50,330** across the fix round, with the vm's PROJECT region moving **3,459,343 → 3,459,716** — row 18's stale-process evidence on both meters. Through the real serving path: **analyst → `ROLE_DENIED` on both `gop=index` and `gop=doc`, and 0 of 9 sections with the page-level denial**; token < 32 characters → `SESSION_EXPIRED`; contributor → the full nine-section module. **(aa5) held for an eighth time and all three numbers were called before the run: `--check` 7 → 7 → 6**, `cooling` clearing `read-next` **and** `what-is-bought-and-on-what` together on its own regeneration. Signature **P1, P2**; content checker **0/0 at 49 lessons**, module assertion **23 → 24**.

- **What Phase 4 row 19 added (v06.30r)** — the second non-S2 contribution to this list, after the v06.20r revision, and it is here because all six findings are about the instruments §10.6 defines rather than about a landscape. **The (ff…) label is spent; S2 session 15 continues at (gg1).** **(ff1) §10.6 (t) FIRED THREE TIMES INSIDE ONE TRACK AND ONE FIRING IS A THIRD CLASS: A DECLARED HANDOFF NAMING THE WRONG SIBLING.** Session 6's (t) was a pre-declared handoff honoured; row 18's was an undeclared pre-emption discovered by reading. This is neither: `where-batteries-stop` **declares the boundary in as many words** — *"the formats, the manufacturing, the yield"* — and hands them to *From Cell to Container*, **which teaches none of the three** (`prismatic`, `pouch`, `slurry`, `calender` and `yield` all appear **zero** times in it). The declaration was written at row 12, before row 19 was scheduled beneath it, so it was stale the moment this lesson landed and wrong even before that. **A declared handoff is therefore not self-maintaining**, and a session inheriting one must check that the named neighbour actually carries the material rather than trusting the sentence. **(bb1) is extended from modules to lessons in the same breath**: its argument was that hand-authored prose has no detector, and a *lesson's* prose has exactly as little. The correction is in the same commit, in place, under the same section id, with one `revisions[]` entry naming that one section and no pin moved.
- **(ff2) A FOURTH OUTCOME FOR THE THREE-PART DEEPENING TEST: FAILURE AT PART ONE, MEASURED.** (w) established the test, (aa5) established that it is structurally irrelevant to a landscape, and (ee4) found a *lexicon coverage* failure — a criterion whose material a mapped lesson teaches and whose keywords the table misses. This row is the case where **the lesson is not in `CRITERION_LEXICON` at all**: all 27 entries were parsed and none names `how-a-cell-is-made`, so part one fails, part three never runs, and the `what-is-bought-and-on-what` half of the deepening **cannot** fire whatever the segment looks like. The distinction matters for forecasting: (ee4)'s gap is a *generator* gap a lexicon edit would close, and this one is a *deliberate absence* — a lesson about how cells are made answers none of `cells-and-chemistry`'s six buying criteria, and should not. **So a Phase 4 row's due-count forecast is derivable in full**: run the lexicon test before writing, and when part one fails the only possible change is `read-next`.
- **(ff3) `reviewBy`'s EMPTY-SET SHAPE REACHES A LESSON, AND THE BOUNDARY OF "ITS OWN MATERIAL" IS WHAT DECIDES IT.** (ee5) met the empty set across a landscape's nineteen `policyExposure[]` arrays. Here the nine stamped study guides carry **not one day-level date of any kind**, future or past — the restricted-entity material is a *status test*, which has no day by construction. The instructive part is the rejection: a genuine future day-level date (**2027-10-01**, the NDAA §154 procurement ban) exists in **three of the members' dossiers** and was rejected on three independent grounds, the first of which is the general one — **a lesson's `reviewBy` is the nearest gate in the material the lesson is STAMPED on, not in the corpus behind it.** Reaching into an unstamped dossier for a date would pin the lesson to a document it never read, which is the same defect G2 guards against from the other end.
- **(ff4) THE DRILL'S QUIZ ANSWER INDEX IS NOT RANDOM, AND IT IS MEASURABLE CORPUS-WIDE — an open item for the developer, recorded rather than swept.** Parsed across all 49 registered lessons **as this row ships**: **245 quiz items, 162 of them (66 %) with the correct answer at index 0**, and **27 of 49 lessons all-zero** — figures that already include this row's own correction. Measured across the **48** lessons that existed before it, the shipped corpus stood at **160 of 240 (67 %)** at index 0 with **27 of 48** lessons all-zero, so every one of the 27 is pre-existing. `Classroom.html` does **not** shuffle choices, so the queue serves them in authored order. A reader who notices can score without reading the question, which is a retention defect rather than a cosmetic one. This row's own five items were authored at indices **0, 2, 1, 3, 0** after the screenshot pass surfaced the pattern; **no other lesson was touched**, because re-ordering a shipped item's choices changes its content hash and re-introduces it to every account that has already learned it (`CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md`, "The content hash"). Whether that reset is worth paying once to fix 165 items is the developer's call.
- **(ff5) THE ACCESS MODEL TREATS A GATED LESSON'S TITLE TWO DIFFERENT WAYS, AND THE GENERATOR IS THE PERMISSIVE ONE.** Measured through the real serving path: an analyst asking for `bess-foundations` gets **five lesson cards and `withheld: 1`** — `spec-sheet-decoded`'s title is not in the payload. But the generated `segment-*` lessons are **public** and their `read-next` prints `lesson_ref` for every lesson in the segment, gated or not, so **two shipped public `segment-*` lessons already name a gated lesson's title** in prose an analyst reads — one names *The Spec Sheet, Decoded*, the other *Where BESS Plugs In: The Three Sockets*. It is a title, not content, and the gate on the sections holds either way — but the two surfaces disagree, and a hand-authored public lesson that names a gated sibling (as this row's `scaling-physics` does, deliberately, to hand the ladder back) is following the generator rather than the track view. Recorded for the developer; **nothing was changed on either surface.**
- **(ff6) THE SCREENSHOTS CAUGHT TWO THINGS FOR A SEVENTH CONSECUTIVE SESSION, AND THE CLASS GENERALISES AGAIN.** Row 18's class was a glyph that lies; session 14's was a number recounted against an adjacent table. This session's first find is the same family one level up: `three-formats` told the reader to read the three cards against **four** named questions, and the Prismatic card answered **three** of them — the heat question had no bullet. Every checker passed 0/0 and no recount would have found it, because the claim is about the *completeness* of the adjacent content rather than about a figure in it. **So the standing instruction takes a third clause: recount every number the prose asserts about an adjacent table, AND check every enumeration the prose promises the adjacent content will answer.** The second find is (ff4). Countermeasures held: **all four `tiles[].k` in pure ASCII**, and the rendered DOM carried only `U+00B0`, `U+00B7`, `U+2014` and the page's own back-arrow. Render at **analyst — the tier that proves a public lesson's gate — 7 of 7 sections, 26,328 characters, zero page errors**, and the count moved on **both** re-renders — **25,968 → 26,279 → 26,328**, once per round of fixes, which is row 18's stale-process evidence. **Viewer through the real serving path: 0 of 7 sections and the page-level denial**; a token under 32 characters returns `SESSION_EXPIRED`.


- **What session 16 added (v06.36r).** **(ii1) THE COVERAGE BOUNDARY IS THE SEGMENT'S ONLY RANKING, MEASURED — so it is the opening section rather than a caveat.** `compute-and-the-rack` is **4 members, 1 · 2 · 1**: exactly the floor and the smallest roster S2 has covered. It has **no named third-party ranking of itself at all** — session 15's `cooling` had three — and the one named independent series it does have measures the adjacent **server** market: IDC's worldwide server revenue share for 1Q26, naming **ODM Direct 50.2 %, Dell 16.5, Supermicro 7.6, HPE 3.0**. **One of the four carries a dossier: 7.6 of the 77.3 points named, 9.8 % of the ranking** — and every figure in it is read out of the one covered member's own file, so **the segment's own member is the source for the fact that the segment is mostly uncovered.** The registry's `notes` had said so in its last sentence since 2026-09-08 and nothing had read it. **The rule to carry: when the floor is also the ceiling, the covered fraction is the module's first measurement, not its last paragraph.** Session 15's **(gg1)** posture, applied to a gap rather than to a refuted hypothesis.
- **(ii2) THE COVERAGE GAP IS VISIBLE IN THE CURRICULUM'S OWN VOCABULARY, AND (hh3)'s COLLISION CLASS FIRES TWICE ON ONE SEGMENT.** The reverse-(t) sweep returned **empty** on `ODM`, `Foxconn`, `Wiwynn`, `Sanmina`, `Wistron`, `Inventec`, `HPE`, `rack OEM`, `contract manufacturer` and `merchant second source` — and found two homonyms instead. **`Dell` appears ten times in `Classroom.gs` and NINE are `Dell'Oro`**, the research firm the cooling landscape cites four times; the single reference to Dell Technologies is a share figure inside the generated `segment-compute-and-the-rack` player table that no prose anywhere explains. **`Quanta` appears forty-two times and ALL forty-two are `Quanta Services`**, the covered EPC contractor — `Quanta Computer`, the ODM, appears **zero** times. Row 20's **(hh3)** found one collision and called it teaching material; here the collisions **point at the coverage boundary** rather than at a teaching hazard: the words are present and the companies are not. **Run the reverse sweep for its collisions, and read what the collisions are about.**
- **(ii3) FIVE OF SIX POSSIBLE PAIRS, SEVEN TYPINGS, AND EXACTLY ONE `competitor` — the densest roster S2 has measured and the least rivalrous.** Four members give six possible pairs; the graph carries **five (83 %)** against cooling's 26 of 55 (47 %). Of the seven curated typings across those five, **one** reads `competitor` — cooling's read 36 of 39. Four of the five edges are **vertical**: one member manufactures another's platform, one is a named OEM for another's rack, one supplies the silicon a third is built on, and two are `partner` **with both sides agreeing**, which the graph rarely shows. **So the segment is a supply chain with a single rivalry in it**, and the standing instrument inverts: (gg1) used the competitor subgraph to test whether a segment splits, and here there is barely a competitor subgraph to test. **And the one missing pair is the two members that do the same job** — both build rack-scale systems to designs somebody else owns, one for each silicon vendor. The two most alike members are the only pair of six the corpus does not connect at all, and the silence is published as a silence.
- **(ii4) EVERY EXTERNAL MEMBERSHIP POINTS UPSTREAM — the segment is a TERMINUS, and that is a graph property rather than a judgment.** Three of the four members sit in another segment, across **six memberships at chain positions 3, 4, 5 and 8** against this segment's 9. **Not one member appears in any of the ten segments downstream.** Four of the six invert, and the direction **sorts perfectly**: no member ranked `incumbent` or `challenger` here ranks higher anywhere else, in **two** chances; the `adjacent` member ranks lower **nowhere**, in **four**. That is **(aa2)** and **(ee3)** firing together for the **second** time after session 15's **(gg4)** — and **complete** here rather than merely dominant. The reading needs no judgment added: a firm that is an incumbent at the end of the chain is a component supplier in the middle of it. **A landscape at the chain's end should measure the DIRECTION of its members' other memberships, not only their sign.**
- **(ii5) THE CROSS-ATTESTATION INSTRUMENT FIRED AS A CROSS-DISAGREEMENT, WHICH IS ITS FIRST NEGATIVE RESULT — and the typing survived it.** **(dd2)** and **(gg2)** established the instrument: two member dossiers stating the same cut about each other independently, in almost the same words, neither citing the other. Run it here and it returns the opposite. **`nvidia` @ v10 puts its own accelerator share at about 80–85 %, per IDC-cited estimates, and marks 92 % explicitly as a 2023 figure; `amd` @ v1 puts the same company at 75–92 %** — a range wide enough to contain that three-year-old peak — **and the registry took AMD's version when it typed the challenger.** Neither names a publication; neither cites the other; nothing reconciles them. **The typing survives** (5–10 % against 80–85 is as decisive as against 75–92) **and the sentence a seller may say does not**, which is the useful half. **An instrument is worth running for its negative result, and a landscape that reports only the agreements is reporting half of what it measured.**
- **(ii6) BOTH DASH ROWS ARE LITERAL-SUBSTRING NEAR-MISSES, AND THE ELIGIBILITY GATE SUPPRESSES ONE CORRECT ANSWER AND ONE PIECE OF NONSENSE IN THE SAME ROW.** The segment resolves **3 of 5** criteria — 8th of 19 by ratio. Criterion 4 reads *"Reference architecture and the qualified power and cooling vendor set"* and the lexicon's key for this segment's **own** lesson is `qualified vendor`: the registry interposed three words inside the phrase the generator looks for. Two *ineligible* entries hit instead — `the-cooling-plant-and-water` on `cooling`, correct and blocked, and `breakers-relays-and-faults` on **`arc`, a substring of `architecture`**, nonsense and blocked. Criterion 5 reads *"Cooling requirement (direct-to-chip, facility-water temperature)"* and `heat-is-the-constraint` is eligible, built and plainly teaches it — with lexicon keys (`heat`, `thermal`, `density`, `kw per rack`, `cold plate`) that use none of the registry's words. That is **(ee4)** on **both** dash rows at once. **And (gg7)'s superlative was RE-MEASURED and is off by one:** the intersection returns **two** segments resolving none (`hyperscalers-and-ai-labs`, `neoclouds`), not three, and neither the generator nor the registry has changed since v05.41r — so it was a mis-measurement at the time. (gg7)'s other half holds: `cooling` at 6 of 6 is still the only segment with no dash row. **§10.4's own lesson, applied to a findings block: do not trust a recorded split — re-run it.**
- **(ii7) `reviewBy` — THE EMPTY SET WAS TOTAL, AND AN ISO-DATE SCAN AND A WORD-FORM SCAN DISAGREE ON THE SAME SEGMENT.** **Nineteen `policyExposure[]` entries across four members, ten dated, and NOT ONE in the future** — the first landscape whose fence has no forward date at all, and a stronger form of **(ee5)**/**(ff3)**. A regular-expression sweep of all four dossiers for future `YYYY-MM-DD` dates returns **nothing anywhere in the files**; the same sweep for dates written in **words** returns **four**. The only one inside six months — **2026-11-10**, an Investor Day two months before the adjacent member's separation, on-subject and held by no other artefact — was **rejected on PROVENANCE**: it lives **only inside a `sources[]` entry's label**, with no `recentDevelopments` entry and no analysis anywhere in the file. **Pinning a module's clock to a bibliography line asserts a significance the record does not.** Three warrant expiries rejected on horizon, five quarter-level candidates rejected for want of a day. `reviewBy` **2027-03-17**, the six-month default, only the **second** of sixteen to take it. **Two rules: run BOTH scans, because an ISO sweep is right about the fence and wrong about the file; and let PROVENANCE, not the scan, do the rejecting.**
- **(ii8) A THIRD `reviewBy` TAUTOLOGY, AND THIS ONE IS UNAVOIDABLE BY CONSTRUCTION.** **(bb4)** requires a landscape to check its own generated segment lesson's clock. It read **2027-03-14** when checked — and then this session regenerated that lesson on 2026-09-17 and the generator recomputed it to **2027-03-17, the same day the module takes.** That is **(dd5)**'s tautology in a third sub-shape: the first two fired on a *sorted gate* the generator had already consumed, this one fires on the *default* and **cannot be avoided**, because §10.6 and the generator apply the same arithmetic to the same date and an S2 session regenerates its segment on the day it writes its module. **It is harmless for the reason that matters — a default is the absence of a bell — and (bb4)'s check should say so rather than reporting a collision.**
- **(ii9) THE SCRAPER ROSTER IS BIASED IN THE SAME DIRECTION AS THE MARKET IT OBSERVES — a class beyond (gg9)'s half-seeded pair.** Denominator re-counted per **(bb5)**: **41 topic seeds / 270 terms and 29 lenses / 249 terms — 519 raw, 494 distinct**, reconciling exactly (267 + 227). Two lenses make the label say covered. Score the terms and the incumbent's roadmap is seeded **exactly** (`blackwell`, `rubin`, `nvlink`) while the challenger's is **entirely absent** (`instinct`, `helios`, `rocm`, `epyc` all zero), and the segment's whole channel vocabulary — `odm`, `odm direct`, `white box`, `contract manufacturer`, `server oem`, `rack oem` — scores **zero**. **A digest scored on that set bands NVIDIA's product news and is blind to AMD's, on a segment whose registry typed AMD a challenger on measured evidence — so the scraper would keep confirming the incumbency it exists to help measure.** (gg9) found a missing half of one pair; this is a **systematic tilt across a roster**, and it is the sharpest argument yet for the term-level audit. **Twelve taken, eight dropped with reasons**, `CUDA` and `ROCm` deliberately taken **together**. The eight-against-fifteen seed shortfall is unchanged at seven and is the developer's; `restart` scores zero for a **ninth** session and session 12's six landlord terms are unseeded for a **fifth**.
- **(ii10) THE SCREENSHOTS CAUGHT SEVEN DEFECTS FOR A TENTH CONSECUTIVE SESSION, AND FIVE OF THE SEVEN ARE THE PROSE CONTRADICTING THE CONTENT BESIDE IT.** Every checker passed 0/0 first and the pre-splice simulations — section ids, micro-markup, glossary usage, ASCII, strict JSON, quiz indices — all returned clean **before** the splice. The seven: **two** paragraphs calling both share ranges *"unattributed"* while the adjacent paragraph and **ledger row 5** say one is sourced to IDC; a sentence calling *"the three parties doing the threatening"* three firms when the sentence before it shows one of them **shrinking** from 6.8 % to 3.0; a `sales` line promising *"three of these four bets"* and enumerating **four**; a table `intro` claiming four rows are *"the shape the spec asks for"* when the spec gives **three** and its own next sentence says so; a `sales` line claiming *"only two of these seven have a date you could put in a diary"* when **not one of the seven does** — every dated cell in the table beside it is a quarter or a half-year; an unsupported *"under a fifth of the market"* against a measured 27.1 against 50.2; and *"four different dates spanning fourteen months"* for four year-ends **six months apart**, repeated in a drill card. **The generalisation, now ten sessions old and sharper each time: a checker can verify that a number is well-formed and never that the sentence beside it is true of the table it points at. RECOUNT, RE-ENUMERATE, AND RE-READ EVERY CLAIM AGAINST THE CONTENT IT DESCRIBES.**

**Phase 4 row 21 — `utility-procurement-meets-ai-load`, v06.38r, 2026-09-17.** Findings from the second gated lesson Phase 4 has authored.

- **(jj1) A BRIEF'S ARITHMETIC ABOUT THE CORPUS IS A CLAIM ABOUT THE CORPUS, AND IT HAS TO BE MEASURED LIKE ONE.** §7.48 stated twice, in both halves, that `market-access` "already carries three gated lessons (`the-certification-stack`, `what-bankable-means`, `the-china-policy-stack`)" and therefore that the analyst `withheld` count would move **3 → 4** rather than 0 → 1 — and called that "a *different* arithmetic and worth calling before you measure it". **All three lessons register zero times in `Classroom.gs`**: they are §7 rows 22–24, unbuilt. The corpus held exactly **three** gated lessons in total (`spec-sheet-decoded`, `where-bess-plugs-in`, `dc-fault-engineering`) and **none of them in `market-access`**, so the real move was **0 → 1**, identical to row 20's. The brief's companion claim that "the analyst index stays at 50 of 51" was wrong on both numbers too: the analyst index **holds at 47** while the corpus goes 50 → 51. **The error was invisible to reading and obvious to a one-line grep, and every downstream number in the brief inherited it.** (r)/(u) say a plan's dates must be re-read rather than carried; this is the same rule for a plan's *counts*. **Grep the corpus for every entity a brief names before you plan against it — including the ones it names only to do arithmetic with.**
- **(jj2) A HANDOFF WRITTEN AS A POINTER SURVIVES THE ROW THAT CLOSES IT — THE FIRST INSTANCE THE PROGRAMME HAS FOUND.** Row 20's **(hh2)** is that a declared handoff can be honoured in substance and false in its *status* clause, because `the-800-vdc-shift` and `where-the-chain-breaks` both said "held by nobody". `where-bess-plugs-in`'s timeline `note` does the same job and **does not break**: it declines "the utility procurement lane — certification orders, storage RFP cycles, new large-load tariffs taking effect" and says it "belongs to the market-access material rather than here". That sentence names **what the material is** and **which track owns it**, and asserts nothing about whether the lesson exists — so the row that closed it changed nothing and corrected nothing. **The rule is now demonstrated in both directions: a handoff that names a destination stays true forever; a handoff that describes a state expires the moment the state changes. Write the destination.**
- **(jj3) THE GENERATOR PUBLISHES THE TITLES OF GATED LESSONS THAT DO NOT EXIST YET — (ff5)/(hh6) IS OLDER AND WIDER THAN RECORDED.** Open item (xi) asked whether this lesson leaks its title through a segment's generated `read-next`. It does, and **it was already leaking before the lesson was written**: `PLANNED_TITLES` renders *The Gatekeeper* **(planned)** in the `read-next` of `segment-hyperscalers-and-ai-labs` and `segment-utilities`, **both `tracks`-gated and both visible to an analyst**. Worse, `segment-utilities` prints **two** unbuilt guidance lessons in one callout — *The Gatekeeper* and *The Rulebook Above the Fence* (`interconnection-for-large-loads`, §7 row 26, also `guidance`). So the asymmetry is not "a built gated lesson's title escapes"; it is that **the generator advertises the titles of gated lessons the analyst will never be able to open, whether or not they have been written.** Registration does not create the leak — it only removes the word that made it honest.
- **(jj4) THE RENDERER'S TIMELINE HAS TWO CONTRACTS NOTHING DOCUMENTS, AND BOTH ARE SILENTLY VIOLABLE.** Read out of `Classroom.html` before the splice. **(a)** `CL_LANE_COLORS = { gen, deploy, eco }` is keyed on the **literal lane names**, and `clTimeline` falls back to `var(--cl-faint)` for both the legend swatch and the dot — so a timeline with semantically-named lanes loses the CVD-validated trio entirely and renders every lane the same grey. `where-bess-plugs-in`'s `rules`/`tx` lanes are in exactly that state today. **(b)** `clTimeline` prints `Math.floor(it.x)` in the left gutter as a year label. On a relative-years timeline that reads correctly; on `where-bess-plugs-in`'s calendar it prints **1 2 3 4 5 6**. This row put real years in `x` (2025.52 … 2027.0) and the gutter reads **2025 / 2026 / 2027**, deduplicated — which is what the field is for. **Neither contract is in `CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md` and neither checker can see a violation; both are cosmetic-looking and both make the instrument say something false.**
- **(jj5) THE (ii7) DISAGREEMENT REPEATED AND THE WORD-FORM SCAN WON BY FIVE TO ONE.** S2 session 16 found an ISO regex and a word-form scan disagreeing about one segment. Here, across the module and the six utility guides, the **ISO regex returns exactly one** future day-level date — the module's own `reviewBy`, which is the answer being sought, so the scan is a tautology — while the **word-form scan returns five** genuine dates the regex cannot see, **two of them earlier than the inherited date**. This is the first Phase 4 sort to surface a candidate that would have beaten the inheritance, and it still does not move it: both were rejected on **scope** and **subject** rather than on lateness, which is a stronger result than an empty set. **A `reviewBy` sort implemented as a regex over `YYYY-MM-DD` is not a sort of the material; it is a sort of the material's machine-readable fields, and guidance prose is not one of them.**
- **(jj6) SIX RECOUNT DEFECTS BEFORE THE SPLICE AND FOUR SCREENSHOT DEFECTS AFTER IT — ELEVEN CONSECUTIVE SESSIONS.** The six pre-splice simulations returned **0/0** and then a manual recount pass over every number and enumeration caught **six**, among them a field note asserting "three of the four moves buy hardware **and** the one that does not is the one that creates a buyer the utility never meets" — **two different moves welded into one sentence** — and a timeline note claiming three future dates where the items give two. The screenshots then caught **four more**, two of them the (ii10) class: a table `note` saying "only one of them actually buys anything" when its own row 2 is gas *procurement*, and a timeline item calling itself "the one date on this line that has not happened" **directly above** a note saying two have. **The new observation is about ordering: the recount pass and the screenshot pass catch different defects and neither substitutes for the other.** The recount caught contradictions *inside* a field; the screenshots caught contradictions *between adjacent fields*, which are only visible once the fields are laid out together. **Run both, in that order, every time.**

- **(kk1) THE BRIEF'S ARITHMETIC WAS RIGHT THIS TIME, AND MEASURING IT IS STILL WHAT MADE IT USABLE — (jj1) APPLIED TO A BRIEF THAT PASSED.** §7.49 stated 20 members at 2 · 3 · 15 and 75 % adjacent. Re-derived off `profiler-segments.json` before a line was drafted: **exactly right**, and the same computation established the part the brief could only assert — that **75.0 % is the highest adjacent share of all nineteen segments**, ahead of `clean-firm-and-nuclear` at 68.8 % and `grid-equipment` at 60.0 %. **A confirmed claim and a measured superlative are different artefacts**, and only the second can open a module. The same pass also caught the *module's own* first-draft claim that "five segments carry no adjacent member at all" — **it is three**. (jj1)'s lesson generalises: the arithmetic to distrust is whichever you did not run, including your own.
- **(kk2) (aa2) AND (ee3) FIRED TOTAL ON BOTH SIDES — THE FIRST COMPLETE DOUBLE INVERSION THE PROGRAMME HAS MEASURED.** 38 external memberships across the roster. **All 15 of the 15 adjacent members are ranked incumbent or challenger in at least one other segment** — eleven of them **incumbent** — and **not one is adjacent everywhere**. Against that, **4 of the 5** external memberships held by this segment's own *ranked* members **demote** them (`flexgen` twice, `stem`, `gridmatic`; only `fluence` rises). **The bench is other segments' ranked players and the ranked players are other segments' bench**, with no exceptions on either side. Session 15's (gg4) and session 16's (ii4) each fired both instruments; neither was complete. And one member — `habitat-energy` — belongs to **no** other segment at all, the only company in the corpus whose entire identity is this segment.
- **(kk3) A SEGMENT DECLINED THE TERMINUS CLAIM ON ITS OWN ARITHMETIC, WHICH IS A NEW OUTCOME FOR (ii4)'s INSTRUMENT.** All 38 memberships point **upstream** — the same shape `compute-and-the-rack` published as a finding. Here it carries **almost no information**: this segment is **position 18 of 19**, so exactly **one** segment lies downstream, where position 9 had **ten**. The module says so rather than banking the superlative, and reports what the distribution *does* say instead — supply 14, build 14, demand 8, services 2, so 36 of 38 reach into the parts of the chain that build and buy the batteries. **An instrument that fires identically from two positions is measuring the position, not the segment; check the denominator before publishing the result.**
- **(kk4) THE BRIEF UNDERSTATED THE RANKING VACUUM, AND THE CORRECTION IS MORE PUBLISHABLE THAN THE CLAIM.** §7.49 recorded "no third party reachable in the P3 research ranks any player in storage bidding except Modo Energy". Measured across the member dossiers: **four** named third parties — Wood Mackenzie, Modo Energy, Amperical, Guidehouse Insights — publish **six** measurements, and **not one ranks this segment**. The sharper finding is the shape: **coverage and relevance run in opposite directions.** The ranking the corpus covers completely (Wood Mackenzie's AC-block integrator table) measures the **wrong market** — and this segment's own incumbent is **deliberately absent from it**, reading that absence in its own file as confirmation of the lane it chose. The two that measure the right market are covered at **44 %** (the US optimiser census, **vendor-written**, which quantifies six peers and lists one of this segment's two incumbents in capability-only language) and **20 %** (a Great Britain table whose leader is uncovered). **(ii1) said the coverage boundary is the only ranking; this row adds that the best-covered ranking can be the least relevant one.**
- **(kk5) (dd2)'s CROSS-ATTESTATION INSTRUMENT RETURNED SEVEN FOR SEVEN — ITS WIDEST POSITIVE.** One member's `ecosystemRole` names **seven** rivals' vendor-bundled software products — Mosaic and Nispera, GEMS, Autobidder, EQ-S, insightOS, mtu EnergetIQ, the AVEVA layer — as divisions "sold with, or to protect, the vendor's own equipment". **Every one is confirmed in that vendor's own dossier.** Previous firings were pairwise; this is one claim about seven counterparties, each attested independently on the other side. **A claim a rival makes about seven companies, which all seven corroborate about themselves, is the strongest evidence a corpus-synthesis module can carry** — and it is what let this module state the segment's spine as fact rather than as one member's opinion.
- **(kk6) `reviewBy` — (jj5) AT ITS MOST EXTREME: THE TWO SCANS' IN-HORIZON RESULTS ARE DISJOINT, AND A NEW REJECTION CLASS WAS NEEDED.** An ISO-date regex finds **5** future day-level dates and a word-form scan finds **13** — and inside the six-month horizon **neither is a subset of the other**: ISO sees only `zhonhen` 2026-10-31, word-form only `habitat-energy` 2026-09-30 and `hunt-energy-network` 2027-02-01, **intersection zero**. Taking either scan alone would have produced a different review date. A second result: scanning only the fields §10.6 specifies returns **nothing** in horizon — the nearest candidate is visible **only** when the scan is widened past `policyExposure[]`, so **the scope of a scan matters as much as its form**. The nearest candidate, a Companies House filing deadline **thirteen days out**, was rejected on **the filer's own cadence** — its previous accounts, for a year ended 31 December 2024, were filed **4 January 2026**, more than twelve months later — and carried into the indicators table instead. That is a rejection on the gate's **compliance history**, a class beyond scope, subject and provenance. Result: **2027-03-17**, the six-month default, only the **third** of seventeen landscapes to take it against **fourteen** that found a dated gate.
- **(kk7) THE RECOUNT PASS AND THE SCREENSHOT PASS CAUGHT NINE DEFECTS BETWEEN THEM AND NOT ONE OVERLAPPED — (jj6) CONFIRMED AT A TWELFTH SESSION, AND THE SPLIT IS NOW ITS OWN FINDING.** All six pre-splice simulations returned **0/0** — section ids, micro-markup, glossary usage, ASCII, strict JSON, quiz indices. The **recount** pass then found **five**: two miscounts ("five segments" for three; "five named third parties" for four parties publishing six measurements) and **three superlatives the adjacent content contradicted** — "the largest company in this segment" when two *bench* members are larger, "the largest recurring base any member discloses" when the module's own next section shows a challenger's line is bigger, and "the largest fence any landscape in this lane has met" when it is the **seventh**-largest at 71 against 142. The **screenshots** then found **four more**, all invisible on the page but plain in the layout: a fence claim that every future dated item belongs to an *adjacent* member when one belongs to an **incumbent**; a sales line calling none of eight rows "a regulatory date" beside a row that is a **statutory filing deadline**; and, twice, **a paragraph promising six near-misses and enumerating five** — the Great Britain table dropped and the last called "the fifth". **Superlatives are the recount pass's richest vein and enumerations are the screenshot pass's; a checker sees neither.**
- **(kk8) THE SCRAPER MATCHER IS BLIND TO ITS OWN PLURALS, CORPUS-WIDE — a measured open item, and the sharpest instance yet of why (i) forbids the label-level glance.** `scTermsHit_()` requires a non-alphanumeric character on **both** sides of a match, a guard that exists so `ABB` does not fire inside `RABBIT`. The same guard means **a singular term can never match its own plural**. Measured across the **506** distinct held terms: **447 of the 451 singular-form terms — 99 % — have no plural counterpart seeded**, and only **4** do. `executive order` cannot fire on "executive orders"; `tariff` cannot fire on "tariffs"; `interconnection` cannot fire on "interconnections". It is pre-existing and **not an S2 session's to fix**, so it is recorded as open item **(xiv)**. It did change this seed: the segment's own class noun was seeded as **two plurals in two spellings** because the trade writes it as a class, and the singulars were **knowingly foregone in writing** rather than silently lost. The seed's other measurement: **33 of 35 candidates scored zero against all 506**, which is a corpus with almost no vocabulary for a segment it has carried since S0.

- **What Phase 4 row 22 added (v06.42r).** **(ll1) A SIMULATION CAN RETURN 0/0 BECAUSE IT NEVER RAN, AND 0/0 IS EXACTLY WHAT A CLEAN PASS LOOKS LIKE.** The pre-splice micro-markup pass reported *0 formatted fields walked, 0 failures* and was accepted for about ten seconds. It had walked nothing: `fmt_strings()` in `check-classroom-content.py` takes **a section** and had been handed **the whole lesson**, so its `sec.get("kind")` missed and the generator yielded immediately. Corrected, the same pass walked **141** fields and caught a nested emphasis — `**5 - Never answer *is it foreign-produced* without naming the instrument.**` — that would have printed literal asterisks to every reader, because clFmt's bold regex uses a character class that cannot contain an asterisk. **The tell was the zero in the denominator, not the zero in the numerator.** (kk1) says a brief's arithmetic is usable only once you have run it; this is the same rule one level down — **a passing check is usable only once you have confirmed it had something to check.** Every simulation in this session was subsequently made to print its own walked-count beside its failure-count, and the per-section breakdown (27 · 2 · 30 · 6 · 28 · 6 · 12 · 30) is the evidence that all eight sections were reached. **Print the denominator.**
- **(ll2) THE SAME CONTRADICTION IN THREE PLACES, AND THE RECOUNT PASS SAW NONE OF THEM.** The lesson's best line is that *foreign-produced* means different things in different instruments. Three separate passages stated it as **"three regimes now use that phrase"** and then, within the same paragraph, **"the tax rules do not use the phrase at all"** — in the EO callout, in red line 5, and in the question of drill card 3. Only **two** instruments share the phrase; the tax rules answer the same practical question on a different one. Every mechanical check passed it: the JSON is valid, the markup is clean, the terms resolve, and no count is asserted that a script could compare. **The recount pass missed it too**, because a recount compares a stated number to a structure beside it and here the number was stated about the *world*, not about a table. **It was visible the moment the paragraph was laid out and read as prose.** The generalisation worth keeping: (kk7) sorts the two passes by defect type — superlatives to the recount, enumerations to the screenshots — and this is a third type that belongs only to the screenshots: **a claim contradicted by a later clause of its own sentence.** It also replicated across three sections because the phrasing was drafted once and reused, which is the cheapest way for one error to become three.
- **(ll3) THE CORRECT ANSWER WAS THE SOLE LONGEST CHOICE IN FOUR OF FIVE QUIZ ITEMS — A DEFECT NO CHECKER, NO RECOUNT AND NO PRIOR SESSION HAS EVER LOOKED FOR.** (ff4) tracks the **index** of the answer and this row varied it properly (3·2·0·1·2). But length is a second channel and it was wide open: measured, the right choice was the strict maximum in **4 of 5** items, by as much as **187 characters against 87**. A reader who knows nothing about policy scores four of five by picking the longest option. The cause is structural rather than careless — the correct choice carries the nuance, so it grows while the distractors stay flat — which means **it will recur on every lesson that is not explicitly measured for it.** Fixed by bringing the distractors up to comparable substance rather than cutting the answers down, so no teaching value was lost; after the fix **no item's correct choice is the sole longest**. **This is now a standing pre-commit measurement for any lesson carrying a quiz**, and it is a screenshot-pass find: the tell was three options on one line and the fourth wrapping to two.
- **(ll4) A SUPERLATIVE BEATEN BY A PARAGRAPH THREE BELOW IT, AND A FOUR-WAY COMPARISON MADE AT A DATE WHEN ONLY THREE EXISTED.** Two more screenshot finds of the (kk7) recount type that the recount nonetheless missed. The EO callout called its 69 kV example **"the cleanest illustration in this lesson"** that the tests are about circumstances — while the next definition but one shows **one inverter** getting two opposite answers from two instruments, which is cleaner on any reading. And the timeline's first entry, at 22 December 2023, called the defense list **"the narrowest lever of the four"** — but the tax lever it is being compared against **was created on 4 July 2025**, eighteen months later, and that creation is the entry two rows below. **A superlative is a claim about everything else on the page, and a comparison is a claim about what existed at the time.** Both were replaced with claims that survive their own neighbours: the first with *why* the answer differs (this instrument scopes on connection, not on product), the second with the sharper historical point that the instrument which would make the list decisive did not yet exist.
- **(ll5) A HANDOFF WRITTEN AS A DESTINATION SURVIVED, AND A GENERATED LABEL DID NOT — (jj2) AND (jj3) MEASURED ON THE SAME COMMIT.** `how-a-cell-is-made` says the interaction of the three domestic-content levers "belongs to *Four Levers and One Order* in the market track". It names a **destination**, so registering this lesson made nothing in it false — (jj2) holding for a second time, now across a *track* boundary. The generated side did the opposite. `segment-cells-and-chemistry`, which **an analyst can read**, renders `*Four Levers and One Order* (planned)` in two places — its `read-next` callout and its `what-is-bought-and-on-what` row — and **as of this commit the word "(planned)" is false**. (jj3) said registration "only removes the word that made it honest"; measured, **registration removes nothing**. The word is removed by a **regeneration**, which this row is forbidden to run, so the corpus spends the interval until the next S2 session telling analysts that a built lesson is unbuilt. The checker knows — that segment is in the due list, differing in exactly those two sections — but nothing tells a *reader*. **Open item (xi) is therefore worse than recorded: the leak's harmless phase ends at registration and its dishonest phase begins there.**

- **What session 18 added (v06.43r).** **(mm1) TWO STANDING INSTRUMENTS HAD AN EMPTY INPUT SET AND BOTH ABSENCES WERE PUBLISHED AS ABSENCES — (kk3) AT ITS LIMIT, AND THE FIRST TIME A LANDSCAPE HAS OPENED ON WHAT IT COULD NOT MEASURE.** `insurance-and-risk-transfer` is **3 members, 2 · 1 · 0** — the **smallest roster of all nineteen** (next is four, largest thirty-four) and the exact inverse of session 17's twenty at 75.0 % adjacent. Two consequences, both measured before a line was drafted. The **role-inversion** instrument of **(aa2)**/**(ee3)** needs a member belonging to two segments: measured across all **314** memberships, **all three hold zero external memberships** — session 17 found `habitat-energy` notable as the only company whose whole identity was one segment, and here **every member is that case**. The **terminus** instrument of **(ii4)** needs something downstream: the segment is **chain position 19 of 19**, so the denominator is **zero**, and with no external memberships there is nothing to point in any direction either — **empty twice over**. Session 17 declined the terminus claim from position 18 on the ground that one segment below nearly forces the result; **from 19 the reading carries no information at all.** Both were written into the module's **first** section, before anything that ranks anybody. **A landscape that reports only the instruments that fired is reporting half of what it measured.**
- **(mm2) THE THIRD INSTRUMENT FIRED COMPLETE — 3 OF 3 POSSIBLE PAIRS, THE FIRST COMPLETE ROSTER THE LANE HAS MET — AND THE TYPINGS DID THE ARGUING.** `profiler-graph.json` (1,429 edges, 174 companies) connects **every one of the three possible member pairs, 100 %**, against **(ii3)**'s previous best of 5 of 6 on a four-member roster. The shape is what matters: **one `competitor` edge, typed by BOTH sides, between the two incumbents; two `partner` edges to the challenger, each typed by the broker's side.** The two brokers compete with each other — in court, after twenty people left one for the other inside a 38-minute window — and both are the challenger's **distribution**, because a broker places risk and a managing general agent prices it. **A structure that would take a paragraph to argue was simply drawn by the graph**, and on a three-member roster the graph is the only instrument with anything in it.
- **(mm3) THE REGISTRY'S PROSE AND THE RELATIONSHIP GRAPH ATTESTED THE SAME FINDING FROM TWO INDEPENDENT DIRECTIONS — (dd2)'s INSTRUMENT FIRING ACROSS ARTEFACT KINDS RATHER THAN ACROSS TWO DOSSIERS.** The segment's organising fact is that **neither incumbent broker anchors its battery-storage guidance to a named standard** — seven Marsh documents across four web properties from 2021 to 2026 and every reviewed Aon battery and renewables publication name no UL 9540A, NFPA 855, DNV, CSA, TUV, IEC, IEEE or FM Global, and both state insurer requirements in engineering terms instead. The registry's `notes`, written at assignment on 2026-09-09 and untouched since, end on exactly that: the gap **"is the gap the assurance segment's members occupy"**. Run independently, the graph says the same thing: fourteen edges touch the roster, eleven reach outside it, and **`dnv` is the only company on the far end of an edge from all three members** — an `assurance` **incumbent** at chain position 17. Neither artefact cites the other. **Previous firings of (dd2) were dossier against dossier; this is a curated prose field against a built graph, and it is what let the module state the gap as measured rather than as one reader's impression.**
- **(mm4) `reviewBy` CAME OUT OF `ownership.ticker`, A FIELD NO PREVIOUS S2 SESSION HAS FOUND A GATE IN, AND TWO NEW REJECTION CLASSES WERE NEEDED.** The fence contributes **nothing** — 17 `policyExposure[]` entries across three members, **5 dated, zero in the future** — and **(jj5)**'s two scans diverge completely: an ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` sweep of all three **whole dossiers** returns **nothing anywhere in any file**, while a word-form sweep returns **ten** future days of which **four** are in horizon. The date taken, **2026-12-31**, is the boundary of two *different* transactions stated in two *different* fields — the challenger's acquisition closing "in the second half of 2026" and its acquirer's own takeover "expected before the end of 2026", the latter inside `ownership.ticker` — and the **segment registry instructs the review at that date in prose**, saying the `challenger` label "may be the wrong label by the end of 2026". **Eight rejections in writing**, two of them classes the lane has not recorded: **a date before which nothing MAY happen is not a date on which anything MUST** (the USI merger agreement's earliest-close floor of 19 October 2026, stated twice, bracketing an expected quarter alongside an outside date of 1 June 2027), and **a date the record's own text hedges as "(unconfirmed)" cannot set a clock** (both incumbents' next results dates, each marked unconfirmed with the reason given). A third rejection is worth keeping for its shape: 2026-12-31 appears a second time in the corpus **only as a superseded date** — an employment agreement extended past it — so **the same calendar day arrives twice, once as a live boundary and once as a dead one.** **(bb4)'s own-segment-lesson check passes clean and (ii8)'s unavoidable default tautology does NOT fire**, the first S2 session since (ii8) was recorded where it does not: the generated lesson recomputes to 2027-03-17 and the module takes 2026-12-31.
- **(mm5) (ll3) FIRED, AND CLOSING IT OPENED THE INVERSE CHANNEL — MEASURE BOTH ENDS.** Row 22 found the correct quiz choice was the sole longest in four of five items and prescribed raising the distractors. Done here, the first measurement read **4 of 5 sole longest**; raising the distractors took it to **0 of 5 sole longest and 3 of 5 sole SHORTEST**, which is the same channel open in the other direction and just as exploitable. A second pass took it to **0 and 0**, with the correct choice ranking **2, 3, 3, 2, 3** of four by length. **(ll3)'s prescription is necessary and not sufficient: the target is a mixed rank distribution, not a longer distractor**, and the check must print both extremes or it will keep reporting a pass while a reader can still score four of five on shape. Indices varied per **(ff4)** at 3, 1, 2, 0, 1.
- **(mm6) (ll1) ONE LEVEL ALONG: THE DENOMINATOR WAS RIGHT AND THE PATH WAS WRONG, THREE TIMES IN ONE SESSION.** Row 22's lesson was that a simulation returning 0/0 may never have run. Every simulation here printed its walked-count and a per-section breakdown (**206** formatted fields at 12 · 12 · 14 · 29 · 8 · 77 · 8 · 16 · 30, **519** held Scraper terms, **218** text fields, **1,429** graph edges), and **three checks still produced confident, precise, entirely false results because they traced the wrong path.** (1) A case-blindness measurement found **83 of 517 held terms (16.1 %) carry an uppercase character** and concluded, from `scTermsHit_()` being called with lowercased text, that none of them can ever match — false, because `scLoadInterestModel_()` lowercases every term **two functions earlier**. (2) A graph query keyed on `source`/`target` reported **0 edges touching the roster** when the real keys are `a`/`b` and the answer is **14**. (3) A first ledger draft cited `strategyRead[8]` for a date that lives in `ownership.ticker`. **Print the denominator, and then trace the whole path before publishing what the denominator means** — a check that measures the right number of the wrong things is indistinguishable from one that works.
