- **(mm7) THE CHECKER CAUGHT A FIELD CLASS THE SIMULATIONS DID NOT KNOW EXISTED, AND THE FIX IS A SIMULATION, NOT A HABIT.** `check-classroom-content.py` failed the first splice on `tiles[0].sub` carrying `{{incumbent}}`: **`tiles[]`, `glossary[]`, section `title`/`read` and the module's own `title`/`short` reach the DOM through `textContent`**, so micro-markup in any of them is printed literally. The micro-markup simulation had counted those same tile strings as legitimate `{{term}}` *uses* — right denominator, wrong scope, **(mm6)** in a third shape. A **plain-text-field guard walking 48 fields** was added to the session's own simulation set in the same hour, so the class cannot recur silently.
- **(mm8) THE BRIEF'S OWN INSTRUCTION FALSIFIED ITS OWN PREDICTION, AND MEASURING IT BEFORE THE WRITE IS WHAT CAUGHT IT — (jj1)/(kk1), A THIRD TIME.** §7.51 states "your regeneration closes it for `cells-and-chemistry`". It cannot: item (5) of the same brief scopes the run to **one** segment and the paste-in half forbids `--all`, so `segment-cells-and-chemistry` still renders `*Four Levers and One Order* (planned)` in both places. **What the regeneration actually closed is the same (ll5) leak in this session's own segment** — `segment-insurance-and-risk-transfer` rendered `*Who Carries Which Risk* (planned)` in `read-next` **and** in row 3 of `what-is-bought-and-on-what`, and `contracts-and-revenue` has been built since v05.91r. **The arithmetic to distrust is whichever you did not run, including a brief's claim about which of its own instructions will have which effect.** `--check` **6 → 6 → 5**, both halves derived from the parsed generator before the write and exact — **(aa5) holds for a FOURTEENTH session.**
- **(mm9) THE SCREENSHOT PASS CAUGHT A REPLICATED POSITIONAL ERROR IN FOUR PLACES — (ll2)'s REUSE MECHANISM, AND ONLY THE LAID-OUT PAGE SHOWED IT.** All eight pre-splice simulations returned 0/0; the recount pass then caught **eleven** defects, including an indicator table whose intro and sales line each stated a day-count the table beside them contradicted — **twice, in opposite directions, on two successive attempts** before the row-by-row count settled at four rows carrying a day, three of those days already past, and not one row putting a future day on the event it asks you to watch. The screenshots then caught **eleven more with zero overlap**, a **fourteenth** consecutive session for **(kk7)**. The sharpest was positional: `assurance` is chain position **17** against this segment's **19**, with `software-and-optimization` at 18 between them, and **one phrase — "one position upstream" — had been drafted once and reused into four places** across the module and the analysis file. A second was a word inherited from a dossier whose own numbers refute it: "**quadrupled** from USD 1.5bn to USD 5bn" is **3.3x**. A third was a contradiction between sections — three passages called one incumbent's programme a **lifecycle** one while two others called it "the construction tower" and said its rival "answered on the phase it had not taken", when the filing's own words are "from construction through ongoing operations". **Superlatives are the recount pass's vein and replicated phrasings are the screenshots'; a checker sees neither, and neither does a recount that reads fields one at a time.**
- **(nn1) THE `--check` FORECAST'S TWO HALVES ARE NOT TWO CHANNELS — THE SECOND IS A SUBSET OF THE FIRST, AND THE GENERATOR SAYS SO IN ONE LINE.** Every brief since (aa5) has asked for the forecast to be derived as a `READ_NEXT` membership half and a `CRITERION_LEXICON` deepening half. Traced through the generator rather than reasoned about: `sec_what_is_bought()` opens with `eligible = set(READ_NEXT[seg.id])` and admits a lexicon hit **only if that lesson is already in the segment's `READ_NEXT`**. Measured for this row: **19 segments walked, 2 name the lesson**; **109 criterion strings walked, 6 keyword hits, 4 of them gated out** — `cells-and-chemistry`, `utilities`, `software-and-optimization` and `insurance-and-risk-transfer` all carry a criterion that names a certification and none of them can ever show this lesson. So the deepening half can never reach a segment the membership half does not, and the forecast collapsed to the same two segments on both halves: **5 → 7 due, each differing in `read-next` AND `what-is-bought-and-on-what`**, exact. **(aa5) holds for a FIFTEENTH session, and the instruction that produced it can now be stated more cheaply: forecast the membership half, then ask only whether each member also carries a matching criterion.**
- **(nn2) REGISTRATION DOES NOT CLOSE THE `(planned)` LEAK — IT OPENS ONE, AND THIS ROW OPENED TWO.** §7.52 recorded that `cells-and-chemistry` was still leaking and that this row would not close it. What it did not say is the other half: the moment a gated lesson registers, **every generated segment that names it starts rendering a false word**. `segment-storage-integrators-and-containers` and `segment-assurance` both render *Listing, Test Method, Code* **(planned)** in two places each as of this commit, and both are wrong the instant the commit lands. One of the two closes itself — `assurance` is the next S2 session's own segment — and **`storage-integrators-and-containers` is named by no scheduled session at all**, so it inherits `cells-and-chemistry`'s position. **(xi) is not a property of one segment; it is the standing cost of every gated registration, and the register should count instances rather than name a segment.**
- **(nn3) (mm6) AT FIRST HAND, IN THIS SESSION'S OWN HARNESS: 0 OF 69 BOUND, AND THE DEFECT DID NOT EXIST.** The render probe asked whether each `.cl-term` span carried a `title`, a `dataset.tip` or a `has-tip` class, reported **0 bound of 69**, and looked exactly like a broken tooltip layer. There are no per-span attributes: `Classroom.html` binds **one delegated `mouseover`/`click` listener on the section root** and resolves `clGlossMap[term.toLowerCase()]` at hover time. Re-probed on the real path, **69 of 69 resolve against a 4,208-key map with zero misses** — and the 69 agrees exactly with the pre-splice simulation's independently counted 69 `{{term}}` uses. **A denominator that is right and a path that is wrong produces a *false alarm* as readily as a false pass, and the tell is the same: the number is suspiciously round at one end.**
- **(nn4) THE PAGE CLEARS `sessionStorage` DURING BOOT, SO A PRE-NAVIGATION SEED IS WIPED — WHICH IS WHY THE BRIEF SAYS *AFTER LOAD*.** Seeding the four keys through Playwright's `add_init_script` is the obvious move and it silently fails: dumped immediately after load, `sessionStorage` held **`{audio-unlocked: 1}` and nothing else**, `clRole()` returned `viewer` and `clAdmitted()` returned false, so the router made **zero** `__gas` requests and the render came back at **51 characters** with no page errors at all. Re-seeded after load, the same run gave `contributor`, admitted, and **25,100 characters**. **A harness instruction that reads like a detail is usually the scar of a silent failure; this one is, and now it is written down with its symptom.**
- **(nn5) THE DENIAL CHANNEL CONFIRMS A GATED LESSON'S EXISTENCE, AND A GATED TRACK'S DOES NOT — AN ASYMMETRY THE SCHEMA DESCRIBES AND NOBODY HAS MEASURED.** Base against head at the analyst tier, `cop=lesson&id=the-certification-stack` answered **`UNKNOWN_LESSON` before the commit and `ROLE_DENIED` after it**. The id and the title stay out of the analyst index payload — which is **byte-identical at 42,265 characters** across the commit — so nothing is enumerable; but an analyst who *guesses* the id learns from the error string alone that the lesson now exists. `CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md` gives `UNKNOWN_TRACK` to a gated track explicitly **"so gated tracks are not enumerable"**, and gives a gated lesson `ROLE_DENIED`. **The two halves of the same access model answer a probe differently, and only the track half was designed for the probe.** Not this row's to change — recorded so a later gate review decides it deliberately.
- **(nn6) A NEIGHBOUR'S PRE-DECLARED HANDOFF HAD GONE STALE, AND §10.6 (t)'s SCAN IS WHAT FOUND IT.** `spec-sheet-decoded`'s `safety-words` callout — the vocabulary layer this lesson is specified to build on — teaches that UL 9540A produces data **"at cell, module and unit level"** with **"no pass or fail"**, and repeats the second in a flashcard and a quiz `why`. Both are **5th-edition facts**. The 6th edition drops unit level for most non-residential systems and applies pass/fail to the installation test from **2027-01-01**. Registering this row does not make that lesson wrong — (jj2) again — but it does put the correction one card away from the claim. **The scan's value was not avoiding duplication; it was finding that the neighbour this lesson defers to is the one that needs the revision.** Written into the new function's header comment so a later revision cannot import the stale phrasing; the revision itself belongs to a session that owns that lesson.
- **(nn7) THE CLAIM I HAD JUST CORRECTED REPLICATED INTO TWO MORE SECTIONS, AND ONE OF THEM CONTRADICTED THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE IT.** *A test method with no pass or fail* is the phrase the whole lesson exists to qualify, and the draft carried it unqualified in a glossary definition, in a closing callout, and — worst — **inside the very paragraph that qualifies it**, which read *"under the 6th it is true of the cell, module and unit levels"* three lines after establishing that the 6th has no unit level for the system in question. The recount pass read that paragraph and passed it; the laid-out page made it obvious. **(mm9)'s reuse mechanism has a sharper form than a wrong fact copied around: a *superseded* fact copied around, in the same document that supersedes it.** Grep the draft for the phrasing of the thing you are correcting, not only for the thing you are asserting.
- **(nn8) THE TWO `reviewBy` SWEEPS DISAGREED AT THEIR WIDEST YET, AND A THIRD FORM CARRIED THE ONLY DATES ONE SOURCE HAD.** Over the gate module: the ISO sweep returned **3 distinct dates, all of them metadata** (`updated`, a revision date, `reviewBy`); the word-form sweep returned **35, all of them content** — the regulatory gates. Over the study guide's three cited sections, **both sweeps returned zero** and a third form, the bare parenthetical year, returned **five**, all past. (jj5)/(kk6)/(mm4) have found the two sweeps disagreeing, disjoint, and one returning nothing; this session found the pair **jointly empty on a source that does carry dates**. **The form a date takes is a property of the source, not of the corpus, so the scan has to be chosen per input rather than run twice and trusted.** Nine rejections written down; `2026-10-01` was the nearest future date in the module and was rejected because it gates the grid gauntlet, which this lesson names only to exclude.
- **(nn9) A REGISTRY ALIAS WOULD HAVE TOOLTIPPED THE WRONG DEFINITION, AND THE CHECKER CANNOT SEE IT BECAUSE THE TERM RESOLVES.** `{{accreditation}}` — a natural word in a lesson about laboratories and certification bodies — resolves through the concepts registry to **ELCC**, *effective load carrying capability*, which carries `capacity accreditation` and the bare `accreditation` among its aliases. The content checker only warns on a term that resolves **nowhere**; a term that resolves to the wrong concept passes silently and prints a confident, irrelevant tooltip. **(hh3)'s collision class, moved from the naming path to the tooltip path** — and the tooltip path is the one a reader actually meets. Caught before the draft used it. **Checking a candidate term against the registry means reading the definition that comes back, not confirming that one does.**
- **(nn10) CLOSING (ll3)'s LENGTH CHANNEL COSTS PROSE, AND THE MEASUREMENT CANNOT SEE THE COST.** The first pass had the correct choice **sole longest in 4 of 5** — (ll3)/(mm5) firing for a third consecutive row. Raising distractors and trimming answers took it to **0 sole-longest, 0 sole-shortest, ranks 2·2·3·2·2**, and left one distractor ending *"...the ground the RFP is reaching for here"* — a word added purely for character count, invisible to every check that reads lengths and obvious in the screenshot. **A channel closed by padding is a channel closed at the reader's expense unless the padding is re-read as prose.** Rewrite the distractor to be *more plausible*, then measure; do not measure first and pad to fit.
- **(nn11) THE SCREENSHOT PASS HAS A STRUCTURAL BLIND SPOT AND (kk7) SHOULD NAME IT: FLASHCARD ANSWERS ARE BEHIND A CLICK.** The drill renders six cards showing their question and **CLICK TO FLIP**; no answer text is in the laid-out page a screenshot captures. So the pass that has caught a defect in fifteen consecutive sessions **cannot read roughly a sixth of this lesson's prose**, and that sixth is the part most likely to carry a restated fact — as (nn7) did. **Flashcard answers are recount-only, and the recount has to know that.**
- **What session 19 added (v06.45r) — the last landscape, and the phase closes with it.** **(oo1) NO LEAGUE TABLE IN THE CORPUS COVERS THE ROSTER, AND THE COVERAGE BOUNDARY IS THEREFORE A TABLE OF INSTRUMENTS RATHER THAN A CAVEAT.** `assurance` is **7 members at 4 · 1 · 2**, and **four separate instruments touch it**: a July 2026 battery-storage TIC assessment naming six majors of which **four are members here**; a May 2026 global TIC revenue league table ranking **two of the seven** (5th at USD 4,619m, 8th at USD 3,106m) and omitting three more by name; the ENR design lists covering **three others**; and, for the fourth incumbent, **nothing at all**. **The largest coverage any single source gives is four of seven, and the two revenue-based instruments share no company.** The sub-market claim is true and small — the two firms it calls leading key players out-book its whole **USD 0.66bn** by **4.6x and 7.0x** on their own group revenue. (ii1) measured a covered *fraction* of one ranking; this measured that **no ranking exists for the whole**, which is a different and commoner shape. **When four instruments each cover a different part of a roster, name the instrument in every ranking sentence and put the table first.**
- **(oo2) THE FIRST WHOLLY COMPETITIVE ROSTER S2 HAS MEASURED — 15 TYPINGS, 15 `competitor`, AND THE SIX MISSING PAIRS DRAW THE SEGMENT'S OWN PARTITION.** 15 of 21 possible pairs carry an edge; 12 are curated and carry **15 typings, every one of them `competitor`** — **no partner, no customer, no supplier edge anywhere inside the roster**, where session 15's `cooling` reached 36 of 39 and session 18's three-member roster ran 1 competitor to 2 partner. The structure is in the **absences**: the six pairs with no edge are exactly `{the three engineering firms} × {the two pure certification bodies}`, and the two members joined to all six others are the two holding **both** kinds of authority. So the graph partitions the segment into a report half and a certificate half and names the two companies in both — **the same boundary the league tables draw, from an artefact that does not cite them**, (dd2) across artefact kinds on a seven-member roster. **Read a roster's missing edges as a partition before reading its present ones as density.**
- **(oo3) THE SEGMENT SELLS AUTHORITY IN FOUR SEPARABLE PIECES AND NOBODY HOLDS ALL FOUR — AND THE PARTY ISSUING MOST OF ITS MARKET'S LISTINGS HOLDS THE FEWEST.** Write the standard · run the laboratory · issue the mark · sign the lender's report. One member holds all four; two hold two each and *different* second ones; one holds one; the challenger holds two today and one after its sale. **Five members, four distinct combinations.** The organising fact is disclosed to the dollar: the default certifier owns the **mark** and not the documents — a sister non-profit publishes them, holds about **61.6 per cent of the equity and 94.1 per cent of the votes**, and is paid **USD 22m a year** for library access out of cost of revenue, while the mark goes back royalty-free. **A segment whose product is authority, in which the leading listing business licenses that authority from somebody else, is a structure no ranking would have found.**
- **(oo4) THE BRIEF NAMED TWO CHANGES OF CONTROL AND THE RECORD CARRIED THREE, WITH THE THIRD POINTING THE OTHER WAY.** §7.53 named the challenger selling its testing subsidiary and an incumbent being taken private. The third, found in the re-derivation, is an incumbent **buying** an independent NRTL and its mark for about EUR 575m — **closing expected in the same quarter as the other two**. So one quarter separates a standards author from its laboratories, takes a listed certifier private, and folds an independent laboratory into an incumbent: **two change a role label on this roster and the third changes the map it competes on.** (kk1)/(jj1) say a brief's arithmetic is a claim about the corpus; this is the case where re-deriving it **added** a finding rather than correcting one.
- **(oo5) (nn8) IN A FOURTH SHAPE: BOTH DAY-LEVEL SCANS WERE COMPLETE AND NEITHER COULD SEE THE EVENTS THAT MATTER.** The fence contributes nothing — **40 `policyExposure[]` entries, 17 dated, ZERO in the future**, two members carrying no array at all. The ISO sweep returned **3** distinct future day-level dates in the whole segment and the word-form sweep **1**, which the ISO sweep already had. **Not one of the four is any of the three deals**, because every deal is stated as a **quarter**: a quarter-form scan returns 30 hits over 3 distinct years and is the only form that sees them. Previous firings found the two scans disagreeing, disjoint and jointly empty; **this is the case where both are right, complete, and blind to the subject.** `reviewBy` **2026-10-31**, read off a **month-only** members' vote and taken at month end per (y5); **nine rejections**, including **2026-12-31 rejected because it is already the downstream landscape's own gate** ((r)/(u), a third firing) and the takeover long-stop rejected as an outside date rather than a gate.
- **(oo6) (mm6)/(nn3) FIRED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS ON A SINGLE CHECK, AND THE SECOND HALF WAS THE FIX FOR THE FIRST.** The micro-markup simulation's first pass used a hand-rolled asterisk heuristic and reported **five** failures: four were phantom — it stripped emphasis in a different order than it measured — and one was real. Replacing it with **the renderer's own `clFmt`, transcribed from `Classroom.html`**, was the correct move and introduced a *new* fault: the transcription wrote `\\1` where the replacement needed `\1`, so every bold span's contents were silently discarded and **a literal backtick the discredited heuristic had caught disappeared from the report**. A **four-probe self-test on the transcription** now runs with the simulation. **A wrong path produces a false alarm and a false pass from the same right denominator, and replacing a bad instrument with a better one is exactly when to test the better one.**
- **(oo7) A GENUINE RENDERER CONTRACT FELL OUT OF THAT, AND IT IS A FIELD THAT *IS* SENT TO `clFmt`.** `clFmt`'s bold pattern is `/\*\*([^*]+)\*\*/`, whose `[^*]+` cannot span an inner asterisk — so **italic nested inside bold breaks both**: `**So *UL-listed* names a mark**` renders as `*<em>So </em>UL-listed<em> names a mark</em>*`, two literal asterisks and emphasis on the wrong words. **(mm7) covered the fields that never reach `clFmt`; this is the complementary case — a field that does reach it and still prints literally.** No checker sees either. Simulate the renderer, do not pattern-match its inputs.
- **(oo8) (nn7) AT A SECOND FIRING, AND THE DIVISION OF LABOUR BETWEEN THE TWO PASSES IS NOW LEGIBLE.** Two over-broad claims were replicated. *The largest incumbent*, used four times for a company the section above ranks **eighth of ten**, contradicting its own page. And the module's best fact: *the second source held the scope first*, read off a challenger's NRTL entry listing two standards beside a 2021 renewal date — **false, because the same dossier records OSHA granting a third standard in 2026 and that standard is in the same list**, so the list is the *current* scope and the record gives **no grant date at all** for that member. **The recount caught both primaries; the screenshots then found the second claim still standing in FIVE trailing positions** — two sales lines, a record-gaps item, a quiz option and a closing clause. **The recount finds the assertion and the screenshots find its restatements**, and the corrected version (one party's dates are published and the other's are not) is a better finding than the false one it replaced.
- **(oo9) (kk7) AT A SIXTEENTH SESSION — EIGHTEEN AND EIGHTEEN, AND THE SCREENSHOTS CAUGHT A TABLE CONTRADICTING ITS OWN INTRO.** Seven pre-splice simulations with printed denominators (197 `clFmt` fields, 48 plain-text fields, 101 term uses over 17 distinct, 8 glossary entries, 5 quiz items, ~60,000 ASCII characters, the structural check) all returned 0 failures; the recount then caught **eighteen** and the screenshots **eighteen more**. Beyond (oo8), the screenshots' sharpest was an indicators table whose intro claimed rows ordered by how soon they resolve while a **two-quarter-wide** row sat above a **single-quarter** one — a claim about layout that only layout can falsify — and a US appellate holding stated as being about codes incorporated into *law* when the ruling is specifically about codes incorporated into **Canadian** law. **(nn11) held exactly:** the seven drill cards render question-side only behind CLICK TO FLIP, so they were recount-only.
- **(oo10) A SEED TERM WAS REJECTED ON PUNCTUATION, AND ANOTHER ON (nn9)'s COLLISION IN A SECOND INSTRUMENT.** Against all **532** held terms the assurance vocabulary scored **zero on all twelve terms taken** — the widest gap any S2 seed check has found, because the segment's own nouns had never been seeded. Two rejections generalise. **`owner's engineer`** would have been the **only apostrophe-bearing term in the roster**, and `scTermsHit_` matches with `text.indexOf`: traced on the real path, the ASCII form returns **false** against the typographic apostrophe news text carries, so the term would have scored as if the layer were covered. **`accreditation`** scores zero and is the third buying criterion's own first word, and was rejected because the concepts registry resolves it to **ELCC** — **(nn9)'s collision moved from the tooltip path to the scoring path: the same word would have printed the wrong definition in the module and scored the wrong article in the digest.** And `independent engineering` is taken **alongside** `independent engineer`, which looks like the superstring (x6) forbids and is not: the word-boundary guard makes the shorter form return false on the longer one, **(kk8)'s plural blindness in an `-ing` shape**.


- **What Phase 4 row 24 added (v06.46r)** — the fourth non-S2 contribution to this list, and it is here for the reason rows 19, 20 and 22 were: every finding is about the instruments §10.6 defines rather than about a landscape. **The (oo…) label is spent; the next session continues at (qq1).** **(pp1) THE `(planned)` LEAK IS NOT A PROPERTY OF GATED REGISTRATIONS, AND MEASURING IT CORPUS-WIDE RATHER THAN NAMING SEGMENTS SETTLED IT — (nn2)/(mm8)/(xi) AT THEIR WIDEST.** §7.54 recorded that *one segment leaks as of this commit and row 24 will take it to five*. Both halves of that are wrong, and a single grep over every generated `segment-*` literal for `*<title>* (planned)` where the title is a **registered** lesson says why: the standing leak was already **15 false renders across 6 segments** before this commit and is **25 across 9** after it, over **10 distinct registered titles**. This row contributes **10 renders across 4 segments** for one title — the largest single-title leak the register has seen and **2.5× the next** — because `assurance`, `capital` and `insurance-and-risk-transfer` each carry the lesson in `read-next` **and** in one or two `what-is-bought-and-on-what` rows. The decisive number is the split: **five of the ten leaked titles are gated and five are public.** So the leak is the standing cost of **any** registration that lands after a segment's last regeneration, and the gate has nothing to do with it. **Count the renders, not the segments, and never attribute the leak to the gate.**
- **(pp2) (oo7) FIRED ONCE, AND IT FIRED ON AN EDIT MADE TO FIX SOMETHING ELSE.** The `clFmt` transcription passed its four-probe self-test on the first write this time, so (oo6) did not recur. What did recur is the renderer contract it discovered. Correcting a screenshot finding — an inconsistent sibling reference in a table `note` — meant dropping an italic lesson title into a sentence that was **already bold end to end**, producing `**… belongs to *Listing, Test Method, Code* and is not restated here.**`, which `clFmt`'s `[^*]+` cannot span. The simulation caught it in the same run. **The lesson generalises past this renderer: a correction rewrites a sentence that already carries emphasis, so the edit most likely to break a markup rule is the one made to satisfy a different rule.** Re-run the markup pass after every fix round, not only after the draft.
- **(pp3) A SELF-REFERENTIAL COUNT WAS WRONG TWICE, AND THE SECOND TIME WAS THE CORRECTION — (oo9) ONE LEVEL ALONG.** The `where-it-fails` callout's own `note` claimed a taxonomy of its six items. The first draft said *three contradictions and three wrong-object*; the recount caught it and rewrote it as *two of the same shape and two more wrong-object*; **the screenshots then caught that too**, because the rewrite had been computed from memory of the list rather than by re-walking it. The laid-out page shows the real distribution: **one contradiction between two documents, two wrong-object, one unsupported assumption, one bid structure and one channel fit.** (oo9)'s indicators table contradicted its own intro once; this contradicted its own list, was corrected, and contradicted it again. **A count about the thing you are writing has to be re-derived from the thing, not from the last sentence you wrote about it — a correction is a fresh assertion and inherits none of the first one's checking.**
- **(pp4) (nn1)'s CHEAPER RULE HELD EXACTLY, AND BOTH HALVES COLLAPSED ONTO THE SAME FOUR SEGMENTS.** Derived from the parsed generator before the write: `what-bankable-means` is named by **4 of 19** segments' `READ_NEXT`, and **3 of 27** `CRITERION_LEXICON` entries target it. Running the real `sec_what_is_bought` gate per segment, **all four members also carry a matching criterion** — `assurance` and `insurance-and-risk-transfer` on two criteria each, `capital` and `storage-integrators-and-containers` on one. One member was already due, so the forecast was **6 → 9 due, every new entrant differing in `read-next` AND `what-is-bought-and-on-what`, and the already-due member's detail line unchanged**. The run returned exactly that. **(aa5) now holds for a SEVENTEENTH session**, and (nn1)'s restatement — forecast membership, then ask only whether each member also carries a matching criterion — is now the whole procedure.
- **(pp5) THE DENIAL CHANNEL OF (nn5) IS VISIBLE AS A TRANSITION, NOT A STATE, AND THE TRANSITION IS WHAT REGISTRATION SELLS.** Measured base against head through the real `handleClassroomOp_` with the same analyst token: `cop=lesson&id=what-bankable-means` answers **`UNKNOWN_LESSON` at base and `ROLE_DENIED` at head**. Every previous gated row measured the head state alone and recorded *answers `ROLE_DENIED`*, which reads as a property of the lesson. It is not: it is a **change** an id-probing analyst can watch for, and it is the one signal that tells them a gated lesson was created on a given day. The analyst's own index is **byte-identical at 42,265 characters** across the same commit, so the transition is invisible everywhere the tier is actually looking and visible only where it guesses. **(xvi) stands and is sharper: the asymmetry is not that a gated lesson confirms its existence, it is that registration publishes the moment of its creation.**
- **(pp6) THE `reviewBy` SORT INVERTED (ii7)/(nn8): THE DATE THE ISO SWEEP COULD NOT SEE IS THE ONE THAT GETS REJECTED.** Across all four pinned inputs an ISO day-level sweep returned **one** future date and a word-form sweep **two**. Previous sessions found the word-form sweep surfacing a *better* candidate than the inheritance; here the extra one is **2026-10-01, PRC-029-1's effective date** — squarely the **grid** gauntlet this lesson and row 23 both exclude by name — so the wider sweep's only contribution was **a rejection**. **One rejection in writing.** And the date taken is attested **three independent ways inside one module**: its own `reviewBy`, the UL 9540A 6th edition's effective date, and the day PRC-029-1's phasing reaches non-BES inverter-based resources. **A word-form sweep is worth running even when it cannot change the answer, because a candidate you rejected in writing is evidence and a candidate you never saw is not.**
- **(pp7) §10.6 (t) FIRED IN A FOURTH SHAPE — ONE BOUNDARY DECLARED AND KEPT, ONE NEVER DECLARED AND FOUND ONLY BY GREP.** The declared half is the cleanest instance the register has: `the-certification-stack`'s own header comment reads *'the MONEY gauntlet (the IE report, the counterparty file, the RFP diligence checklist) is curriculum plan 3.5's next row and is not previewed here, not even by title'*, written one day before this row ran, and the check confirms it held — that lesson contains no IE anatomy, no contract skeleton and no checklist. The undeclared half is two tracks away: **`duration-and-degradation`, public, `bess-foundations` position 2, already carries the literal example §3.5 names for this lesson's callout** — *a curve that is optimistic by two points a year moves an augmentation event forward by years* — and says nothing about handing anything on, because it was authored long before this row was scheduled. Found by grepping all 53 built lessons for the subject's own nouns rather than by reading the two named neighbours. **A declared handoff protects the neighbour it names; only a corpus-wide grep protects the ones it does not.** The callout now takes the money half of that sentence and credits the neighbour by title.
- **(pp8) (kk7) AT A SEVENTEENTH SESSION — TWENTY-FOUR AND NINE, AND THE SCREENSHOTS' SHARPEST WAS A COUNT THAT NAMED FIVE OF ITS OWN SIX.** Seven pre-splice simulations with printed denominators (113 `clFmt` fields, 44 plain-text fields, 95 term uses over 48 distinct, 8 glossary entries, 5 quiz items, ~38,800 ASCII characters, the structural check) returned **one** failure — the (pp2) nesting — and the recount then caught **twenty-four** the simulations could not see, of which the sharpest was the opening paragraph claiming the report judges technology, contracts, counterparties, permits **and forecasts** together while the paragraph three below it says the engineer does not opine on price. **The screenshots then caught nine more with near-zero overlap**, and the sharpest of those was a table `note` that promised the six diligence folders were ordered by who asks and then named the asker for **five** of them. **(nn11) held exactly:** the six drill cards render question-side only behind CLICK TO FLIP, so their answers were recount-only.

**Phase 4 row 25 — `reading-the-numbers` (v06.47r, 2026-09-17), the fifty-fifth lesson, the first PUBLIC row since row 19, and the one that completes `market-access` at nine of nine.**

- **(qq1) THE `(planned)` LEAK AND THE `--check` DUE SET ARE THE SAME NINE SEGMENTS, WHICH RETIRES (xi)'s FRAMING AS UNOWNED DEBT.** (pp1) measured the leak corpus-wide at **25 false renders across 9 segments over 10 registered titles**; this row reproduced that exactly and took it to **27 renders across the same 9 segments over 11 titles**, contributing **2** — one in `segment-capital`, one in `segment-storage-developers-and-ipps`. The finding is what fell out of listing the nine: **they are byte-for-byte the nine segments `build-classroom-segments.py --check` reports as due**, and every one of them lists `read-next` among its differing sections. That is not a coincidence, it is the mechanism — a segment leaks `(planned)` precisely because a lesson was registered after its last regeneration, which is the same fact that makes its `read-next` differ. **So the leak is not debt no checker sees; it is the visible face of staleness the checker already reports in full**, and a single regeneration pass over the nine due segments clears all 27 renders. (xi) has been carried for six sessions as unowned; it is owned, by `--check`, and has been all along.
- **(qq2) THE ELIGIBILITY GUARD IN `sec_what_is_bought` IS LOAD-BEARING, AND THE TWO HITS IT SUPPRESSES ARE THE TWO THAT ARE SUBSTANTIVELY RIGHT.** Tracing the whole path rather than counting entries: **1 of 27** `CRITERION_LEXICON` entries targets this lesson, on the keys `kpi` / `reporting` / `disclosure` / `backlog`. Across the **11** buying criteria of the two eligible members the keys hit **zero** times, so the half fires not at all and the forecast held. But the same keys hit **twice corpus-wide** — `epc-and-construction`'s *Bonding capacity, backlog quality and project-controls record (percentage-of-completion)* and `neoclouds`' *Backlog quality and customer concentration behind the lease* — and both are blocked by the generator's `lid in eligible` test, whose comment reads *a bare keyword hit across the whole curriculum is noise*. **Here it is the opposite of noise:** the EPC criterion names two of this lesson's nine section subjects in its own words. Neither segment is in today's due nine, so without the guard the count would have gone **9 → 11** rather than holding. **(cc3) recorded the mapped set and the keyword set being disjoint as a diagnosis of a dash row; this is the same disjunction deciding whether a correct link is ever offered.** The remedy is one line in `READ_NEXT`, not in the lexicon; no data was touched.

Probing every candidate term against the 4,200-key map before drafting found **`book value` resolving to `net asset value`**, defined as what a firm's assets are worth after subtracting what it owes and glossed *for a trading and investing business*. HLBV is **hypothetical liquidation at book value**, where the phrase means the carrying amounts inside one partnership's accounts — a different object, and the tooltip would have taught the wrong one on the lesson's own core term. Written as plain prose instead. **The probe that catches this is reading the definition that comes back, and it has now paid three times; the cheap version — checking whether the term exists — would have passed all three.**
- **(qq4) THE OVERLAY'S OWN RULE FAILS INSIDE THE CORPUS, ON THIS LESSON'S OWN THESIS, AND THE SCHEMA SUPPLIES THE TEST THAT PROVES IT.** `PROFILER-SCHEMA.md` defines `backlog-gwh` as *contracted-but-undelivered storage backlog at period end* and says the prose is the source of truth: *if they ever disagree, `actual` is right and the overlay is the bug.* One profile carries `backlog-gwh: 84.057` on a metric whose own `name` reads **Storage development pipeline**, and the same dossier's stage split for that day names **4,378 MWh of backlog** inside it — so the stored figure is about **nineteen times** the object the key is defined to hold, and **84.7 %** of it is early-stage development on the company's own definition. The FY2025 marker has the same shape at **15.4x**. That holds for the unit and not for the **basis**: one yieldco's `mw-energized` is stored as **1,439** where the prose reads **1,439 MW rated / 849 MW net** — a **41 %** gap between two legitimate readings of one fleet, with no field in which to record which was taken. The proximate cause is a parenthetical in the session-context standings — *G6 (the register row, not the module)* — which inverts the truth: the register row is what closed *because* the module landed. **The cost was almost a whole misdirected session**, and the check that caught it was one grep against `guidanceDocs_()`. **A dependency asserted in a handover is a claim about the repository and has to be re-derived from the repository, exactly like a count** — (kk1)/(jj1) applied to a blocker rather than to arithmetic. **Row 26 is unblocked, it is the last row, and Phase 4 closes on it.** **The (qq...) label is spent; the next session continues at (rr1).**

**Phase 4 row 26 — `interconnection-for-large-loads` (v06.48r, 2026-09-17), the fifty-sixth lesson, the row that completes `aidc-grid-to-chip` at eleven of eleven, and THE ROW THAT CLOSES PHASE 4 AT 26 OF 26.**

- **(rr1) THE BLOCKER (qq9) RETIRED WAS RE-DERIVED IN ONE GREP BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE, AND THE CHECK COST FOUR SECONDS AGAINST A SESSION IT WOULD HAVE WASTED.** The first tool call after the repo-state checks was `grep -n guidanceDocLargeLoadInterconnection_` against `Classroom.gs`, which returned **two hits — the registration inside `guidanceDocs_()` at line 47584 and the definition at line 50151**. That is the whole verification, and it is the shape every future handover-asserted dependency should get: **a blocker is a claim about the repository, so the repository answers it, and the answer is one grep long.** (qq9) named the failure; this row is the first to run the remedy as a first action rather than as a discovery, and the point worth keeping is the **ordering** — the grep ran before the brief was read, so no plan was ever built on the wrong premise and there was nothing to unwind.
- **(rr2) A GATED ROW'S BYTE-IDENTICAL ANALYST PAYLOAD IS ONLY BYTE-IDENTICAL WHEN NOTHING ELSE IN THE TIER'S VIEW MOVED — AND A TRACK-COMPLETING ROW ALWAYS MOVES SOMETHING.** (nn5)/(pp5)/(qq7) established that a gated registration leaves the analyst index unchanged to the byte, and five gated rows had confirmed it. This row is gated and the analyst payload **moved**: **43,071 → 42,708 bytes, exactly -363**. The raw number reads as a gate leak and is not one. Decomposing it: the only two keys that differ on the track object are `short` and `withheld`; the `withheld` integer 1 → 2 is the same width and contributes nothing; and the track `short` shrank **596 → 233 characters, which is -363 exactly**, because completing the track obliged the `"So far:"` enumeration to move to the whole-track form. **Swapping only that string into the base payload reproduces the head payload byte for byte, so the gated registration's own contribution is +0.** The analyst's lesson-card set is unchanged at 48 with nothing added or removed. **The invariant survives; the instrument needed a decomposition to say so** — which is (nn3) in its exact shape: a correct denominator read through the wrong path produces a false ALARM, and the alarm here was a headline byte count on a signal whose whole value is that it does not move.
- **(rr3) THE DEEPENING HALF OF THE `--check` FORECAST CAN FAIL FOR A REASON THE GUARD NEVER GETS TO HAVE.** (qq2) measured the generator's `lid in eligible` guard suppressing two substantively correct links, and row 25's forecast held because the `CRITERION_LEXICON` half fired zero times across eleven criteria that the guard then blocked. This row's half fired zero times for a different reason entirely: **zero of the 27 `CRITERION_LEXICON` entries name `interconnection-for-large-loads` at all**, so there is no keyword to hit and nothing for the guard to suppress. The `READ_NEXT` half is equally thin — **exactly one segment** (`utilities`) names the lesson, and it was already due. **Forecast: hold at 9. Measured: 9.** The distinction matters for the next forecast: *zero hits* and *zero eligible hits* look identical in the count and are different facts, and only the second one is evidence about the guard. **(aa5) holds for a NINETEENTH session.**

- **(rr5) TWO CORPUS SOURCES CHARACTERISE THE SAME APRIL 2026 EVENT DIFFERENTLY, AND NEITHER CITES THE OTHER — RECORDED, NOT FIXED.** `study:talen-energy`'s `what-ferc-actually-decided` says the sequence after the rejection included *further guidance in April 2026*, and `clean-firm-power` inherited that phrasing verbatim into a public lesson. - **(rr6) (kk7) AT A NINETEENTH SESSION — SIX AND SEVEN, AND THE SCREENSHOTS' SHARPEST WAS A SECTION'S HEADLINE CLAIM CONTRADICTED BY ITS OWN THIRD COLUMN.** Eight pre-splice simulations with printed denominators (a four-probe `clFmt` self-test, 150 `clFmt` fields, 63 textContent-only fields, 48 term uses over 22 distinct, 8 glossary entries all used, ~46,800 ASCII characters, the structural check against §3.3, and the quiz answer-length channel at both extremes) returned **zero** failures.
