The built `the-players` tables carry **314 rows across nineteen segment lessons** — the registry grew between S0 and S1's generation and the design note was never re-measured. Deriving it took one command, and the same pass established six other properties the deck depends on and none of which the spec states: every row is 4 columns wide against one identical header set, every segment lesson holds **exactly one** `the-players` section (at index 3 in all nineteen), all 314 dossier slugs match the id grammar, all 314 company cells are `**bold**` (so the emphasis must be stripped, because the drill card reaches the DOM through `textContent` where `**` is a literal), **not one** of the 314 basis lines contains an asterisk, and the 314 ids are **distinct with zero collisions**. Roles decompose 122 + 80 + 112 = 314, so the denominator closes. **(kk1)/(jj1) applied to a spec rather than to a handover** — a number a design note asserts about the corpus is a claim about the corpus, and ages exactly as fast.
- **(rr8) THE TWO ID NAMESPACES ARE SEPARATED BY A PRE-EXISTING REGEX RATHER THAN BY CONVENTION, WHICH MEANT THE NEW PATH HAD TO BE EXPLICIT.** `CL_DRILL_ID_RE` requires a trailing `:<n>` index and an `rc:` id ends in a dossier slug, so **0 of 314** roster ids match it (measured per tier, not reasoned). The consequence is the good one: the pre-K2 `cop=grade` guard refuses every roster id on its own, so the roster branch could not be inherited by accident and had to be written deliberately. The invariant is therefore enforced by code that predates the feature, and the test asserts the disjointness directly so that a future widening of either regex fails here rather than silently letting the mechanism grade path accept roster ids.
- **(rr9) THE GATE-DIGEST DELTA WAS DECOMPOSED BEFORE THE DIGEST WAS REFRESHED — (rr2)'s DISCIPLINE ON A DIFFERENT SIGNAL.** P3 fired as §7.57 forecast. "The gate surface changed" is a true headline that says nothing about **which** gate, and the refresh snippet will happily bless any change at all. Decomposing it per symbol: of the **32** `GATE_SYMBOLS`, **exactly one** moved — `handleClassroomOp_`, 5,294 → 8,601 characters (**+3,307**) — and substituting only that symbol's base text back into the head source reproduces the base digest **byte for byte**. So nothing in the access matrix, the provenance fold, the stamp reader, the per-tier views, the progress store or the two schema-version constants contributed, which is what a new op parameter should cost and is worth knowing **before** acknowledging the change rather than after.
- **(rr10) A BYTE FIGURE CARRIED FORWARD IN A HANDOVER WAS A CHARACTER COUNT, AND ONLY MEASURING BOTH UNITS CATCHES IT.** §7.57 records row 26's PROJECT region as "3,930,651 to 3,979,280 **bytes**". `origin/main`'s region measures **3,979,225 characters** and **3,999,876 bytes** — so the carried figure is within 55 of the *character* count and **20,651 short** of the byte count, because `Classroom.gs` is dense with em-dashes and `›`. A session that measures bytes and compares against the handover's "bytes" sees a spurious +20k and has no way to tell a unit error from real growth. **(pp3) in a new shape: the unit on a number is part of the assertion**, and both units are now stated in §7.58 so the next comparison is like for like.
- **(rr11) `CL_DRILL_ACCOUNT_CAP` IS ALREADY BINDING AND K2 DID NOT MAKE IT SO — THE HEADLINE WAS FALSE AND DECOMPOSING IT SAID WHY.** The cap is **3,000** state rows per account and `CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md` calls it "headroom rather than a limit" against "~2,050 items". Measured: the study and lesson pools total **2,702** and the guidance half adds **437** (275 `gc:` + 162 `gq:`), so a contributor's mechanism deck alone is **3,139 — 139 over the cap before the roster deck exists**. K2's 314 take the gap from 139 to 453. The obvious reading — "opting into the roster deck pushes an account past the cap" — is wrong: the cap was crossed when guidance items joined the pool at C3 session 3, and the schema's headroom note is two generations stale. **Recorded, not fixed**: raising a documented guard is a decision for the developer and does not belong bundled into a feature commit.
- **(rr12) A PRE-EXISTING DEAD-CODE BLOCK IN THE CONTENT CHECKER — THREE AUDIT ASSERTIONS THAT HAVE NEVER RUN.** `run_gate_truth_table()` ends `return (…) + cases`, and **below that return** sit three checks that the `classroom_capability_denied` / `classroom_bad_provenance` / `classroom_not_admitted` denials were audit-logged. They are unreachable and always have been, so the audit-trail half of the gate test is unverified while reading as covered. Pre-existing and outside K2's scope under Chesterton's Fence — enabling them may surface genuine failures and wants its own commit.
- **(rr13) A SINGLE-MUTATION TEST CAN READ AS A HOLE WHEN THE CODE HAS TWO INDEPENDENT GUARDS, AND THE WAY TO TELL IS TO REMOVE BOTH.** Eight mutations were run against the new assertions with the error counts printed: leaking `rc:` into the mechanism pool fired **5**, defaulting the opt-in flag on **6**, resolving the table columns by position instead of by header name **7**, dropping the asterisk strip **2**, hashing `q+a` instead of the basis **1**, removing the deck-state split **2**, and changing the caps **1**. Removing the `segment-` prefix filter fired **zero** — which reads as an untested invariant and is not one: `CL_ROSTER_ID_RE` independently requires the prefix, so either guard alone holds the line. Removing **both** fires **5**. A mutation that survives is evidence of *something*, but redundancy and absence of coverage produce the identical reading, and only the second mutation distinguishes them.
- **(rr14) EVERY ONE OF THE RENDER HARNESS'S FIRST THREE FAILURES WAS THE INSTRUMENT — (oo6)/(rr6) THREE TIMES IN ONE HARNESS.** Playwright could not be installed this session: `pypi.org` timed out on both attempts and `registry.npmjs.org` returned **503**, so the browser route named in §7.57 was unavailable and the page half was measured instead by loading the page's **real** `// PROJECT START … // PROJECT END` region and the `.gs`'s real region into **one** Node process behind a 60-line DOM shim, stubbing the transport at `_gasPost` so the page's own `clApi` still ran. Its first three readings each looked like a page defect and each was a shim gap: **no `nextSibling` / `firstChild`**, so `clRenderRosterCard`'s insert-after-the-drill-card landed at position 0 and the roster card appeared **above** the drill card; **no `#cl-app`**, so the real `clShell()` returned null and the entire drill view rendered as 0 characters; and **a role-less session stub**, so `clAdmitted()` read the analyst as a viewer and `clRoute()` returned early, making the route table look dead. All three were fixed in the shim and **no page code changed**. The lesson that generalises: a substitute instrument built under time pressure fails in its own idiom, and the first three failures of a new harness should be assumed to be the harness until each is traced.
- **(rr15) THE CHANGELOG ROTATION CARRIES THE FILE'S FOOTER INTO THE MIDDLE OF THE ARCHIVE, AND IT HAD ALREADY HAPPENED ONCE UNNOTICED.** *(Written at K2 into `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.58 and, until v06.50r, never into this register — see (rr16).)* The rotated sections are the **oldest**, so the last one's body runs to end-of-file and takes the `Developed by:` line with it, out of the main file and into the archive between two version sections. The dry run caught it for `Classroomgs.changelog.md`. The same seam then explained something standing: **`CHANGELOG-archive.md` line 10993 is a displaced footer, and `CHANGELOG.md` was the only changelog in the repo without one** — an earlier rotation did exactly this and nobody noticed, because the symptom is a missing last line. The footer was restored at v06.49r; the stray inside the archive was left alone, because archived sections move verbatim. **Detach the footer before moving the oldest group, and check the main file's last line afterwards.**
- **What the segment-lesson regeneration pass added (v06.50r)** — the fifth non-S2 contribution to this list, and the first from a run that authored nothing at all: every finding below is about the instruments §10.4 and §10.8 define, measured by a pass whose entire write set is generated literals.
- **(rr16) A FINDING WRITTEN INTO A BRIEF BUT NOT INTO THE REGISTER IS A FINDING THE NEXT SESSION DOES NOT HAVE — (qq9)/(rr1) TURNED ON THE REGISTER ITSELF.** `SESSION-CONTEXT.md` asserts that K2 wrote *"findings (rr7)–(rr15)"*. Eight of the nine landed: this register ended at **(rr14)**, and **(rr15) existed only inside §7.58**, a paste-in brief that is spent the moment its session runs. The brief told this session to read *"§10.6's findings list through (rr15)"* — following that instruction is what surfaced it, because the list stops one short. The cost would have been silent: the next label is (rr16) either way, so the gap closes over itself and the finding is simply lost. **A finding is not recorded until it is in the register the next session reads, and the check is one grep of the label against both files** — the same shape as (qq9)'s blocker and (rr7)'s card count, applied to the bookkeeping that carries every other finding.
- **(rr17) THE BRIEF NAMED THE WRONG NUMBER AS THIS PASS'S TARGET, AND THE HEALTH SCRIPT SAYS SO IN THE TEXT OF ITS OWN OUTPUT LINE.** §7.58 and the paste-in prompt both state that *"the stale-pin count is what this pass exists to move"*, pointing at `check-classroom-curriculum.py --strict`'s **28 stale pins**. It cannot move them: the stale-pin loop `continue`s on every id beginning `segment-` (`scripts/check-classroom-curriculum.py:240`), and the total it prints is labelled **"28 stale pin(s) on the hand-authored lessons"**. Segment-lesson staleness is routed to the generator's `--check` instead, which §10.9 states explicitly. The two numbers are **disjoint by construction** — one is the hand-authored corpus's pin debt, the other is this pass's whole subject — and they sit four lines apart in the same report, which is how they came to be read as one. **Forecast before the run: 28 → 28 and `--check` 9 → 0. Measured after: exactly that.** The pass is also incapable of moving a segment pin: `--check` reported `inputs moved: none` on all nine and the generator logged `no pin moved` nine times.
- **(rr18) `--all` DOES NOT MEAN ALL, AND THE FORECAST HAD TO BE READ OUT OF THE WRITE LOOP'S GUARD RATHER THAN OFF THE FLAG'S NAME.** `--all` marks all nineteen segments `forced`, but the write loop skips `state == "forced" and not secs and not moved`, and **no section builder reads `self.today`** — it is assigned in `Builder.__init__` and never referenced again, so rebuilding a current segment at today's date is identical to rebuilding it at its own `updated`. **Forecast: 9 written, not 19, no track line. Measured: 9 written, no track line.** The corroboration is on the other side: **47 of 56 lessons are byte-identical after the run, ten of them segment lessons** — which is §10.4's determinism claim (*"same inputs → the same bytes"*) measured rather than asserted. The brief's own DO-NOT warned that *"a regeneration writes nineteen `updated` dates"*; it writes **nine**, and `reviewBy` moved on only **four** of those — the four on §10.3's `updated + 6 months` default, while five pinned to a future policy `effectiveDate` correctly held.
- **(rr19) P8 SILENT AND P8 NOT RUN ARE THE SAME OUTPUT, SO THE NAMING HAD TO BE RE-DERIVED OUTSIDE THE CHECKER — (ll1) ON A CHECK RATHER THAN ON A PROBE.** §7.58 said to *expect* P8 and to **read** it rather than assume it. Read: **P8 does not appear in the report at all.** It is an error-only check — it fires when a section is rewritten without being named in `revisions[].changed[]` and prints nothing when the naming is right — so its silence carries no denominator and is indistinguishable from a check that never executed. Computing the comparison independently (parsing base and head literals, diffing sections per lesson, and comparing the result against `revisions[-1].changed[]`) returns **9 of 9 matching**: `read-next` in all nine, `what-is-bought-and-on-what` in six. That is the positive evidence the silent check cannot give, and it is what licenses the claim that the generator's `changed[]` is the differs set.
- **(rr20) THE ACCEPTANCE TEST THE BRIEF SPECIFIED HAS A HOLE THAT ONLY THE PAIRING CLOSES, AND IT IS THE EXACT CASE THE BRIEF WAS REACHING FOR.** §7.58 asks for the roster deck's **id set** and its **hash multiset** compared separately, reasoning that *"a swapped pair of rows keeps the count and moves two hashes"*. True — but two cards that **exchange** hashes with each other keep the count, the id set **and** the multiset identical, and are invisible to all three. Only the **id→hash pairing** catches it. Measured base against head over the real `handleClassroomOp_`: **314 → 314 cards, id set identical, hash multiset identical, and every one of the 314 id→hash pairs identical — 0 of 314 cards moved**, which is what `the-players` being absent from every differing-section list predicts. The same dump gives a second reading of (rr7) for free: the 314 hashes are **314 distinct values**, so the zero-collision property holds at head and nothing in code asserts it.
- **(rr21) STATING BOTH UNITS TURNED AN UNEXPLAINED +69 INTO A BOUNDARY CONVENTION IN ONE STEP — (rr10) PAYING OFF ONE SESSION LATER, AND THE FIRST ARITHMETIC OFF IT WAS STILL WRONG.** §7.58 records the v06.49r PROJECT region as **3,992,980 characters / 4,013,693 bytes**, both units, because (rr10) required it. The harness extracts the region **inclusive** of its two markers and read 3,993,049 / 4,013,764 — apparently +69 / +71. Measuring the boundary instead of reasoning about it: the marker lines are **+70 characters / +72 bytes** (`// PROJECT START — …` 54 + `// PROJECT END` 14 + two newlines; the em-dash is the only non-ASCII), so the first arithmetic — done in the head, not in a command — was itself off by one. Exclusive of markers the base region reads **3,992,979 / 4,013,692**, leaving a residual of exactly **1 character / 1 byte** against §7.58: one newline of boundary convention, and **no growth at all**. Had only one unit been carried, the same reading would have been indistinguishable from a 20,651-byte regression. After the run the region reads **3,994,244 / 4,014,957 — +1,265 in BOTH units**, and the equality is itself the finding: the addition is pure ASCII, which is exactly what nine `revisions[]` entries of dates, section ids and one English sentence should cost, and what a rewritten prose section would not be.
- **(rr22) (rr15) IS NOT ABOUT CHANGELOG ROTATION — IT IS ABOUT ANY EDIT THAT MOVES OR APPENDS PAST AN END-OF-FILE FOOTER, AND THERE ARE NOW THREE INSTANCES IN TWO DIRECTIONS.** (rr15) was written as a rotation hazard: the oldest section's body runs to end-of-file and carries `Developed by:` into the archive with it. The generalisation showed up twice in the same hour of this session, neither of them a changelog rotation. **(1)** This session's own `SESSION-CONTEXT.md` two-session cap **dropped** the footer — the capped Previous-Sessions block ran to end-of-file and the footer went with the text that was discarded, the mirror image of the archive case. It was caught by exactly the check (rr15) prescribes (*"check the main file's last line afterwards"*) and restored in the same commit, which is the rule paying for itself within one session of being written. **(2)** `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` carries a **stranded footer at line 1927** with **none at end-of-file** — displaced not by a rotation but by a §7.x brief **appended past it**, and it has been that way across many sessions because the symptom is invisible: the file reads fine and only its last line is wrong. So the hazard is neither rotation-specific nor removal-specific; it is that **`Developed by:` is positional, every large-file edit that touches the tail can move it, and nothing checks it.** **The check is one line — `tail -1` on every file the commit touched — and this session ran it across all six and found both defects.** The plan file's stray is **recorded and NOT moved**: it is pre-existing, it predates this session, and whether a 2,900-line planning document should carry a trailing footer at all is a convention question for the developer rather than a repair to bundle into a maintenance commit (Chesterton's Fence). It is carried into §7.59's row as a fifth item.
- **(rr23) THE UNIT OF C5 IS NOT "PER SEGMENT", AND THE SESSION COUNT ONLY HOLDS ONCE THE UNIT IS RIGHT.** `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.3 order 9 reads *"scenario modules per segment"*. Measured against `profiler-segments.json` on 2026-09-18: **nine** of the nineteen segments are buyers for the two seats of §10.10 (developers and IPPs, landlords, EPC, utilities, hyperscalers, neoclouds, capital, assurance, insurance); the other ten are supply-side and neither seat sells *to* them. The unit is **buyer segment × seat × mode**, the inventory is fourteen, and "~5 authoring sessions" holds only at two to three scenarios per session with one of the five carrying the machinery — `C5-SALES-SIMULATIONS-DESIGN.md` §3 D10–D11, §9, §13. *(Design session, Fable 5.1 xhigh, v06.51r.)*
- **(rr24) A PLAN'S PREMISE FOR A GATE INVERTED UNDER MEASUREMENT, AND ITS COUNTS WERE STALE BY MORE THAN DOUBLE.** `IMPROVEMENT-PLAN.md` §5 held C5 on *"a scenario built from a report is admin-only, which is most of them."* Measured: every fact layer C5 draws on is public (147 dossiers with `policyExposure[]` and 596 entries; 1,362 relationships, 62 pinned to nine projects; 3,267 developments) and the only judgment layer a scenario needs is the contributor-tier landscape — so **none** need be admin-only, and the design makes the landscape a required input precisely so the fold lands at `guidance`. The same paragraph's figures — 60 dossiers with policy exposure, 497 relationships, eight projects — are what the corpus held when it was written. (kk1)/(jj1) applied to a plan's premise: a number a plan asserts about the corpus ages exactly as fast as one a handover carries.
- **(rr25) TWO DOCUMENTS DISAGREED ABOUT WHAT A SHAPE CHANGE IS, AND THE HIDDEN COST DECIDED IT.** `IMPROVEMENT-PLAN.md` §5 assumed a new lesson `type` forces a `CL_LESSON_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump and therefore a load-time adapter; `CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md` "Extending the schema" rule 2 says a bump is for a **shape** change and additive optional fields need none. What settled it is `CLASSROOM-COMMITTER-CONTRACT.md` §5.1 step 4: a schema-version mismatch is a `BLOCKED` pre-flight, so a bump would have stopped **every** unattended run from the moment it landed until the contract was rewritten — for a change that adds no shape. Decided additive, no bump (design D2).
- **(rr26) `clStudyNext_` WALKS TRACKS ONLY, AND TWO DEVELOPER FILES DESCRIBE IT AS WALKING THE REGISTRY FIRST.** The function iterates `clTracks_()` and each track's `lessons[]`; it never reads `clLessons_()` order. `CLASSROOM-COMMITTER-CONTRACT.md` §4.4 and the P5 row of `.claude/rules/classroom-app.md` both say it *"walks registry order then track order"*. For C5 the drift is benign — a scenario registered in no track is unreachable by study-next with no code change, which is what design D8 wants — and it is recorded for the developer rather than fixed, because the contract is the developer's file (design §12 item 7).
- **(rr27) THE PAGE ALREADY PARTITIONS CARDS BY `type`, WHICH IS THE CONCRETE REASON THE DISCRIMINATOR IS A TYPE AND NOT AN ID PREFIX.** `clRenderIndex` splits the index payload into briefings (a dated feed) and *loose* modules by lane, on `card.type`. A `type: scenario` card with no third partition lands among the loose modules; a `scenario-` prefix on `type: module` would have needed the same page edit **plus** a string test in the track checker, the drill enumerator and the index — three places keyed on a convention instead of one field. The segment layer's prefix-only choice (§10.3) was right for a lesson that *is* a module; a scenario is not one (design D1).
- **(rr28) PROJECT-PINNED RELATIONSHIPS CONCENTRATE ON THREE PROJECTS, WHICH BOUNDS WHERE AN AIDC-SEAT ROOM CAN BE SET.** Of 62 relationships carrying `project`, **45** sit on Stargate (24), Lighthouse (11) and Hyperion (10); River Bend has 6, Colossus 4, Project Jupiter 3, Trimount 2, Homer City and Frontier 1 each. A scenario set at a named project has material only at the top four, and Stargate's twenty-four parties make it a report's subject rather than a rehearsal's. The inventory uses Lighthouse (Vantage · Turner) and River Bend (Hut 8 · Google) from two sides each and sets nothing at Stargate itself (design §9).
- **(rr29) THE SEAT SPLIT IS ALREADY IN THE LANDSCAPES, BUT IN SIX OF NINETEEN, SO A SCENARIO'S SEAT IS CHOSEN, NOT READ.** `the-sellers-play` names the storage seller in **8** of the nineteen modules and the AI-data-centre power seller in **8**, both in **6** (integrators, cells, developers and IPPs, utilities, capital, EPC); nine name neither by those words. So `scenario.seat` cannot be derived from the landscape mechanically; the authoring session chooses it and reads the play through it, and where a module addresses one seat only the other seat's scenario leans harder on the mechanism lessons — the case for `landscape-aidc-developers-and-landlords-2026-09`, which names the storage seller and not the AIDC seat, and is the segment the inventory gives two AIDC-seat scenarios. Measured on the day; **corrected at (rr33) — the AIDC module names both seats.**

- **(rr30) THE DESIGN SAID "NOTHING ELSE ON THE SERVER CHANGES" AND ITS OWN LIBRARY SPEC REQUIRED ONE MORE THING.** C5 design §7 states the server change is a single line in `clDrillLessonItems_`, then specifies a Rehearsal library "grouped **seat → segment**, each card showing mode, counterparty, stage" — and `clLessonCard_` emits none of those. The library reads the same `cop=index` payload the index already fetches, so the block has to be on the card or the grouping cannot exist. C5 session 1 added `scenario` to `clLessonCard_`, which **is** a `GATE_SYMBOLS` member, so **P3 fired where the brief forecast it would not**; decomposed per (rr9), exactly **1 of 32** symbols moved and the digest was refreshed in the same commit (`sha256:f568052fa2fb…` → `sha256:3d09700026d2…`). **The field is emitted CONDITIONALLY** (`if (lesson.scenario)`) rather than as a `null` on every card, and that is load-bearing: an unconditional key would have moved the bytes of every card at every tier and broken the "analyst index byte-identical base → head" invariant that a gated registration exists to satisfy. Measured after: the analyst payload **is** byte-identical, and the admin/contributor delta decomposes to exactly the two new cards and nothing else.
- **(rr31) A BRIEF CAN FORECAST "NO P2" WHILE INSTRUCTING AN EDIT THAT FIRES P2 BY CONSTRUCTION.** §7.60 said "**P2, P3 and P8 are not expected**" and, in the same paragraph, instructed the one-line scenario exclusion in `clDrillLessonItems_` — which sits **below `// CONTENT END`**. P2 asserts that a diff to `Classroom.gs` lies inside the fence plus the `VERSION` line, so *any* session that edits a server function fires it. The measured signature for a session that touches the server is therefore **P1 · P2 · P3 · P10 · P13**, not P1 · P12 · P13. None of the five is a defect; the forecast was. Read a forecast's assertion list against what the same brief asks you to edit before you treat a firing as a surprise.
- **(rr32) `note` IS A SECTION FIELD, AND A `note` INSIDE `items[]` REACHES NO READER.** All six beats of C5 session 1's two scenarios were first written with the conversation-carrying `note` inside `items[0]`, where the schema allows it, `node --check` passes it, the content checker passed it and **nothing renders it** — `clRenderSection` prints `sec.note` and only `sec.note`. It was invisible in the JSON, in every checker and in the diff, and visible in the first screenshot of a beat. Fixed on all six, and turned into two assertions (a missing section-level `note`, and a `note` present inside `items[0]`) so the next four sessions cannot repeat it. **The general form: a field the schema permits everywhere is not a field the renderer reads anywhere** — (rr6)'s rule, now with a named field attached.
- **(rr33) (rr29)'s PARENTHETICAL IS WRONG ABOUT THE ONE MODULE C5 SESSION 1 NEEDED IT FOR.** (rr29) records that `landscape-aidc-developers-and-landlords-2026-09` "names the storage seller and **not** the AIDC seat", and concludes that that segment's AIDC-seat scenarios must lean harder on the mechanism lessons. Measured on the day, its `the-sellers-play` carries **two labelled halves** — "**For the AIDC-power seller.**" with three numbered moves, and "**For the BESS seller.**" with three sockets. The seat-count finding (8 · 8 · both in 6) stands; the parenthetical does not, and the AIDC-seat scenario was written straight off the module's own play. **(rr24) applied to a finding rather than to a plan: a register entry's premise about the corpus is a claim about the corpus, and the next session re-measures it.**
- **(rr34) A REPORT NUMBER THAT LOOKS LIKE THE DRILL POOL SILENTLY STOPPED BEING IT.** `check-classroom-curriculum.py`'s section 4 counts `lq:` items off every registered lesson's `quiz` sections, while the server's `clDrillLessonItems_` now skips `type: scenario` — so the moment two scenarios landed, the health report read **2,708 drillable** against a server pool still measuring **970 / 446**, with no warning and no finding. Caught by comparing the report against the tier test rather than by reading either alone. Fixed: the loop excludes scenarios and prints their beats on a line of their own, labelled not drillable. **This is (rr17)'s shape one section down** — the defect was never the label, it was that two numbers which cannot be added sat where a reader would add them.
- **(rr35) THE DESIGN'S "FOUR-WAY GC BENCH" IS THREE ENTITIES ON FOUR BUILDINGS, AND THE DOSSIER SAYS SO IN THE SENTENCE THAT LOOKS LIKE IT SAYS OTHERWISE.** §9 row 2 and `profiler-projects.json`'s Lighthouse note both describe a "four-way GC bench". `vantage` v9's `relationships[]` entry for the joint-venture builder states it explicitly: the JV builds **two** of the four buildings and two further firms build **one each** — "**three GC entities, not a four-way split**" — and a fifth firm that trade coverage lists alongside them is a subcontractor. The scenario was written to the dossier, and beat 3's strong move rests on the correction (a qualification won at one builder is worth one building of four). **A premise column is a reading, not a pin** — §9 says so, and this is the first row where re-reading changed the room.
- **(rr36) TWO HARNESS STUBS PRODUCED UNIFORM, PLAUSIBLE, WRONG ZEROS IN ONE SESSION.** The tier harness keyed its session stub on the **padded** token, so every tier resolved to the default role and every index came back empty with every `cop=lesson` `ROLE_DENIED` — including admin, which is the tell. The render harness stubbed `_gasPost` with a **callback** where the real transport returns a **Promise**, and also left `loadSession()` unstubbed, so every route rendered zero sections and looked like a broken renderer. Neither threw. Both were caught only because the harnesses print `clLessons_().length` **before** anything else and the number was right while everything downstream was zero. **(ll1) is not one check at the start of a harness — it is the discipline of making the instrument state a number you can independently verify, and then disbelieving every other number until that one is right.** P10's module counter has the same shape and is recorded rather than fixed: it counts a new scenario as a "new module" against a cap of 1, which is unreachable in a real pipeline run because P13 refuses the write first.

- **(rr37) A BRIEF CAN FORECAST A COUNT THAT THE SAME BRIEF'S OWN INSTRUCTION MOVES.** §7.61's VERIFY line says `--strict` should read "**28 stale pins and 4 review items** unchanged", and two paragraphs earlier the same brief instructs a `scenario-utilities-objection` whose `reviewBy` must be **on or before 2026-10-01** — a date **inside** the health script's own thirty-day horizon. Measured after authoring: **5 items due**, the fifth being that scenario. Nothing is wrong: design §6 predicts it in terms ("ships inside its own 30-day horizon and renders a gold chip on day one — the rule working"), the checker's own `reviewBy ≤ the landscape's` warning is silent because 2026-10-01 **equals** the landscape's, and the stale-pin count is genuinely unchanged at 28 while its denominator moves 39 → 42 hand-authored lessons. **This is (rr31)'s shape on a different surface** — there the forecast was an assertion list contradicted by the edit the brief mandated; here it is a count contradicted by the content the brief mandated. Read a forecast's numbers against what the same brief asks you to author before you treat a movement as a defect.
- **(rr38) DESIGN §9 ROW 4's "IRP → CPCN → RFP" CONFLATES TWO PROCUREMENT LANES, AND THE SCENARIO IS ABOUT THE DIFFERENCE.** Measured against `dominion-energy` v1: for **storage purchases** the sequence is plan → annual solicitation → petition for approval → commission order → contract (the October 2025 clean-energy petition asked for two storage projects totalling ~155 MW and was allowed **one of ~80 MW**, in service 2028–2030). The **certificate of public convenience and necessity** is the *build-and-own* branch — the 944 MW simple-cycle station certified in the same November 2025 order — and no storage purchase passes through it. Written as the design has it, the premise makes the two lanes one; written off the record, the two lanes are the whole of beat 1, whose strong move is to ask **which lane this conversation is about**. (rr35) applied to a second row, and the third time a §9 premise has needed re-deriving.
- **(rr39) "FOUR PARTIES" IS FOUR ACTORS AND THREE SIGNATURES, AND THE PROJECT SLUG IS NOT THE ONE THE BRIEF NAMES.** Design §9 row 5 and §7.61 both describe River Bend as "landlord, tenant of record, credit support and end user as four parties". `hut-8` v2's own strategy read calls it **"a three-party arrangement that reporting routinely collapses into two"** and states that the end user **"is not a party to the lease at all"**. Both are true and they are not the same claim: the room has four actors, the lease has three signatures, and the fourth actor is the one with the operating requirement and no standing. Beat 3's fourth option turns on exactly that (calling the party that backstops the rent confuses the credit with the buyer). Separately, §7.61 names the pin `project:river-bend`; the registry slug is **`river-bend-campus`**, and `profiler-projects.json` carries a note warning that the campus is not the nuclear station of the same name in the same parish. **A brief's ref is a reading too.**
- **(rr40) THE FOUR-TILE RULE PRINTS THE SAME WORD TWICE ON A DISCOVERY SCENARIO AT THE DISCOVERY STAGE.** Design §4 fixes tile 3 as `"Objection" | "Discovery"` (the exercise) and tile 4 as the capitalised `stage`. For rows 3 and 5 — both `mode: discovery` at `stage: discovery` — the card therefore reads **Discovery · Discovery**, and the checker passes it because it asserts only the four `v` values in order. Four of the nine remaining scenarios (§9 rows 3, 5, 11, 14) have that shape. Recorded rather than fixed: the tile order is the design's and the `stage` vocabulary is an open developer call (§12 item 5), so a session that changed either on its own would be amending a design it was told to author against. **The card is correct and reads as though it repeated itself** — which is a design question, not an authoring defect.
- **(rr41) DESIGN §9 ROW 6's "BEFORE AN RFP EXISTS" IS FALSE IN BOTH HALVES, AND THE ROOM IS THE LANE COUNT.** Measured against `southern-company` v2: two solicitations are live — a **500 MW** storage solicitation the annual report states was initiated **11 February 2026** with an independent evaluator, and the next all-source request of **2,000–6,000 MW for 2032–2033** opened in June 2026 with certification expected mid-2027. So there is no *before an RFP exists*. What is true and sharper is that this buyer runs **three** lanes for the same equipment — owned-and-operated under its own supply agreements with named engineering contractors, purchased power where the counterparty picks the hardware, and the solicitation a developer bids — and that the owned lane is larger than the solicited one by an order of magnitude (**3,022.5 MW** of owned storage certified in one December 2025 order against 500 MW solicited). Its own strategy read adds the part that makes the room: the purchasing decisions were taken a year or more before the certificate, so **the docket is a lagging indicator**. Beat 1's strong move is therefore *which lane*, not *when the solicitation comes* — (rr38)'s shape on a buyer with three lanes rather than two, and the fourth §9 premise in four to need re-deriving.
- **(rr42) DESIGN §9 ROW 7 NAMES A MECHANISM THE RECORD DOES NOT CARRY, AND THE RECORD CARRIES THE COUNTER-EXAMPLE INSTEAD.** The premise proposes "a qualified-vendor set the hyperscaler's own infrastructure organisation controls". **No qualified-vendor list appears anywhere in `meta` v9** — not in `productsAndServices`, not in `strategyRead`, not in any relationship note — so it could not have been ledgered at all, which is a worse failure than a stale premise: a stale premise is a wrong date, an absent mechanism is an invented fact. What the file *does* carry, dated, is the exception to the objection it also supports: on **8 December 2025** the buyer signed about 2.5 GW across eleven power purchase agreements **plus two energy-storage agreements contracted directly — 168 MW, in service 2026–2028**. So *we buy storage through the utility* is an accurate description of the pattern with a counter-example in the same announcement, and beat 1's strong move is to name it. **Check that a premise has an address before you check that it has a date.**
- **(rr43) DESIGN §9 ROW 8's "GUARANTEED MAXIMUM PRICE" AND "THE CODES" ARE BOTH ABSENT, AND ONE OF THEM IS ABSENT BY CONSTRUCTION.** `turner-construction` v8 contains no occurrence of a guaranteed maximum price; its delivery model is construction-management, general-contracting and design-build agreements, frequently in joint ventures, with **open-book** manufacturer sourcing through a wholly owned procurement subsidiary engaged at **preconstruction**. And "the codes" has no address here at all, because **this dossier is the only one of the three with no `policyExposure[]` array** — a builder's regulatory exposure is its clients', so the codes enter a scenario only as the mechanism lesson's test and never as a fact about this counterparty. Re-derived, the objection is *open books plus a reserved slot*, which changes the answer completely: against a guaranteed maximum price a seller negotiates price, and against open books a price concession moves a number the buyer can already see. **Three of three §9 premises re-derived in one session; six of the last six rows overall. The premise column should be read as a hypothesis with a prior below one half.**
- **(rr44) THE RENDER HARNESS'S FOURTH TRAP: `Storage` IS A LEGACY PLATFORM OBJECT, SO THE SEED MUST PATCH THE PROTOTYPE.** Session 2 recorded that seeding the session **after** load renders the lesson but leaves `#cl-app` at `display:none`. Seeding inside `add_init_script` is necessary and **not sufficient**: `Object.defineProperty(sessionStorage, 'getItem', …)` on the *instance* accomplishes nothing, because `Storage` has named-property semantics and the definition is absorbed as a stored item called `getItem`. `loadSession()` then still reads null, `clRole()` returns `viewer`, and `clAppMount()` returns early inside the masthead wrapper's `try/catch` — so **`showApp()` reports no error and the app simply stays hidden**. Patch `Storage.prototype.getItem` instead. The diagnostic shape is (ll1)'s: an instrument that fails silently and plausibly, caught only by making it state a number — here `loadSession()`'s own return value — that can be checked independently.
- **(rr45) A `reviewBy` CAN LEGITIMATELY BE THE LANDSCAPE'S BOUND RATHER THAN A GATE IN THE LEDGER, AND ALL THREE ROWS LANDED THERE BY DIFFERENT ROUTES.** Design §6 defines `reviewBy` as the nearest dated gate among the ledgered claims, never later than the landscape's own. Row 6's nearest gate is an Alabama statute effective **2026-10-01**, which **equals** `landscape-utilities-2026-09`'s own review date. Row 7's segment carries **no future day-level date at all** — the hyperscalers landscape's indicator table says a scan of all eight dossiers found exactly one, and it belongs to a statute about model disclosure — so the bound (**2026-12-31**) is the answer. Row 8's ledger holds one genuine future dated gate, a placed-in-service tax cliff on **2027-12-31**, which is **later** than `landscape-epc-and-construction-2026-09`'s **2027-03-16** — and that landscape records having rejected the same date as its own review date for the same reason. Three scenarios, three routes, one rule. Where the bound governs, **the reason belongs in the ledger as an analysis row** rather than in the session's head, which is what row 8's last ledger row now does.

- **(rr46) DESIGN §9 ROW 9's THREE NAMED QUESTIONS HAVE NO ADDRESS IN THE COUNTERPARTY'S DOSSIER AT ALL, AND THE ROOM THE RECORD SUPPORTS IS SHARPER THAN THE ONE PROPOSED.** The premise for `scenario-capital-objection` reads *"warranty backing, the IE report and the counterparty's credit are the questions"*. Measured against `brookfield` v2 on 2026-09-18: `independent engineer` **0**, `warranty` **0**, `approved vendor` **0**, `conditions precedent` **0**, `diligence` **0**, `tax equity` **0**, `bankab*` **0**. Not stale — **absent**, exactly as (rr42) was for row 7, and this is the **seventh of seven** §9 premises to need re-deriving. The mechanism is real but it lives in the *landscape* as analysis (*the approved-vendor file, the lender's conditions precedent, and the definition of what the asset is worth on exit*) and in the bankability lesson as teaching — never in the counterparty's own record as fact. What the record does carry is better: the word **battery appears exactly once in the whole dossier**, as an asset class inside a strategy description; the firm's own framing is that vendors reach it through its portfolio companies; and the platform it is buying is **signed and not closed**. The room is therefore the **seat** — owner, lender, or neither yet — rather than the equipment, and every premise in it has an address.
- **(rr47) AN ABSENT MECHANISM AND A POSITIVELY-RECORDED ABSENCE ARE DIFFERENT FINDINGS, AND THE SECOND IS WORTH MORE THAN THE PREMISE IT REPLACES.** Design §9 row 10 proposes that the independent engineer asks for *"test reports with the edition named, degradation curves, the large-scale test procedure, warranty terms read as claims"*. In `sargent-lundy` v2: `degradation` **0**, `edition` **0**, `test report` **0**. But the dossier does not merely lack them — it **records the absence as a finding about the firm**: *the firm names neither NFPA 855 nor UL 9540A anywhere on its channels*, its formulation being that fire protection must be verified against local and national standards, with its own reading of that silence rated **low confidence** in its own `strategyRead`. So the certification conversation a seller rehearses is one this counterparty has never had in public — which is a **better room than the premise asked for**, and the fourth section of `the-position` is built on it. What the record does publish is the firm's own **four review pillars** (use case and revenue, equipment and design, commercial agreements, and construction, commissioning and operating plans) reviewed as *an integrated full scope*, with the warranty warning conditioned on **depth of discharge, cycles and temperature**. (rr42) was an absence you have to route around; this is an absence you can teach from.
- **(rr48) A PREMISE CAN BE RIGHT ABOUT THE SHAPE AND WRONG ABOUT THE TENSE, AND THE SAME DOSSIER CARRIED BOTH ANSWERS.** Design §9 row 11 opens *"leased capacity rather than built"*. True of `fluidstack` v2's five leased campuses, and no longer true of the company: in **July 2026** it bought the **50.1%** of the Abernathy joint venture it did not hold for about **USD 530 million** in three instalments — its first owned campus — and at Okmulgee its own project site states it is **paying for all of the energy and energy infrastructure**, while at River Bend the **landlord pays for the generators and the substation**. Two opposite answers to *who buys the power equipment*, both first-party or landlord-filed, **differing by site rather than by company** — and the segment landscape independently names this member among *the three that do not buy*. There is no account-level answer, so the room is the split, and beat 1 asks for it. The lesson generalises: when a premise describes a company's model, check whether the model changed since the sentence was written, not only whether the facts under it are current.
- **(rr49) (rr37) HAS A SECOND FORM — A BRIEF CAN FORECAST A STATE OF THE WORLD THE CALENDAR HAS NOT REACHED, AND DICTATE A SENTENCE THAT WOULD BE FALSE.** §7.63 states that `landscape-neoclouds-2026-09`'s `reviewBy` of **2026-09-30** *"is probably past when you run"* and instructs the session to **say in the CHANGELOG that the module is overdue**. The session ran on **2026-09-18** — twelve days *before* that date — so the module is **due, not overdue**, and writing the dictated sentence would have put a false statement into a public-facing arithmetic note. The brief was written the same day it was read; the forecast was about a session it expected to run later. (rr37) warned that a brief can forecast a number its own instruction moves; this is the same failure applied to a **date**. **Re-derive the day, then write what is true rather than what the brief predicted** — and a brief that dictates a sentence should mark it conditional on the day.
- **(rr50) A FOURTH `reviewBy` ROUTE APPEARED, AND FOR THE FIRST TIME IN C5 A SCENARIO'S OWN LEDGER GOVERNED ITS REVIEW DATE.** (rr45) recorded three routes, all landing on the landscape's bound. This session's three landed differently. **Row 9 is the new one:** its nearest future day-level gate — the **14 October 2026** affiliate special meetings, at which the entity names and tickers behind the transaction change — falls *inside* `landscape-capital-2026-09`'s bound of **2026-11-06**, so the ledger governs and the bound merely bounds. **Row 10 repeated (rr45)'s second route:** `sargent-lundy` v2 carries **no future day-level date anywhere** — one dated policy entry, already in effect from May 2024; all thirty development entries in the past; its own indicator list year- and month-level — so the bound (**2026-10-31**) is the answer, and the reason is an analysis row. **Row 11 repeated the first route with a twist worth recording:** the nearest gate and the bound are **the same date read off the same document** — the counterparty's statutory accounts deadline of **30 September 2026** is both the only scheduled disclosure in its file and, in the segment module's own indicator table, that module's review date. Four routes now; record which one each row took, because a later session cannot reconstruct it.

### 10.7 · K1 — the study-pool fix (Fable 5.1 High; independent of S1)

**The defect** (§8 item 5, found at Phase 3): `clDrillStudyItems_()` in `Classroom.gs` enumerates a guide's **top-level `flashcards[]` only** (`sf:<slug>:<n>`), and every guide authored from 2026-09 — 95 guides, 1,154 cards — carries its cards inside a section of kind `flashcards` instead, so the drill's study pool is 766 items against a corpus of ~1,920. **The decision (S0): fix the pool builder, not the guides.** Lifting the cards to the top level would touch 95 public files, move every `study:` pin the built lessons carry (26 inputs already moved; this would move them all again) and shift every `sf:` id's positional meaning; reading the sections touches one function and no content.

- **Item identity.** A new kind in `CLASSROOM-SCHEMA.md` → "Drill items and history": `ss:<slug>:<sectionId>:<n>` — a study guide's `flashcards`-kind section, `cards[n]`. Positional like the others; the section id is permanent per the study schema, so the id is stable; the content hash absorbs a mid-array insert exactly as it does for `sf:` and `lc:`. `sf:` ids are untouched, so no account's history moves.
- **Server.** `clDrillStudyItems_()` walks each fetched guide's top-level `flashcards[]` **and** every `sections[]` entry whose `kind` is `flashcards`, emitting ids and hashes only, cached six hours as today. **`CL_DRILL_INV_CAP` 1200 → 2400** (1,920 items today; the cap stays a guard). Cache size: ~1,920 × ~30 bytes ≈ 60 KB, under the 100 KB per-value limit of `CacheService`; if the corpus takes it past ~90 KB, split the cached value by slug initial before raising anything else. `cop=grade` validates an `ss:` id by the same containment rule as `sf:` (public registry, then the guide). No gate symbol is touched — none of the drill functions is in `GATE_SYMBOLS` — so `gateDigest` is unchanged; confirm with `check-classroom-pipeline.py --base origin/main` and refresh only if P3 reports.
- **Client.** `Classroom.html` resolves an `ss:` id from the fetched guide's sections (it already fetches the guide for `sf:`); the source link points at the section. Page bump.
- **Verification.** Both Classroom checkers; `node --check`; Playwright read of the drill landing card showing the pool ≈ 1,920 and an `ss:` card rendering with its answer; the schema's identity table updated in the same commit. GAS + page bump; public changelog line generic.

**Landed — v05.10r (2026-09-07, Fable 5.1 High).** As specified, with one deviation the measurement forced: the cached value is the compact `{ id: hash }` map (~61 KB at 1,920 items), not the `{ kind, slug, hash }` shape the callers use — that shape measured ~146 KB against the live tree, past the 100 KB `CacheService` cap, so it would have fallen through to an uncached rebuild on every request. `clDrillStudyExpand_()` restores the full shape on read; the cache key moved to `cl_drillstudy_v2`. A `CL_DRILL_SECTION_ID_RE` guard keeps a section id the grade regex would reject out of the pool. Pool verified at **1,920 = 766 `sf:` + 1,154 `ss:`** by running the real builder under Node shims; Playwright read the landing card at 1,920 and an `ss:` card with its answer. `gateDigest` unchanged (no P3). Classroom GAS v01.17g · page v01.08w.

> **BUILT 2026-09-18 at v06.49r in ONE session** (Classroom GAS v01.73g, page v01.15w). Everything specified below landed as written, with three deviations recorded rather than taken silently:
>
> - **The card count is 314, not 283.** The figure below was written against the S0 registry's memberships; the built `the-players` tables carry **314 rows across the nineteen segment lessons**, re-derived in one pass at the top of the K2 session. That pass also established that every row is 4 columns wide, every segment lesson holds exactly **one** `the-players` section (at index 3), all 314 dossier slugs are valid, all 314 company cells are `**bold**`, **no basis line contains an asterisk**, the 314 `rc:` ids are **distinct with zero collisions**, and the roles decompose 122 incumbent / 80 challenger / 112 adjacent. **(rr7)** — (kk1)/(jj1) applied to a spec's own arithmetic.
> - **The item id keys on the LESSON id, so it reads `rc:segment-cells-and-chemistry:byd`.** §10.8 writes `rc:<segment-id>:<slug>` and §10.3 says the lesson `id` is "permanent, because progress and the roster deck key on it" — so the lesson id is what was taken. Three reasons beyond that sentence: every other item kind keys on its source object's own id as the server holds it (`lc:<lessonId>`, `gc:<moduleId>`, `sf:<slug>`); deriving the bare segment id needs a strip step that could collide if a non-segment lesson were ever named `segment-*`; and the back-link needs the lesson id anyway.
> - **The opt-in flag is its own KEY in the progress property store, not a member of the progress map.** §10.8 says "a `rosterDeck` flag in the progress property store", and the store is what was used — but `clProgressVisible_` filters that map to lesson and module ids the tier may read and would drop a preference key on every read, so a flag inside the map could not have worked. Turning the deck off **deletes** the key, so "off" has one representation and an account that opts out leaves no residue.
>
> **The content hash is the basis text alone, exactly as specified — and one consequence is recorded, not fixed.** A row whose **role** changes while its basis line does not would keep its schedule. In the built corpus the basis quotes the reasoning the role rests on, so the two move together; but that is a property of the generator's template rather than of the hash, and widening the hash to cover the role is a §10.8 amendment, not an implementation detail.
>
> **PROPOSED AMENDMENT — written 2026-09-19 at v06.57r by the carried-items row (§7.59 item (iii)); NOT APPLIED, the developer's call.** Amend the *Items* bullet below from *"Hash = the basis text"* to **"Hash = the role and the basis text, joined `role + '||' + basis`"** — the same join the guidance quiz items use — so that a row whose role moves in the registry while its basis line does not is re-introduced exactly as a moved basis is. Measured at proposal: 314 cards, 314 distinct basis hashes, id→hash pairs unchanged across six consecutive pushes since K2, and the role-only case never yet observed (rr59) — so the amendment closes a gap the corpus has not exercised rather than one it has. **The cost, and why it is a decision:** the hash formula is not versioned, so changing it re-keys **all 314** cards at once for every account that has graded any — each graded roster row resets to fresh on its next grade and is re-drawn at `CL_ROSTER_NEW_CAP` 10 a day. If taken: one line in `clDrillRosterItems_`, this bullet, the schema's identity table, a GAS version bump, and the roster probe held at 314 with **every pair moved** rather than none — the opposite acceptance test from every push so far.
>
> **The caveat §7's ordering table attached to K2 was measured away rather than waited out.** `build-classroom-segments.py --check` still reports **9 of 19 due**, and all nine differ on `read-next` and/or `what-is-bought-and-on-what` **only** — `the-players` is never among the differing sections. The deck's source was already current, and `--check` **held at 9 → 9** because nothing was regenerated.
>
> **Verification as built.** `check-classroom-content.py` **0 errors / 0 warnings at 56 lessons / 8 tracks**, gate cases **142 → 192** (6 tiers × 6 + 10 + 4 = 50, decomposed), module assertion **28** — both frozen numbers unchanged, since K2 authors neither a lesson nor a module. The "mechanism queue never contains an `rc:` item" line is now a **test**, asserted on counts in both directions with the pool size beside them, over five fixture shapes (a public segment lesson, a gated one, a **non-segment lesson carrying a the-players-shaped table**, one with **reordered columns**, one **missing a column**). `--strict` no structural findings — and §10.9's own K2 detector flipped from *"K2 not built — no roster caps in Classroom.gs"* to *"CL_ROSTER_SESSION_CAP 20"* with the health script untouched. `--selftest` 13/0; `--base origin/main` **P1** on the developer paths, **P2** outside the fence (a code row by construction) and **P3**, whose delta decomposed to **exactly one** of the 32 gate symbols — `handleClassroomOp_` — before `gateDigest` was refreshed.

- **Items.** `rc:<segment-id>:<slug>` — one card per row of a segment lesson's `the-players` table. Front: "*Company* — which segment, and which role?" Back: the segment name, the role, and the `basis` line. A company in two segments yields two cards on purpose — the role differs. Hash = the basis text, so a moved basis re-introduces the card (the same reasoning as `revisions[].changed[]`).
- **Enumeration.** Server-side from the `segment-*` lessons the session may read (`clLessonVisible_` — all public, so analyst+), so the deck follows regeneration automatically and no registry read is needed at drill time.
- **Separation.** Its own ops parameters (`cop=drill&deck=roster`; `cop=grade` recognises the `rc:` prefix and validates it against the enumeration), its own caps (`CL_ROSTER_SESSION_CAP` 20, `CL_ROSTER_NEW_CAP` 10), the same two sheet tabs (the id prefix separates the rows). **Opt-in per account** — a `rosterDeck` flag in the progress property store, default off; the mechanism queue never contains an `rc:` item and the roster queue never contains anything else. A `#drill/roster` route and a toggle on the drill landing card.
- **Verification.** Both checkers; the analyst-view test (an analyst sees the roster toggle and can drill it — every input is public); Playwright read of both queues. GAS + page bump.

### 10.9 · The health script — `scripts/check-classroom-curriculum.py` (S1, Fable 5.1 High; built v05.17r)

A **report**, exit 0 by default; `--strict` exits 1 on structural findings only. It reads `Classroom.gs` (via the content checker's parser), `profiler-segments.json`, the registries and the guidance module list, and prints:

- **Lessons per lane and per track**, against §4 and §10.5 — and the gate distribution per lane (`tracks` / `guidance` / `briefing` / `reports`), so the analyst-visible share is a number.
- **Segment coverage vs the floor** — per segment: members, incumbents, challengers, floor met, segment lesson present, landscape module present. *Strict:* a landscape on a below-floor segment; a registered segment with no lesson (after S1); a member slug with no profile; `unassigned[]` non-empty is reported, not strict.
- **Stale pins** — per lesson, inputs whose live date is later than the pin (the pipeline's candidate list, made visible); for `segment-*` lessons this is **"regeneration due"** (it calls the generator's `--check`). **Since v06.52r (2026-09-18, C5 session 1) the stale-pin line prints its denominator and says the segment lessons are not counted in it, and the generator's due count follows under its own labelled heading with a denominator of its own** — the (rr17) adjacency fix §7.59 item (iv) asked for, confirmed done rather than re-done at v06.57r.
- **Drill pool size** — `sf` · `ss` · `lc` · `lq` · `rc` counts against their caps, so the study-pool gap of §8 item 5 could never again go unnoticed. **Since v06.58r it also prints each tier's deck against `CL_DRILL_ACCOUNT_CAP`, with and without the roster deck, and `--strict` flags a deck the cap would refuse** — the composite it printed before equalled no tier's deck (rr58, rr61).
- **Review dates** — every lesson and module `reviewBy` within 30 days or passed.

And **one assertion added to `check-classroom-content.py`** (the schema checker, so it fails a commit): for every `segment-<id>` lesson, the ten section ids are present in order, its `profile:` inputs equal the registry's member set for that segment exactly, and `the-players` rows equal the members — a generated lesson that drifted from its registry is an error.

### 10.10 · The two implicit role paths

Two readers use the same curriculum in a different order, and neither path is a field in the data — the study-next pointer walks registry order and `prereqs[]` only suggests. They are written down so a session (and later C6's placement quiz) can point a reader at the right door:

- **The storage seller** — product first: `bess-foundations` → `electrical-foundations` → **Value Chain I** → `market-access` (through `reading-the-numbers`) → **Value Chain III** → then `aidc-grid-to-chip` and `aidc-campus` for the socket → landscapes for integrators, cells, developers and IPPs, utilities, capital (contributor).
- **The AIDC power seller** — the chain first: `electrical-foundations` → `aidc-grid-to-chip` (through `interconnection-for-large-loads` for contributors) → `aidc-campus` → **Value Chain II** → **Value Chain III** → then `bess-foundations` and `market-access` → landscapes for landlords, hyperscalers, bridge generation, in-hall power, grid equipment, EPC.

Both begin with `reading-the-graph` before any segment lesson, and both end on the landscapes their tier can read. When C6 makes a role explicit (a placement quiz that sets a starting track), it sets one of these two; until then they are documentation.

### 10.11 · Recorded for the developer's taxonomy review (S0's "done when")

The registry is the developer's taxonomy applied by one session against 154 dossiers; these are the places the application strained, each recorded in the registry's `notes` or `basis` so the review has something to read:

- **`compute-and-the-rack` is below the floor**, not above it as §7.2 counted: NVIDIA is the one incumbent and Flex the one adjacent (a contract manufacturer of AMD and Cerebras systems). No rack or server OEM, no AMD, no ASIC programme is covered. Either the segment stays thin until a coverage row fills it, or it folds into `power-conversion-and-rack-power-silicon` — the developer's call; no Phase E row was invented for it. **Decided 2026-09-07 PM (v05.11r): filled, not folded.** AMD (the challenger to NVIDIA) and Supermicro (the rack/server OEM) are Phase E group E7 in `PROFILER-COVERAGE-PLAN.md` §8/§10, both on Opus 5 xhigh. The fold was rejected because NVIDIA and Flex already sit in power conversion — a fold would have moved nothing and deleted the load's own buying criteria (performance per watt, allocation, rack density and the NVLink domain, the qualified vendor set, cooling requirement). The segment lesson generates thin until E7 lands; the landscape waits for the floor.
- **`software-and-optimization` is not at zero** — FlexGen and Fluence's software lines are there on the dossiers' own words. The floor is met on the letter; the landscape still waits for S3 batch 1.
- **Roles are the dossier's characterisation, not geography.** CRRC Zhuzhou and HyperStrong are `incumbent` integrators with no US channel; the basis says so, and the landscape carries the US reading. A per-market role was considered and rejected — it would double the registry for one segment's benefit.
- **The role vocabulary strains on the demand tier.** The three AI labs are `challenger` hyperscalers (new buyers scaling up, not competitors of the clouds); the utilities' `challenger` members are the competitive retail suppliers (NRG, Vistra) because a regulated franchise has none; Galaxy Digital — C9's category mismatch — is a landlord `challenger` because Helios is a campus even though the company is a broker-dealer. Each is written into `basis`.
- **Two incumbencies rest on thin third-party evidence and say so:** Power Electronics (one dated Wood Mackenzie joint mention, C7's verdict) and Vicor (the last-centimetre reference, absent from NVIDIA's roster). EVE is `challenger` by its own dossier's word despite a #3 rank.
- **Constellation** is registered `developer` and placed as the clean-firm incumbent and an IPP adjacent — the registry category and the segment role disagree, which is the evidence rule working as designed.
- **Not decided here:** the G6 module's id (fixed at authoring); whether a `project:`-keyed deck should join the roster deck; whether the Value Chain tracks should take `prereqs[]` on the mechanism tracks or stay independent (left independent).

## 11 · The simulation layer — C5, the ledger (designed 2026-09-18 at v06.51r, Fable 5.1 xhigh) — **14 OF 14 BUILT — C5 CLOSED at v06.56r, 2026-09-18**

**Provenance.** C5 is `PHASE6-CLASSROOM-DESIGN.md`'s sales-simulation phase and `INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.3 order 9. Its design is **`C5-SALES-SIMULATIONS-DESIGN.md`** — read that, not this: the eleven decisions (§3), the schema (§4), the ten-section template (§5), the stamp and gate rule (§6), the page and server changes (§7), the checker extensions (§8), the inventory with its premises (§9), a worked example (§10), the done-when (§11), the developer's open calls (§12) and the session plan (§13). **This section is the ledger** the five Opus authoring sessions flip, in the role §7 plays for the mechanism lessons and §10.6's "Built so far" plays for the landscapes. The design's decisions are **proposed** — the developer approves or amends them before session 1 (`INTEGRATED-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` §7.60) runs.

**In one sentence.** A scenario is a schema-v1 lesson with `type: "scenario"` and a five-field `scenario` block (mode · seat · segment · counterparty · stage), ten fixed sections with three `quiz` beats, a claims ledger, a stamp that must carry its segment's landscape so it folds to `guidance` (contributor and admin, never analyst, never admin-only, no `report:` input), never written by the unattended pipeline (P13), in no deck and no track, played through a Rehearsal library. Where it sits in §10.1's model: the third cross-cutting feed, the **rehearsal** — a layer-3 roster member rehearsed against layer-4 judgment.

**The rule for flipping a row.** A session flips a row here **in the same commit** as the literal lands in `clLessons_()` — `—` → the repo version — and records any counterparty substitution (same segment, same role, reason in the CHANGELOG). A row is not built until its literal is on `main`. **Never split a scenario across sessions.**
